# Model experiments to improve fit to KC remapping, with APL on/off

last model experiment: {{meta:last_experiment}}, edited: {{meta:generated}}

[TOC]

%%% hover
isotonic: Order-preserving. An isotonic fit is the non-decreasing curve (a step function) closest to the data in the least-squares sense, with no assumption about its shape; see the footnote on the monotone fit.
VCF: Odor distances derived from how often odors co-occur across natural odor sources (the hyperbolic embedding of natural-source statistics in the thesis), used as a reference for structure the ORN input does not predict.
oracles: Model manipulations handed part of the answer (which KCs or which glomeruli to change, read off the KC data or off the model's own output scored against them); ceiling tests, never mechanisms. See the footnote on the term.
oracle: On this page, a model manipulation that is handed part of the answer: it is told exactly which KCs or which glomeruli to change (a list read off the KC data, or off the model's own output scored against the data) and changes exactly those. A ceiling test, never a mechanism; see the footnote on the term.
oracle constructions: The manipulations given the answer: the oracle co-responder suppression, the oracle glomerulus gain applied at fixed parameters and its glomerulus subsets, and the block-specific glomerulus set of the breadth x shared-input rule. Their results are ceilings, so the progress plot counts none of them, nor any model that carries the gain as a fixed setting (the reference model included).
oracle co-responder suppression: The ceiling test for the block: the KCs that respond, with APL off, to at least one fruity ester (2h, IaA, pa) and to at least one small ester (2-but, eb, ep) are read off the model's own APL-off run and the APL->KC weights of their claws are multiplied by 4 (also 8, and 1000), with the APL re-tuned. An oracle because the set is defined by the outcome wanted.
oracle co-responder experiment: The experiment that defined the fruity x small-ester co-responders from the model's own APL-off responses and inhibited exactly those KCs about four times harder than the rest, to ask whether removing them is sufficient for the block. Given the set to remove, so a ceiling test, not a mechanism.
oracle's co-responder set: The KCs the oracle co-responder suppression targeted: those responding, with APL off, to at least one fruity ester and at least one small ester, identified from the model's own output rather than by any circuit rule.
oracle's suppression: What the oracle co-responder suppression did: the model's fruity x small-ester co-responders inhibited about four times harder than other KCs, which gave the block at the measured size.
oracle suppression: The claw-dynamics base with the coincidence-3 rule, the APL->KC weights of its own fruity x small-ester co-responders (defined with APL off) multiplied by 4 and the APL re-tuned to the 5 % response rate: the ceiling test for the block, given the KC set to remove.
oracle glomerulus gain: The PN->claw weights of eight glomeruli (VA7m, DL1, D, DM1, DP1m, VC1, DC2, DM5), the glomeruli the five weak-input odors drive most, multiplied by 1.5 (a true PN->claw gain, on the wiring's columns, before the per-claw threshold), the threshold re-tuned. An oracle because the glomeruli were chosen from the odors the model was known to miss, and the 1.5 from nothing the model has.
oracle gain: The 1.5x PN->claw gain on the eight glomeruli the weak-input odors drive most (VA7m, DL1, D, DM1, DP1m, VC1, DC2, DM5): a fixed factor set by hand because those odors were known to be missing, not fitted and not derived from the model or the anatomy, hence oracle. In the fitted models it is held at 1.5 (or 1.25 where stated) while every other parameter is fitted.
oracle glomeruli: The eight glomeruli the oracle gain scales: VA7m, DL1 and D (ms), DM1 (B-myr, paa), DP1m and VC1 (euc, -aPine), DC2 and DM5 (1-8ol), each weak-input odor's most-driven glomeruli in the lab's ORN data.
oracle set: The oracle gain's eight glomeruli (VA7m, DL1, D, DM1, DP1m, VC1, DC2, DM5) treated as a set, here compared with the other ORN-driven glomeruli in the hemibrain's calyx wiring.
oracle test: The oracle glomerulus gain experiment: the PN->claw weights of the weak odors' eight glomeruli scaled by 1.5, with the same gain on eight random glomeruli as the control. A test of whether glomerulus-specific claw strength would recruit the weak odors, given which glomeruli.
Curveball: A randomization of a binary matrix that keeps every row and column sum (here each KC's number of distinct glomeruli and each glomerulus' number of KCs) by repeatedly swapping glomeruli between pairs of KCs; a claw permutation instead re-assigns the claws themselves, keeping each KC's and each glomerulus' number of claws.
the current reference model: The combined model with interpretable bounds (40 Hz per-claw threshold, breadth exponent 2, KC–KC inhibition, the 1.5× oracle gain on eight glomeruli, APL→bouton capped at 3× the baseline), fitted so that 6.8 % of KCs respond with APL on, the fraction measured in the control flies, instead of the conventional 5 %. It became the reference on 2026-11-08, when the operating point experiment re-fitted the earlier reference at that fraction: the refit kept the block and the agreement with both KC matrices, raised response rate ρ with APL on and had a lower loss once the response-rate term is left out, so fits have used the data's fraction since and the later experiments start from this model.
one-spike floor: An offset b added to the spike count of every KC that fired, before the saturating readout is applied, (n + b) / (n + b + k), so that the weight of a single spike is calibrated on the recordings instead of following from k. Tried in the low end of the readout and not adopted; not the floor of the APL-effect loss.
survivors: In a model, the KCs that fire twenty or more spikes to some odor with APL off (its high-firing set) and also reach twenty spikes to some odor with APL on; APL does not silence them, and with APL on they dominate the responses of the odors they respond to. Defined in the experiment on the high-firing KCs that survive APL.
high-firing set: A model's KCs that fire twenty or more spikes to some odor with APL off; those of them that also reach twenty spikes to some odor with APL on are its survivors.
firing ceiling: In a model, a refractory period after each spike together with an adaptation that raises the KC's spiking threshold at every spike and decays slowly, which bounds how many spikes a KC fires to one odor. Off in every model on this page except those of the firing ceiling for the KCs; see the footnote on how the ceiling works.
literature setting: The firing ceiling at which the largest spike count of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit with APL on is nearest 14 spikes per odor, the mean plus three standard deviations of the counts of α′/β′ KCs in whole-cell recordings (Turner, Bazhenov and Laurent, 2008).
stronger setting: The firing ceiling with a stronger adaptation than at the literature setting, the weakest tried at which the calibrated readout has nothing left to compress, so that raw spike counts and the readout give the same scores. It allows a KC fewer spikes per odor than whole-cell recordings of α′/β′ KCs show.
input-scaled KC threshold: In a model, each KC's somatic threshold multiplied by a power of the KC's summed claw weight relative to the mean over KCs, the same with APL on and with APL off, so that a KC with many claws needs more input to fire. Off in every model on this page except those of the subsection on the input-scaled KC threshold.
draw term: The standard deviation of the APL-effect loss of one model family over the real wiring and its re-drawn versions, each with its own fit: how much of a single wiring's loss is that wiring's accident. Two fits that differ by about this much or less on one wiring are compared on the same re-drawn wirings.
baseline-noise units: A KC's response divided by its own baseline noise, the standard deviation of its fluorescence signal within the pre-odor windows of its recording (each window's mean removed). Unlike the recordings' z-scored unit, it does not grow with how much the KC responds. The realism bands count the flies' responses in these units, above one threshold set so that the control flies keep their measured response rate; see the footnote on baseline-noise units.

## What the data show: the ORN→KC remapping in control flies, and what blocking APL output does to it

Yang imaged Kenyon cell (KC) responses to 16 odors in seven chemical groups (fruity esters 2h, IaA, pa; small esters 2-but, eb, ep; aldehydes 6al, t2h; alcohols 1-8ol, 1-5ol, 1-6ol; ms; paa; terpenes -aPine, euc, B-myr) in control flies (11 recordings from 8 flies) and in flies whose APL output is blocked by tetanus toxin (8 recordings from 7 flies; "APL output blocked (TNT)" in the figures and tables).

For every pair of odors, the distance between the two odors' KC responses is one minus the cosine similarity of the two population response vectors (one entry per KC, trial-averaged), computed per recording and averaged over recordings; the 120 pair distances of a genotype form its 16 × 16 matrix. The same 16 odors exist in the lab's ORN data, so distances can also be computed between the glomerular response vectors at the input of the circuit ([paa, euc and -aPine are at substituted ORN concentrations](#fn-concentrations), a caveat that applies to every number below). For the ORNs every comparison in this page uses one minus the Pearson correlation across the 36 glomeruli imaged in both ORN panels (the glomeruli of the model's ORN input), computed per fly and averaged over flies where both odors were imaged in the same flies; the megamat and validation2 odors were imaged in different flies, so pairs across the two panels use fly-mean responses.

Cosine distance ranks the ORN pairs similarly (ρ=0.96 on odor-odor distances between the two) and gives a ρ on odor-odor distances with the KC matrices about 0.04 higher. The collapsible figure under the first one shows everything with Pearson correlation instead.

![Top: the three 16 × 16 odor–odor distance matrices (ORNs; KCs, control flies; KCs, APL output blocked (TNT); odors in group order). Bottom left and middle: each KC matrix against the ORNs, pair by pair (blue: pairs within a chemical group; red circles: the nine fruity × small-ester pairs). Bottom right: control flies against APL output blocked (TNT), with the best monotone (isotonic) fit; the red circles sit above the curve, the fruity × small-ester block.](results/summary_background/summary_background.png)

??? The same figure with Pearson correlation instead of cosine distance
![The same six panels with every matrix as the Pearson correlation between the two odors' response vectors (r, from −1 to 1; ORNs across the 36 glomeruli, per fly and averaged over flies within a panel; KCs across KCs, per recording and averaged over recordings), in a blue–white–red colormap whose lightness is linear in |r| (white at 0; the diagonal is masked). Bottom: the same pair-by-pair comparisons in correlation; bottom right, the nine fruity × small-ester pairs sit below the monotone fit, less correlated in control flies than their correlation with APL output blocked (TNT) predicts.](results/summary_background/summary_background_pearson.png)
???

The statistics used throughout, in plain terms:

- **ρ on odor-odor distances:** the Spearman correlation between two sets of the 120 pair distances (a model matrix and a KC matrix, the two KC matrices, or a KC matrix and the ORNs): how far the order of the pairs agrees. A model's APL-on matrix is compared with the control flies' and its APL-off matrix with the APL output blocked (TNT) flies'.
- **Measurement ceiling:** the most any model can be expected to reach. Mean matrices of random halves of the recordings of one genotype agree at ρ={{data:ctrl_split_half_rho ~ .2f}} on odor-odor distances (control flies) and {{data:tnt_split_half_rho ~ .2f}} (APL output blocked (TNT)); corrected to the full set of recordings (Spearman–Brown), the square root of that reliability is the ρ a noise-free model would have with the observed mean matrix, {{data:ctrl_ceiling ~ .2f}} and {{data:tnt_ceiling ~ .2f}}. For the per-odor response rates the same construction gives {{data:ctrl_rate_rho_ceiling ~ .2f}} and {{data:tnt_rate_rho_ceiling ~ .2f}}.
- **Response rate ρ:** the Spearman correlation between the fraction of KCs responding to each of the 16 odors in a model and in the KC data (the per-odor response-rate pattern); the response rate of a model or a genotype is the mean of that fraction over odors.
- **Noise floor and the corrected distance:** two noisy response vectors are never parallel, so noise inflates every cosine distance toward 1. From the three trials of each odor, the distance between two independent trial means of the same odor is the noise floor ({{data:ctrl_trial_mean_floor ~ .2f}} in control flies, {{data:tnt_trial_mean_floor ~ .2f}} with APL output blocked (TNT); between single trials {{data:ctrl_single_trial_floor ~ .2f}} and {{data:tnt_single_trial_floor ~ .2f}}), and dividing each pair's cosine by the odors' signal norms (the trial mean's squared norm minus its noise share) gives the distance a noise-free recording would show. The order of pairs, the block and the isotonic R² are unchanged by the correction; only the level moves, and a deterministic model is compared with the corrected level.
- **Monotone relation and the block:** the control matrix [is fitted with the best monotone](#fn-isotonic) (isotonic) function of the APL output blocked (TNT) matrix; the fraction of variance it explains is the isotonic R², and a pair's residual from that curve says how far it deviates from the common trend. The block is the mean residual of the nine fruity × small-ester pairs.
- **Excess shared responders:** for each pair, the KC distance actually measured minus the distance expected if the two odors' responders were drawn independently with the odors' own response fractions; negative means the two odors share fewer KCs than their response density alone predicts, positive that they share more. Reported for the block pairs and for the other between-group pairs; the two numbers together are called the excess profile.
- **Structure beyond the ORN input:** the part of a KC matrix not explained by [a linear prediction from the ORN matrix](#fn-orn-residual) (the residual). Two references are used for it and are named separately below: the **structure vs wild-type somata** is the ρ of the residual with the same residual in [Remy's wild-type KC soma recordings](#fn-yang-remy) (the 12 odors shared with the megamat panel, 66 pairs); the **natural-source alignment** is the ρ of the residual with odor distances derived from natural-source odor statistics (the hyperbolic VCF embedding; the raw co-occurrence distances give no alignment, −0.09 / −0.10, so the result depends on the embedding).

| | ORNs | KCs {span=2} |
| statistic | | control flies | APL output blocked (TNT) |
|---|---|---|---|
| mean pair distance (120 pairs) | {{data:orn_mean_dist ~ .2f}} | {{data:ctrl_mean_dist ~ .2f}} | {{data:tnt_mean_dist ~ .2f}} |
| mean pair distance with sub-threshold responses set to 0 (the models' convention) | — | {{data:ctrl_mean_dist_thresholded ~ .2f}} | {{data:tnt_mean_dist_thresholded ~ .2f}} |
| trial-to-trial noise floor: distance between two independent trial means of the same odor | — | {{data:ctrl_trial_mean_floor ~ .2f}} | {{data:tnt_trial_mean_floor ~ .2f}} |
| mean pair distance corrected for that noise (the level a noise-free model should match) | — | {{data:ctrl_mean_dist_noise_corrected ~ .2f}} | {{data:tnt_mean_dist_noise_corrected ~ .2f}} |
| range of pair distances | {{data:orn_min_dist ~ .2f}}–{{data:orn_max_dist ~ .2f}} | {{data:ctrl_min_dist ~ .2f}}–{{data:ctrl_max_dist ~ .2f}} | {{data:tnt_min_dist ~ .2f}}–{{data:tnt_max_dist ~ .2f}} |
| pairs more than 0.8 apart | {{data:orn_n_gt08 ~ .0f}} | {{data:ctrl_n_gt08 ~ .0f}} | {{data:tnt_n_gt08 ~ .0f}} |
| mean distance within a chemical group | {{data:orn_within_mean ~ .2f}} | {{data:ctrl_within_mean ~ .2f}} | {{data:tnt_within_mean ~ .2f}} |
| mean distance, fruity × small-ester pairs | {{data:orn_fruity_small_mean ~ .2f}} | {{data:ctrl_fruity_small_mean ~ .2f}} | {{data:tnt_fruity_small_mean ~ .2f}} |
| ρ on odor-odor distances with the ORNs | — | {{data:rho_ctrl_orn ~ .2f}} | {{data:rho_tnt_orn ~ .2f}} |
| ρ on odor-odor distances between the two KC matrices | — | {{data:rho_ctrl_tnt ~ .2f}} | {{data:rho_ctrl_tnt ~ .2f}} |
| isotonic R² of control flies on APL output blocked (TNT) | — | {{data:iso_r2 ~ .2f}} | {{data:iso_r2 ~ .2f}} |
| fraction of KCs responding per odor (mean) | — | {{data:ctrl_frac ~ .3f}} | {{data:tnt_frac ~ .3f}} |
| measurement ceiling on odor-odor distances (from split halves of the recordings) | — | {{data:ctrl_ceiling ~ .2f}} | {{data:tnt_ceiling ~ .2f}} |

**The remapping in control flies.** KCs push nearly every between-group pair to near-maximal distance: {{data:ctrl_n_gt08 ~ .0f}} of the 120 pairs are more than 0.8 apart in the control KC matrix, against {{data:orn_n_gt08 ~ .0f}} in the ORN input, while pairs within a chemical group stay partly similar (mean distance {{data:ctrl_within_mean ~ .2f}}). The order of pairs follows the ORN input only loosely (ρ={{data:rho_ctrl_orn ~ .2f}} on odor-odor distances, against a measurement ceiling of {{data:ctrl_ceiling ~ .2f}}): pairs that are close in the ORN input are spread over most of the KC range, and pairs already far apart in the ORN input are compressed at the top. This expansion is what any model of the circuit must produce first.

**What blocking APL output does, mostly: a uniform sparsening.** Twice as many KCs respond to each odor ({{data:tnt_frac ~ .3f}} instead of {{data:ctrl_frac ~ .3f}} of the KCs, with the per-odor pattern conserved), and the mean pair distance falls from {{data:ctrl_mean_dist ~ .2f}} to {{data:tnt_mean_dist ~ .2f}}, below the ORN input's own {{data:orn_mean_dist ~ .2f}}. The order of pairs is largely kept (ρ={{data:rho_ctrl_tnt ~ .2f}} on odor-odor distances between the two KC matrices) and the relation between them is monotone (isotonic R² {{data:iso_r2 ~ .2f}}, as high as the measurement ceiling allows).

**What it does not undo.** The code with APL output blocked (TNT) is more ORN-like than the control code (ρ on odor-odor distances with the ORN input {{data:rho_tnt_orn ~ .2f}} instead of {{data:rho_ctrl_orn ~ .2f}}), yet it keeps structure the ORN input does not predict: its natural-source alignment is {{data:tnt_vcf ~ .2f}}, which a linear model of the circuit does not reproduce. About half of the similarity gain with APL output blocked (TNT) is explained by the higher response density alone; the rest is structure, and both genotypes have fewer non-specific shared responders than a linear model gives (excess shared responders: control flies {{data:ctrl_excess_block ~ .2f}} for the block pairs and {{data:ctrl_excess_other ~ .2f}} for other between-group pairs; APL output blocked (TNT) {{data:tnt_excess_block ~ .2f}} and {{data:tnt_excess_other ~ .2f}}).

**The one pair-specific exception: the block.** The nine fruity × small-ester pairs, 0.51 apart in the ORN input and 0.88 in control flies, fall to 0.60 when APL output is blocked (TNT), well below the monotone trend. That residual, {{data:block ~ +.3f}} (90 % confidence interval {{data:block.lo ~ +.3f}} to {{data:block.hi ~ +.3f}}), is the block referred to throughout; it means APL removes a population of KCs that respond to both ester groups.

## What a model has to reproduce

### The main target: the per-pair APL effect {#apl-effect}

What APL does to each pair of odors is the first thing a model is held to. For each of the 120 pairs, the per-pair APL effect is the pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off: in the KC data, the control flies' distance minus the distance with APL output blocked (TNT), from the two KC matrices of the first figure (cosine distance, mean over recordings); in a model, the distance with APL on minus the distance of the same model with APL removed and nothing re-tuned (the acute knockout), as cosine distance on spike counts. It is positive where APL moves two odors apart.

![The KC data behind the main target (odors in group order). Left: KCs, control flies (APL on). Middle: KCs, APL output blocked (TNT) (APL off). Right: the per-pair APL effect, APL on minus APL off; red marks a pair that APL output moves apart, blue one that it would bring closer, and every pair is red.](results/apl_effect/matrices/data.png)

In the flies APL output moves every one of the 120 pairs apart. The effect is largest for the nine fruity × small-ester pairs (the block, which shows here as a larger difference instead of as a residual from the monotone trend) and smallest for the pairs with -aPine or euc, which are far apart even with APL output blocked (TNT), and for the pairs among the three alcohols.

How a model is scored against it:

- **APL-effect loss:** the root mean squared difference between the model's and the data's per-pair APL effect over the 120 pairs, in units of cosine distance. Lower is better, and this is the number a model is asked to bring down.
- **Mean APL effect, and the mean part of the loss:** the effect averaged over the 120 pairs says how far APL moves odors apart overall. The model's mean minus the data's is the mean part of the loss.
- **ρ on the per-pair APL effect, and the pattern part of the loss:** the Spearman correlation across the 120 pairs between the model's and the data's effect says whether APL moves the same pairs most. The pattern part is the loss that is left when each side's own mean is subtracted first. The two parts make up the loss exactly (the squared loss is the squared mean part plus the squared pattern part), so a model can be right on average and wrong pair by pair, or the reverse, and both are reported beside every loss.
- **Floor of the loss:** the lowest loss worth reaching. The recordings are a sample of flies, and the measured effect would come out somewhat differently with other flies. Drawing the recordings of each genotype again with replacement (the two genotypes are different flies, so the effect exists only between their mean matrices) and scoring the resampled effect against the measured one, as if it were a model, gives the loss that a model with the flies' true effect would still show; a loss below it would be fitting the noise of these recordings.
- **Ceiling on ρ on the per-pair APL effect:** the same limit for the pattern, built as the ceilings on the matrices are: the effects computed from two random halves of the recordings are correlated, the value is corrected to the full set of recordings (Spearman–Brown), and its square root is the ρ that a noise-free model with the true effect would be expected to reach. It is lower than the matrices' own ceilings ({{data:ctrl_ceiling ~ .2f}} and {{data:tnt_ceiling ~ .2f}}) because the effect is a difference between two groups of flies: what the two matrices have in common cancels in it, and the variability of both remains.

| statistic | KC data | 90 % interval |
|---|---|---|
| mean APL effect over the 120 pairs (interval: resampling the recordings) | {{data:apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}} | {{data:apl_effect_mean.lo ~ +.3f}} to {{data:apl_effect_mean.hi ~ +.3f}} |
| smallest / largest per-pair APL effect | {{data:apl_effect_min ~ +.3f}} / {{data:apl_effect_max ~ +.3f}} | — |
| standard deviation of the effect across the 120 pairs | {{data:apl_effect_sd ~ .3f}} | — |
| mean APL effect of the nine fruity × small-ester pairs | {{diff:data:ctrl_fruity_small_mean -- data:tnt_fruity_small_mean ~ +.3f}} | — |
| mean APL effect from the mean distances corrected for trial-to-trial noise | {{diff:data:ctrl_mean_dist_noise_corrected -- data:tnt_mean_dist_noise_corrected ~ +.3f}} | — |
| mean APL effect from the mean distances with sub-threshold responses set to 0 (the models' convention) | {{diff:data:ctrl_mean_dist_thresholded -- data:tnt_mean_dist_thresholded ~ +.3f}} | — |
| floor of the APL-effect loss (interval: resampling the recordings) | {{data:apl_effect_rmse_floor ~ .3f}} | {{data:apl_effect_rmse_floor.lo ~ .3f}} to {{data:apl_effect_rmse_floor.hi ~ .3f}} |
| ρ on the per-pair APL effect between two random halves of the recordings (interval: over the splits) | {{data:apl_effect_rho_split_half ~ .2f}} | {{data:apl_effect_rho_split_half.lo ~ .2f}} to {{data:apl_effect_rho_split_half.hi ~ .2f}} |
| ceiling on ρ on the per-pair APL effect | {{data:apl_effect_rho_ceiling ~ .2f}} | — |

Two values give the loss a scale. A model in which APL changed no distance would score the root mean square of the data's effect, a little more than its mean; a model in which APL moved every pair apart by exactly the data's mean, with no pattern at all, would score the data's standard deviation across pairs. The floor is below both, so a correct pattern can still lower the loss, but most of the way from a model in which APL changes nothing down to the floor is the mean.

**How well the mean is known.** The data's mean effect carries two uncertainties of about the same size. One is the sample of flies: the 90 % interval in the table. The other is how the distances are computed: from the mean distances corrected for trial-to-trial noise the mean effect is larger, and from the mean distances with sub-threshold responses set to 0, the convention closest to a model's spike counts, it is smaller (both in the table). The loss uses the matrices as measured, so a mean part smaller than these differences is within what the data leave open. A third choice is on the model's side: whether its spike counts are compared raw, as here, or compressed as the calcium indicator compresses them, which for the best models moves the mean effect by more than either ([the main target under a compressive readout](#readout)), unless the model's KCs are given a ceiling on their firing, with which the two come to nearly the same number ([the firing ceiling for the KCs](#firing-ceiling)).

**Realism is a constraint, not a target.** The mean part of the loss can be lowered without APL doing anything right pair by pair: if more KCs respond with APL off, the APL-off distances fall and the effect grows. A loss is therefore always read with the response rates beside it, and counts only inside four bands, the realism bands. All four count a response in a model as two or more spikes to the odor, and a response in the flies as a three-trial mean above one threshold in [baseline-noise units](#fn-baseline-noise-units): the KC's change in fluorescence divided by its own noise in the seconds before the odors, with the threshold set where the control flies' response rate is the one the recordings' own criterion gives. The recordings' z-scored unit grows with how much a KC responds, so responses counted in it are not compared with spike counts here (the footnote has the unit, the reasons and what the change does to the flies' values). Three are on the response rates: a response rate with APL on between 0.045 and 0.085 (the control flies' is {{data:ctrl_frac ~ .3f}}; the models are tuned to 0.05 or to the flies' value); a response rate with APL off between 1.6 and 2.9 times the response rate with APL on (the flies' ratio is {{data:noise_rate_ratio_fraction ~ .1f}}, 90 % interval over recordings {{data:noise_rate_ratio_fraction.lo ~ .1f}} to {{data:noise_rate_ratio_fraction.hi ~ .1f}}; a ratio, so that models tuned to 5 % are held to the same proportion as models tuned to the flies' value); and a response rate ρ with APL on of at least 0.37, no more than 0.10 below the reference model's {{E34:thr40_gain1.5_k2_cap3:spearman_rate_on_vs_data ~ .2f}}, so that the right overall response rates are not reached with the wrong odors. The fourth is on how the responses are spread over the KCs, which the response rates leave open: the same rates can come from many KCs that each respond to a few odors or from a few KCs that respond to most, and a model that, with APL off, adds its responses mostly to KCs that already respond brings its APL-off distances down, and its effect up, without the recruitment of new KCs that the flies show (table below). The band is on the number of odors each KC responds to (odors per KC). The KCs are sorted by how many of the 16 odors they respond to, by the criterion of the response rates, into five groups (none, one, two or three, four to seven, eight or more), and the model's five shares are compared with the flies' mean shares, with APL on against the control flies and with APL off against the flies with APL output blocked (TNT). The distance between two such distributions is half the sum of the absolute differences between their shares (the total variation distance: the share of KCs that would have to change group for the two to agree). The band is the largest distance between one recording's distribution and the mean of the other recordings of its genotype, {{data:noise_ctrl_odors_per_kc_tv_max ~ .2f}} with APL on and {{data:noise_tnt_odors_per_kc_tv_max ~ .2f}} with APL off: a model is outside when it is further from the flies than the most atypical recording is from the rest. Unlike the band on the ratio, this one is on the distribution itself and not relative to a model's own response rate: a model tuned to 5 % has fewer responses to spread than the flies have, and so more silent KCs, and that counts against it. A model outside a band is marked wherever its loss is shown and is not counted as progress; a model whose responses were not saved cannot be scored on the fourth band and is held to the other three.

The number of odors each KC responds to in the KC data, a response counted in baseline-noise units as the realism bands count it: the share of KCs in each group (mean over recordings), and the distance between one recording's distribution and the mean of the other recordings of its genotype ([the same by the recordings' own criterion](#fn-baseline-noise-units))

| | KCs {span=2} |
| statistic | control flies | APL output blocked (TNT) |
|---|---|---|

**What the loss does not see.** Being a difference, the effect is blind to whatever a model gets wrong in both conditions alike: a pair that the model puts too close with APL on and equally too close with APL off costs nothing. The agreement with each of the two matrices and the other targets below are therefore still scored for every model.

**What the data's trial-to-trial noise means for these targets.** Every model on this page is one deterministic run, while the KC matrices are means of three noisy trials. Putting [trial-to-trial noise](#fn-trial-noise) into the reference model at the level that reproduces the data's single-trial and three-trial floors, and passing ten pseudo-recordings through the data's own pipeline, shows three things. Fast noise on the PN rates cannot reach the data's floors at all (it averages out over the response window), while per-trial jitter of each KC's excitability or of each glomerulus's input gain can, at an unchanged response rate. Measured that way, the model's block shrinks by about a third, so a deterministic block equal to the measured one understates the data's noise-free block (the data's noise-corrected block is {{E43/onoff_noise.csv:model=data;matrix=corrected;transform=graded:block ~ +.3f}}). And noise lowers the model's mean distances rather than raising them, so the noise correction behind the corrected mean-distance targets above over-corrects model trials: those targets assume a noise the model's noise does not follow. The odor-odor order (ρ on odor-odor distances, isotonic R², response rate ρ) is unchanged by the noise.

**Re-fitting to the noise-corrected block does not help.** Re-fitting the reference model and the eight glomeruli at 1.25 with the loss's block target at the data's noise-corrected value moves their deterministic block by at most +0.01 and the block a recording of them would show by at most +0.004; pushing the target to +0.10 gets a deterministic +0.061 that still processes to +0.01. Every fit loses more than half of its block through the matched noise, and the loss is largest for the fit that is best deterministically (the eight at 1.25 at 70 Hz), so the processed block reverses the ordering the deterministic one gave: the model family's block is noise-fragile where the data's (itself a three-trial-mean measurement) is not. The best-models table below therefore carries the processed block beside the deterministic one, the recommendation to shrink the gain to 1.25 waits, and the open mechanism question is what would make a block carried by responses well above threshold rather than by marginal threshold crossings. The re-fits were first started from their counterparts by a seeding rule that reset the APL's bouton-input scale to the base model's value, so they also differed from their counterparts in the unfitted balance between the APL's bouton and KC inputs; repeated from exactly their counterparts' optima (the exact-start rows of the table), they give the same answer, with smaller deterministic moves and none through the noise.

**Fitting the block through the noise does not find a noise-robust one.** Where the fitted block lives: the KCs that separate a fruity × small-ester pair with APL on are ordinary responders (the same threshold margins and spike counts as every other APL-on response), and the APL-on response as a whole sits at threshold (about half of all responses within one jitter σ, against a third with APL off), so the block's fragility is the APL-on response's, not a special population's; the block-specific weak point is the KCs that APL turns into separators, whose suppressed odor sits just below threshold and comes back on one trial in seven. Joint fits whose block term was computed on nine jittered runs (three pseudo-recordings of three trials, fixed draws) found no noise-robust block in any of the three bases: the fit of the eight at 1.25 stayed at its seed, the fit of the reference model at 40 Hz tuned its own draws' block onto the target by lowering the deterministic block while the block through independent noise followed the deterministic one, and the fit of the reference model at 70 Hz, whose seed's own-draw block already sat on the target, did not move at all. With nine draws the fitted statistic carries a sampling error as large as the effect, so the fits fit the draw. One direction did appear: two simplex vertices one step from the eight at 1.25, with KC–KC inhibition at 27–33 instead of 12 and a lower somatic threshold, keep three times the optimum's block through independent noise (half the data's), paid with the APL-off response rate; whether that survives once a fit recovers the APL-off statistics is the next experiment.

**KC–KC lateral inhibition carries a block through the noise, at a cost in the APL-off statistics.** The noise-robust block's one positive direction was tested directly: the KC–KC scale held at 30 (the vertices' value; the fits had chosen 11–12) with the other three parameters fitted from the operating-point optima, each fit scored through the trial-noise pipeline. For the eight glomeruli at 1.25 at 70 Hz the fit went where the vertices sat (somatic threshold 58 instead of 79, APL→KC 0.43 instead of 0.34) and kept their block through independent noise ({{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc30:block_raw ~ +.3f}} against the seed's {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_det:block_raw ~ +.3f}}; the data {{data:block ~ +.3f}}), but could not recover the APL-off statistics: the response rate per trial with APL off {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc30:rate_trial_off ~ .3f}} against the seed's {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_det:rate_trial_off ~ .3f}} and the data's {{data:tnt_frac ~ .3f}}, its per-odor pattern (response rate ρ with APL off) {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc30:fit_po_off_spearman ~ .2f}} against {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_det:fit_po_off_spearman ~ .2f}}, the loss {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc30:loss_recorded ~ .2f}} against {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_det:loss_recorded ~ .2f}}. Held at 20 the block through the noise is the same ({{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc20:block_raw ~ +.3f}}) from a smaller deterministic block ({{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc20:block_det ~ +.3f}}), with the APL-off response rate back to {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc20:rate_trial_off ~ .3f}} and the loss {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc20:loss_recorded ~ .2f}}; held at 45 the block through the noise is the largest of the axis ({{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc45:block_raw ~ +.3f}}; grand mean {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc45:block_grand ~ +.3f}}) and the APL-off response rate is recovered ({{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc45:rate_trial_off ~ .3f}}), but in a different regime (somatic threshold 34 instead of 79, the APL scales doubled) that pays with the odor-odor structure itself (ρ on odor-odor distances through the noise {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc45:rho_raw_on ~ .2f}} / {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc45:rho_raw_off ~ .2f}} against the seed's {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_det:rho_raw_on ~ .2f}} / {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_det:rho_raw_off ~ .2f}}) and the loss {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc45:loss_recorded ~ .2f}}. For the reference model (the eight at 1.5 at 40 Hz) KC–KC 30 halves the deterministic block ({{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5_kckc30:block_det ~ +.3f}} against {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5_det:block_det ~ +.3f}}), leaves the block through the noise where it was ({{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5_kckc30:block_raw ~ +.3f}} against {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5_det:block_raw ~ +.3f}}) and costs the same APL-off response rate ({{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5_kckc30:rate_trial_off ~ .3f}} against {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5_det:rate_trial_off ~ .3f}}), at a loss of {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5_kckc30:loss_recorded ~ .2f}} against {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5_det:loss_recorded ~ .2f}}. Common to both bases: KC–KC inhibition remains when APL is blocked, so a stronger scale caps the rise of the response rate with APL off (the APL-off / APL-on ratio 1.8–2.0 against the data's 2.2), which bounds what this mechanism can carry through the noise: about half the data's block, for the eight at 1.25 only, with the somatic threshold near 60.

**The margins alone do not predict the block through the noise where it matters.** Each KC × odor entry's [threshold margin](#fn-margin), read off the deterministic run, gives the probability that the entry changes on a jittered trial. [Replaying every KC at the pipeline's own jittered thresholds](#fn-replay) with the rest of the network held at the deterministic run reproduces the pipeline exactly for the bouton base without KC–KC inhibition (block {{E55/per_model.csv:model=base70:obs_block_raw ~ +.3f}} in both), ranks the {{E55/agreement.csv:key=block_raw:n ~ .0f}} models' block through the noise (ρ {{E55/agreement.csv:key=block_raw:spearman ~ .2f}}, mean absolute error {{E55/agreement.csv:key=block_raw:mae ~ .3f}}) and matches their response rates and mean distances, but it misses what the KC–KC inhibition does with the jitter, in proportion to the inhibition's weight relative to the somatic threshold (the network's share of the single-trial floor against KC–KC / somatic threshold: ρ {{E55/agreement.csv:key=net_floor_vs_kckc:spearman ~ .2f}}). In the gain-1.5 family, where the fitted KC–KC is weak, the margins give the pipeline's block within its own sampling error; for the eight at 1.25 they promise too much; and in the strong-KC–KC models that carry a block through the noise they are off in either direction (KC–KC held at 30: {{E55/per_model.csv:model=E53-thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_kckc30_k2_cap3:pred_block_raw ~ +.3f}} promised against {{E55/per_model.csv:model=E53-thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_kckc30_k2_cap3:obs_block_raw ~ +.3f}} delivered). A margin term in the loss would therefore reward the KC–KC direction for block the network does not deliver: noise-robustness in that direction has to be simulated, or the proxy has to include the network's first response. A single margin statistic, the fraction of APL-on responses within one σ of threshold, is unrelated to the block a model loses through the noise (ρ {{E55/agreement.csv:key=within_sigma_vs_lost:spearman ~ .2f}}).

**[Iterating the KC–KC loop over the replayed trials](#fn-network-proxy) recovers the pipeline, at a cost that scales like a simulation.** Feeding each KC the inhibition from the other KCs' replayed spikes of the same trial, and repeating, closes the margin proxy's gap: after five passes the block through the noise of {{E56/agreement_pass.csv:key=block_raw;pass=5:n ~ .0f}} models agrees with the pipeline to a mean absolute error of {{E56/agreement_pass.csv:key=block_raw;pass=5:mae ~ .3f}} (ρ {{E56/agreement_pass.csv:key=block_raw;pass=5:spearman ~ .2f}}; the margins alone {{E56/agreement_pass.csv:key=block_raw;pass=1:mae ~ .3f}}), the APL-on single-trial floor to {{E56/agreement_pass.csv:key=floor_on;pass=5:mae ~ .3f}}, the response rates and mean distances having matched from the first pass. The iteration is a damped oscillation that has converged by the fifth pass for KC–KC up to about 33 over somatic thresholds near 60 (KC–KC held at 30: {{E56/per_model_pass.csv:model=E53-thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_kckc30_k2_cap3;pass=5:pred_block_raw ~ +.3f}} against the pipeline's {{E56/per_model_pass.csv:model=E53-thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_kckc30_k2_cap3;pass=5:obs_block_raw ~ +.3f}}) and still oscillates at the strongest inhibition tested (KC–KC held at 45: {{E56/per_model_pass.csv:model=E53-thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_kckc45_k2_cap3;pass=5:pred_block_raw ~ +.3f}} against {{E56/per_model_pass.csv:model=E53-thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_kckc45_k2_cap3;pass=5:obs_block_raw ~ +.3f}}). Each pass is a network computation over every trial, so five passes cost about a sixth of the pipeline's thirty simulated trials and twenty-five times a deterministic evaluation: enough to score candidates and to stand in for the pipeline as the validation of a KC–KC-carried block, not yet cheap enough to sit inside a fit.

**The glomerulus gain above 1.5 makes a smaller block more robust and lowers the agreement with both KC matrices.** The other candidate carrier of a noise-robust block, a drive large enough that the block's carriers sit well above threshold, was tested along the gain axis: the eight-glomerulus gain at 1.75 and 2, fitted from the gain-1.5 optima at both per-claw thresholds and scored through the trial-noise pipeline, with the no-gain and gain-1.25 fits run through the same pipeline as the lower anchors. On the reference base (40 Hz) the block through the noise falls above 1.5 ({{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5:block_raw ~ +.3f}} at 1.5, {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.75:block_raw ~ +.3f}} at 1.75, {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain2:block_raw ~ +.3f}} at 2; the data {{data:block ~ +.3f}}) with the deterministic block ({{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5:block_det ~ +.3f}} to {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain2:block_det ~ +.3f}}), the APL-off response rate overshoots ({{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.75:rate_trial_off ~ .3f}} and {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain2:rate_trial_off ~ .3f}} against the data's {{data:tnt_frac ~ .3f}}) and the loss rises from {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5:loss_recorded ~ .2f}} to {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain2:loss_recorded ~ .2f}}. At 70 Hz the share of the deterministic block that survives the noise rises with the gain (three-trial means {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25:block_raw ~ +.3f}}, {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.5:block_raw ~ +.3f}}, {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.75:block_raw ~ +.3f}}, {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain2:block_raw ~ +.3f}} at gain 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2) while the deterministic block falls ({{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25:block_det ~ +.3f}} to {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain2:block_det ~ +.3f}}) and the loss rises from {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.5:loss_recorded ~ .2f}} to {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain2:loss_recorded ~ .2f}}. At both thresholds ρ on odor-odor distances with the two KC matrices falls along the axis (40 Hz: {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5:rho_raw_on ~ .2f}} / {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5:rho_raw_off ~ .2f}} at 1.5 to {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain2:rho_raw_on ~ .2f}} / {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain2:rho_raw_off ~ .2f}} at 2; 70 Hz: {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.5:rho_raw_on ~ .2f}} / {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.5:rho_raw_off ~ .2f}} to {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain2:rho_raw_on ~ .2f}} / {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain2:rho_raw_off ~ .2f}}), as do the natural-source alignment and the structure beyond the ORN input: the gain's contribution to noise-robustness is spent by 1.5, and above it the gain is closed for the agreement with the two KC matrices as KC–KC inhibition is bounded by the APL-off per-odor spread. The lower anchors reverse the picture for the block: the two no-gain fits, which the loss ranks worst, carry the most block through the noise of any fitted model ({{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1:block_raw ~ +.3f}} three-trial means / {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1:block_grand ~ +.3f}} grand mean at 40 Hz with KC–KC {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1:kc_kc_inh ~ .0f}}, the data's measured {{data:block ~ +.3f}}; {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1:block_raw ~ +.3f}} / {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1:block_grand ~ +.3f}} at 70 Hz with KC–KC {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1:kc_kc_inh ~ .0f}}), reach the data's APL-on single-trial floor and, at 40 Hz, the best APL-off agreement with the KC matrix in the noise tables ({{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1:rho_raw_off ~ .2f}}), at the cost of the APL-off response rate ({{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1:rate_trial_off ~ .3f}} and {{E58/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1:rate_trial_off ~ .3f}} against {{data:tnt_frac ~ .3f}}) and its per-odor pattern: across the table the block through the noise follows the fitted KC–KC scale, not the gain, so the gain and KC–KC inhibition are the two ways the fits make the deterministic block and only the KC–KC way survives the noise, which the deterministic loss cannot see. The two-ρ fit ([below](#gap-pairs)) later separated the two for the no-gain base: re-fitted out of the strong-KC–KC regime, it keeps {{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_nogain_rho:block_raw ~ +.3f}} through the noise (three-trial means) with KC–KC at {{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_nogain_rho:kc_kc_inh ~ .0f}}, so across the gain the block through the noise goes with the absence of the gain rather than with the KC–KC scale.

**A fit whose block term sees the noise finds the KC–KC regime by itself, carries no more block through the noise than the held fits, and moves the main target the wrong way.** Two changes made the [network-aware margin proxy](#fn-network-proxy) cheap enough to be a term of the loss: the thirty trials are replayed together in one pass of the time loop, and the iteration is damped (each KC's KC–KC inhibition in the third pass comes from the mean of the first two passes' spikes). For the eight glomeruli at 1.25 at 70 Hz the second pass over both conditions then takes {{noise_proxy_batched_relax/per_model_pass.csv:model=E45-thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3;pass=2:pass_seconds ~ .0f}} s where the per-trial form took {{noise_proxy_batched_relax/per_model_pass.csv:model=E45-thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3;pass=2:e56_pass_seconds ~ .0f}} s, and three damped passes give the pipeline's block through the noise over the same {{noise_proxy_batched_relax/agreement_pass.csv:key=block_raw;pass=3:n ~ .0f}} models to a mean absolute error of {{noise_proxy_batched_relax/agreement_pass.csv:key=block_raw;pass=3:mae ~ .3f}} (ρ {{noise_proxy_batched_relax/agreement_pass.csv:key=block_raw;pass=3:spearman ~ .2f}} across the models); undamped, the passes oscillate around the pipeline's value. That base was then re-fitted from its optimum with the structure loss, its block term computed on the proxy's block through the noise instead of the deterministic block, every other term deterministic and the KC–KC scale free, and the fitted model was run through the trial-noise pipeline. The optimizer went where the fits with the KC–KC scale held had been put by hand: KC–KC from {{E45/fits.csv:thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3:kc_kc_inh ~ .0f}} to {{E60/fits.csv:tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3:kc_kc_inh ~ .0f}} and the somatic threshold from {{E45/fits.csv:thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3:fixed_thr ~ .0f}} to {{E60/fits.csv:tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3:fixed_thr ~ .0f}} ({{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc20:fixed_thr ~ .0f}} and {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc30:fixed_thr ~ .0f}} in the fits held at 20 and 30), with both APL scales raised and the response rates kept. The block a recording of the model would show rose from the seed's {{E48/partB/onoff_noise.csv:model=E45-thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3;level=match_on;transform=graded;matrix=raw:block ~ +.3f}} to {{E60/partB/onoff_noise.csv:level=match_on+match_off;transform=graded;matrix=raw:block ~ +.3f}} (three-trial means), which is the held fits' level ({{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc20:block_raw ~ +.3f}} at 20, {{E53/comparison.csv:key=thr70_gain1.25_kckc30:block_raw ~ +.3f}} at 30) and under half the data's {{data:block ~ +.3f}}, while the deterministic block fell from {{E45/fits.csv:thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3:fit_block ~ +.3f}} to {{E60/fits.csv:tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3:fit_block ~ +.3f}}. It got there at a smaller cost in the APL-off statistics than the held fits: the response rate with APL off and most of its per-odor pattern are kept (table below). The block through the noise can therefore be steered by a fit, and the criterion is not what limits it: free to choose, the optimizer takes KC–KC inhibition as the carrier and finds no better regime than the one imposed by hand.

The proxy over-read its own optimum. Inside the fit, run at the seed's matched jitter (σ {{E60/fits.csv:tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3:proxy_sigma ~ .3f}}), it put the block through the noise at {{E60/fits.csv:tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3:fit_proxy_block ~ +.3f}}, {{diff:E60/fits.csv:tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3:fit_proxy_block -- E60/partB/onoff_noise.csv:level=match_on+match_off;transform=graded;matrix=raw:block ~ +.3f}} above what the pipeline measured for the fitted model at its own matched jitter (σ {{E60/partB/onoff_noise.csv:level=match_on+match_off;transform=graded;matrix=raw:sigma ~ .3f}}), well above its mean error over the models it was checked on and at a KC–KC scale inside the range where the passes converge. An optimizer finds where a surrogate errs in its favour, so the proxy can steer a fit, but the number to quote for a fit made on it is the pipeline's at the optimum.

On the main target the fit moved the wrong way, to where the held fits already were. Its [APL-effect loss](#apl-effect) is {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E60_proxy_fit;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} against the seed's {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E45_gain_at_ctrl_frac;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} (the fits held at 20 and 30: {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E53_kckc_held_block;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_kckc20_k2_cap3:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} and {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E53_kckc_held_block;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_kckc30_k2_cap3:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}; the data's floor {{data:apl_effect_rmse_floor ~ .3f}}), its mean APL effect {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E60_proxy_fit;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}} against {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E45_gain_at_ctrl_frac;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}} (the data {{data:apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}}) and its ρ on the per-pair APL effect {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E60_proxy_fit;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_spearman ~ .2f}} against {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E45_gain_at_ctrl_frac;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_spearman ~ .2f}} (ceiling {{data:apl_effect_rho_ceiling ~ .2f}}); under the calibrated readout of the spike counts ([below](#readout)) the loss rises as well, from {{E65/by_variant_readout.csv:folder=E45_gain_at_ctrl_frac;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=saturating_cal_pr:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} to {{lit:0.107 # the saturating readout calibrated on the APL-on participation ratio (E65_spike_count_readout.score_readout) applied to data/model_outputs/E60_proxy_fit/thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3 spike counts; E60_proxy_fit.md, part B table}}. Three models in this regime, one found by the optimizer and two imposed, sit at the same place on the main target, so the cost belongs to the regime: in it more of the inhibition is carried by the KC–KC connections, which remain when APL is blocked, the somatic threshold is lower and the KCs that respond fire harder, and blocking APL changes the distances less. On this base the block that survives the noise and the main target therefore pull against each other, and a model that is to have both needs a carrier of the block other than the strength of KC–KC inhibition; the candidates are the mechanisms that act on which KCs respond, whose block survives the compressive readout ([below](#readout)). The fitted model is inside all four realism bands (table below). What the bands do not show is its high-firing tail: {{lit:59 # KCs with 20 or more spikes to at least one odor, APL on, counted in data/model_outputs/E60_proxy_fit/thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3_on_spike_counts.parquet; E60_proxy_fit.md, Realism table}} KCs reach 20 spikes to some odor with APL on and {{lit:403 # KCs with 20 or more spikes to at least one odor, APL off, counted in data/model_outputs/E60_proxy_fit/thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3_acute_off_spike_counts.parquet; E60_proxy_fit.md, Realism table}} with APL off, against the seed's {{lit:22 # KCs with 20 or more spikes to at least one odor, APL on, counted in data/model_outputs/E45_gain_at_ctrl_frac/thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3_on_spike_counts.parquet; E60_proxy_fit.md, Realism table}} and {{lit:264 # KCs with 20 or more spikes to at least one odor, APL off, counted in data/model_outputs/E45_gain_at_ctrl_frac/thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3_acute_off_spike_counts.parquet; E60_proxy_fit.md, Realism table}}, the responses on which [a firing ceiling for the KCs](#firing-ceiling) acts. Of the KCs that fire that hard with APL off, {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E60_proxy_fit;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3:n_survivors ~ .0f}} also do so with APL on, against the seed's {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E45_gain_at_ctrl_frac;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3:n_survivors ~ .0f}}, and they hold {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E60_proxy_fit;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3:survivor_share_sq_on ~ .0%}} of an odor's summed squared responses with APL on (the seed's {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E45_gain_at_ctrl_frac;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3:survivor_share_sq_on ~ .0%}}); with these left out of both conditions after the fact, nothing simulated again, the fitted model's mean APL effect is {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E60_proxy_fit;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_proxyP3r_k2_cap3:raw__survivors__mean ~ +.3f}}, above the seed's {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E45_gain_at_ctrl_frac;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} as saved, so the regime's cost on the main target runs through these cells ([the high-firing KCs that survive APL](#high-firing-kcs)).

## The model and its baseline

The model (olfsysm) runs ORNs → projection neurons (with antennal-lobe inhibition) → PN boutons (443 hemibrain boutons) → KC claws (hemibrain claws, about 9,500 claws on 1,732 KCs, about 6 claws per KC) → KC somata, with one APL neuron inhibiting claws and boutons through connectome synapse counts as weights, and somatic thresholds tuned so that 5 % of KCs respond to an odor. The bouton model with connectome APL weights is the baseline for everything below, and "the bouton model" means this model with whatever mechanism the row names added.

APL block is modelled as the acute knockout: the APL output weights set to zero with every other parameter unchanged. The flies express the toxin chronically, so a homeostatic re-tuning of the thresholds after the block is the other candidate comparison; the knockout baseline experiment ran both, and the data's doubling of the response fraction argues against full compensation, so the acute knockout is used throughout. One consequence is stated once here: in every tuned model the threshold is set so that twice the 5 % target respond with APL off, so an APL-off / APL-on response-rate ratio near 2 is built into the tuning, and only the fitted models' ratios are predictions.

The baseline's APL is a uniform gain (isotonic R² 0.99, no block); the model is more ORN-like than the flies with APL output blocked (TNT), agreeing with their matrix better than with the control matrix even with APL on; its APL-on distances are below the noise-corrected level and its APL-off distances above it; and it carries none of the control-specific structure. Two things were therefore missing: a pair-specific APL action, and whatever makes the code with APL output blocked (TNT) less ORN-like than a linear model.

## What made a difference

### The per-claw threshold on the PN→KC transfer: the decorrelation level and the excess sharing

The one KC-level manipulation with leverage was the claw-coincidence rule found in the KC heterogeneity experiment: a KC spikes when two or three of its claws are individually driven above a per-claw threshold. The per-claw saturation experiment then showed what the rule is. A pure cap on each claw's contribution (saturating or hard) moves every statistic the wrong way, whereas a rectified per-claw offset (each claw's input minus a threshold, floored at zero, summed linearly at the soma with the ordinary tuned somatic threshold) reproduces the coincidence models at the rule's own per-claw thresholds (ρ=0.92–0.95 on odor-odor distances between the two, on both wiring bases).

The rule is a threshold, not a bound: a KC should respond because a few of its claws are driven strongly, not because many are driven weakly, and the linear model's spurious shared responders are KCs of the second kind. The offset is a model parameter that keeps somatic integration, so it combines with the mechanisms below; "per-claw threshold" always means this offset, and "somatic threshold" the tuned threshold at the soma.

| statistic | bouton baseline | bouton model<br>per-claw threshold 70 Hz | KC data |
|---|---|---|---|
| mean pair distance, APL on | 0.70 | 0.79 (better) | {{data:ctrl_mean_dist_noise_corrected ~ .2f}} noise-corrected ({{data:ctrl_mean_dist ~ .2f}} as measured) |
| excess shared responders, APL on (block / other) | −0.42 / −0.15 | −0.28 / −0.09 (better) | −0.08 / −0.10 |
| excess shared responders, APL off (block / other) | −0.43 / −0.17 | −0.32 / −0.10 (better) | −0.24 / −0.17 |
| natural-source alignment, APL on / off / on minus off | {{E13:bouton_base:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ +.2f}} / {{E13:bouton_base:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ +.2f}} / {{diff:E13:bouton_base:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp -- E13:bouton_base:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ +.2f}} | {{E16:bouton_thr70:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ +.2f}} / {{E16:bouton_thr70:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ +.2f}} / {{diff:E16:bouton_thr70:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp -- E16:bouton_thr70:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ +.2f}} (better; {{E16:claw-dynamics_thr70:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ +.2f}} / {{E16:claw-dynamics_thr70:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ +.2f}} / {{diff:E16:claw-dynamics_thr70:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp -- E16:claw-dynamics_thr70:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ +.2f}} on the claw-dynamics base) | {{data:ctrl_vcf ~ .2f}} / {{data:tnt_vcf ~ .2f}} / {{data:vcf_apl_effect ~ +.2f}} |

The threshold brings the APL-on decorrelation level to the noise-corrected data and the excess sharing toward the data, and gives the first positive natural-source alignment with APL off; its APL-off distances overshoot the noise-corrected level. It costs agreement with both KC matrices, the per-odor response-rate pattern, and gives no block.

??? Biophysical realizations of the per-claw threshold
The threshold is about 40–70 Hz of PN rate above zero, roughly 1.5–2× a claw's spontaneous drive, and it must be present with APL output blocked (TNT). Candidates:

- a local nonlinearity in the claw itself: each claw is a discrete postsynaptic compartment wrapping one bouton, and a regenerative event there (Ca²⁺ / Na⁺ spikelet, NMDA-like voltage dependence) would make its output to the soma a threshold function of its input;
- subthreshold-activated K⁺ conductances in thin KC neurites (KCs express A-type Shal / Kv4 currents) that shunt small depolarizations, so only strong claw inputs propagate;
- presynaptic: PN→KC release that needs high-rate input (facilitating or priming-limited synapses), an input-rate threshold rather than a postsynaptic one;
- a tonic, subtractive inhibition of every claw from an APL-independent source (glia-derived GABA, or an APL output tetanus toxin does not block, such as electrical coupling), which with rectification is exactly an offset.

The counting rule's "2–3 claws" is then just the number of such events the somatic threshold needs.
???

**Does weak claw input have to count for nothing?** Letting a claw's input below the threshold count with a reduced slope, a quarter or a half, instead of nothing removes most of what the threshold does: the structure beyond the ORN input and the natural-source alignment fall steadily toward the model without a dead zone, the decorrelation level drops, and the weak odors are not recruited, since their PNs sit too far below the knee for a reduced slope to matter. What the slope buys is agreement with the APL output blocked (TNT) matrix, at the cost of more over-sharing among the esters with APL on, and at the fitted combined models it raises the extended loss ({{E41/stats_variants.csv:B_thr40_g0:xl_loss ~ .2f}} to {{E41/stats_variants.csv:B_thr40_g0.25:xl_loss ~ .2f}} with a quarter slope at 40 Hz). The per-claw threshold therefore works as a hard rectifier, which predicts that PN input below about 50 Hz contributes little to a claw's drive onto the soma, against the graded claw calcium reported in imaging.

### Breadth-weighted APL plus KC–KC inhibition: the block

The block needed a pair-specific removal, and the [oracle co-responder experiment](#fn-oracle-coresponders), the first of this page's two [oracle constructions](#fn-oracle), found what has to be removed: the KCs that respond to both ester groups when APL output is blocked (TNT) (8.5–11 % of KCs, broadly tuned, all with claws from the eight glomeruli both ester groups drive). Suppressing exactly those KCs gives the block, the control's excess profile, the best ρ on odor-odor distances with the control matrix of its base and a positive natural-source alignment, while suppressing random KCs or alcohol × aldehyde co-responders does nothing of the kind. The question became how a circuit finds them.

**Breadth-weighted APL** makes each KC's APL→KC weight proportional to a power of its tuning breadth (the number of odors it responds to with APL off, or, in the combined models and joint fits, its total spike count over the 16 odors with APL off): each claw's connectome APL→claw weight is multiplied by its KC's (breadth + 1) raised to the breadth exponent, and the overall KC↔APL scale is re-matched so that the number of responses with APL on is unchanged; the KC→APL weights are not reweighted. Every statistic moved toward the data and the non-specific excess sharing reached the data's level, but the block pairs kept sharing responders: breadth finds the broad KCs, and the remaining co-responders are not broad. A term for claws from the glomeruli shared by the two ester groups (the breadth × shared-input rule) got the block, but it is circular (only the block-specific glomerulus set works; scores defined from the whole panel fail) and anatomically backwards (hemibrain APL→claw synapse counts are *lower* on those glomeruli's claws).

**KC–KC inhibition** supplied the non-circular second ingredient: connectome-weighted inhibition between KCs whose claws contact each other, each KC's filtered spike output subtracted from its neighbours' somatic drive with a 50 ms time constant. Alone it is a weak second APL. On top of breadth-weighted APL it gives the block, the best ρ on odor-odor distances with the control matrix of any bouton model and the control's excess profile, with the per-odor response-rate pattern kept.

**Why it works.** With the threshold re-tuned, the term reassigns responses: the oracle's co-responder set supplies 55–67 % of the fruity / small-ester responses lost but gets only 25–42 % of those gained, so shared responders fall for the block pairs while other pairs keep theirs. A KC's lateral inhibition grows with how many of its connectome neighbours are co-active, which is largest in the dense, overlapping ester populations: the oracle's suppression without the oracle. The weight structure is part of it: with KC identities shuffled or uniform all-to-all weights, the generic effects (decorrelation, excess sharing, the APL-off matrix) are unchanged but the block falls to the noise floor, so the hemibrain's claw-to-claw contacts, which pair KCs sharing calyx neighbourhoods, are what make the term find the ester co-responders.

Costs of the KC–KC term: the APL-off / APL-on response-rate ratio falls (inhibition that survives APL block damps the expansion), the APL-off matrix does not improve, and a slow version (300 ms) does little.

??? Biophysical realizations of breadth-weighted APL and KC–KC inhibition
*Breadth-weighted APL→KC synapses* (weight proportional to (1 + breadth)^k with k about 3): the natural implementation is activity-dependent synaptic scaling, KCs that fire to many odors having strengthened their APL→KC (and KC→APL) synapses by Hebbian or homeostatic plasticity over days; both synapse classes are plastic in the mushroom body. The intrinsic version (more active KCs raise their own threshold) identifies the same KCs but removes sharing proportionally everywhere, so it does not substitute; a purely structural version (broad KCs having more claws, hence more APL contacts) is too weak, since claw count does not matter in the wiring and heterogeneity experiments.

*KC–KC inhibition* with the connectome's claw–claw contacts, acting within about 50 ms:

- direct KC→KC synapses, which the hemibrain has in large numbers in the calyx and peduncle; KCs are cholinergic, but they express the inhibitory muscarinic receptor mAChR-A, which reduces KC odor responses, so cholinergic KC→KC transmission can be net inhibitory (the metabotropic timescale is slower than the model's 50 ms, which the model's onset-dominated response window may exaggerate);
- a KC→APL→KC loop confined to a calyx neighbourhood (APL's output is electrotonically local), admissible only if part of APL's local output survives tetanus toxin (gap junctions or non-vesicular release);
- ephaptic or extracellular K⁺ coupling among the tightly packed KC neurites and somata, a fast, activity-dependent and APL-independent lateral interaction;
- competition for a shared presynaptic resource at the bouton (dense co-active claws deplete it), which the bouton depletion experiment tested and ruled out as implemented.

The required property in all cases is co-activity dependence with the right partners: inhibition that scales with how many of a KC's calyx neighbours (the KCs whose claws contact its claws) are active, not with the KC's own breadth and not with the population as a whole; the first and third have that locality built in, the second only if APL's local output survives the toxin.
???

### APL inhibition of PN boutons: the odor-level drive dependence

Letting APL inhibit the PN boutons (connectome APL→bouton weights, about twice the fitted scale) gives the per-odor APL effect's dependence on ORN drive at matched response counts, with the pair-level statistics unchanged; more antennal-lobe contrast does not. Biophysically this is presynaptic GABAergic inhibition at PN terminals in the calyx (GABA-B receptors on PN boutons), which reduces release in proportion to the bouton's APL input; its effect is glomerulus-wise because a bouton's ~25 claws share it. The data-only confound controls later showed the odor-level effect is largely density, so this is a supporting rather than a primary ingredient; the bouton model is the APL-on reference for the mechanisms above, and the joint fit below shows the APL→bouton pathway is also what sets the APL-off / APL-on response-rate ratio.

### Glomerulus-specific claw strength: the weak odors, and the structure beyond the ORN input

The weak-odor analyses (below) had narrowed the five weak-input odors' problem to the claws of their own glomeruli counting for too little, and [the oracle test](#fn-oracle-gain) confirms it. With the per-claw threshold in place, scaling the PN → claw weights of the eight glomeruli that carry those odors (VA7m, DL1, D for ms; DM1 for B-myr and paa; DP1m, VC1 for euc and -aPine; DC2, DM5 for 1-8ol) by 1.5 recruits the weak odors with the fruity-ester overlap the fly's responders have, halves the over-removal of the block pairs' shared responders, gives the best ρ on odor-odor distances with the control matrix of any threshold model, and lifts the natural-source alignment to the data's level with APL output blocked and nearly to it with APL on, the first model to do so. The same gain on eight random glomeruli does none of this (it recruits nothing and disorders the code), so the effect is the identity of the glomeruli, not extra drive.

The costs are moderate: the strongly driven esters lose responders, so the per-odor response-rate pattern and the mean distance with APL on move away from the data, and the block stays small without breadth-weighted APL and KC–KC inhibition.

**What the gain is not: the anatomy.** Each claw weighted by its PN → KC synapse count, the anatomical version of claw strength the hemibrain offers, recruits paa and -aPine but not ms or B-myr, loses the threshold's natural-source alignment and doubles the over-sharing among the other pairs. The eight glomeruli are not special in anything the model has (their PN peaks rank 14th to 36th of 36, their claws per glomerulus and synapses per claw are ordinary), which is why the per-glomerulus peak normalization did not find them. A direct check of the model's calyx wiring against the hemibrain for these eight glomeruli closes the calyx anatomy as the substrate: PNs are missing from the wiring for most glomeruli (a median of 62 % present), but the [oracle set](#fn-oracle-glomeruli) is not worse off (63 % against 71 % for the others), and in the calyx output represented it is if anything over-represented (more boutons, 10.4 against 7.4 per glomerulus, and more claws per hemibrain calyx presynaptic site); the correction the anatomy would justify is 0.83 to 1.12, centred on 1, against the 1.5 the oracle gain needs.

**Nor the ORN → PN stage, applied uniformly.** Sweeping the model's antennal-lobe normalization and PN gain on the threshold base shows that weakening the normalization moves the weak odors away from the data (their top PNs fall, the esters' rise, and B-myr's responders go from 0.7 % of KCs to none), the default being already the strongest normalization the stage expresses. A uniform PN gain is the per-claw threshold in disguise: doubling every PN rate recruits the weak odors about as the 40 Hz threshold does (ms 2 %, B-myr 3 % of KCs with APL off) at the threshold's costs (the other pairs over-share, the mean distance falls to 0.73, the natural-source alignment is lost), and quadrupling it gives a dense code with no threshold left.

**What the oracle gain stands for** is therefore glomerulus-specific and upstream of the calyx: a glomerulus-specific ORN → PN gain, a release probability the synapse counts do not show, or, most simply, ORN input to these glomeruli that the lab's data understate for these odors by about 1.5× at the PN level, now with a concrete prediction of which glomeruli and by how much. The Hallem-input test (below) supports the last reading for ms and paa and not for B-myr.

??? Biophysical realizations of a glomerulus-specific claw strength
A 1.5× gain confined to a few glomeruli' claws:

- more or larger boutons per PN for those glomeruli than the model's wiring gives them (the model takes bouton counts from the hemibrain, so this would have to be bouton size or release site number rather than count);
- higher ORN → PN gain in those glomeruli (PN input resistance, dendritic integration, or less lateral inhibition in the antennal lobe for narrowly tuned channels), which the model's uniform transform does not represent;
- claw-level synaptic scaling that strengthens claws from glomeruli that are rarely co-active with others (a homeostatic version of the oracle gain);
- no circuit mechanism at all, if the ORN data understate these odors.
???

**The gain in the combined model.** Put on the three-mechanism points, the gain does not add the way the threshold and KC–KC inhibition added to each other: at a fixed 5 % operating point it trades the block for the structure. At the block-first point (threshold 70, breadth 2, KC–KC 100) the block goes from +0.050 at gain 1 to −0.001 at gain 1.5 while the natural-source alignment with APL output blocked rises from 0.10 to 0.40 (data {{data:tnt_vcf ~ .2f}}), and the balanced point (threshold 40, breadth 2, KC–KC 30) does the same (+0.035 to −0.007; 0.18 to 0.37).

The reason is in the excess profile: the gain raises the tuned somatic threshold (74 to 134 Hz at the block-first point), which removes the broad ester co-responders with APL off as well as on (APL-off block-pair excess −0.24 to −0.11; data −0.24), so the on-minus-off difference that is the block disappears; KC–KC inhibition had produced the block precisely by removing those KCs only with APL on. The structure vs wild-type somata falls with the gain too (0.66 to 0.34; data {{data:ctrl_struct_wt ~ .2f}}), so the gain's structure and KC–KC inhibition's are not the same thing. The per-odor response-rate pattern collapses at the block-first point (response rate ρ 0.61 / 0.45 to 0.35 / 0.01) and the response-rate ratio stays at 1.3–1.5 at every point. Whether one parameter set can hold the block and this structure is the joint fit's question.

### The joint fit

The joint fit optimizes one parameter set (the somatic threshold, the KC↔APL loop gain, the APL→bouton scale and the KC–KC scale) against both matrices at once, with a loss that also contains the block, the 5 % operating point, the APL-off / APL-on response-rate ratio, both genotypes' per-odor response-rate patterns and the monotonicity, for a fixed breadth exponent (0 to 3) and, in its second round, a fixed per-claw threshold (40 or 70 Hz). Nine fits in all.
