**Without a per-claw threshold** the breadth-weighted APL plus KC–KC model reaches the data on every fitted target at once: the block at the data's size, the best ρ on odor-odor distances with the control matrix of any bouton model, the data's monotonicity, the APL-off / APL-on response-rate ratio and both per-odor response-rate patterns. It fixed the response-rate ratio by moving inhibition from the KC↔APL loop to the APL→bouton synapses: bouton-level inhibition is APL output too, so blocking APL then releases more responders, which confirms the hint from the bouton depletion experiment that the low ratio of the earlier KC–KC models was a matter of APL recruitment. The loss is nearly flat across breadth exponents 1 to 3, with different trade-offs (exponent 1 the best APL-off matrix and ratio, exponent 2 the best control matrix and per-odor response rates, exponent 3 the least KC–KC inhibition); without breadth weighting the fit still gets most of the block from KC–KC inhibition plus the APL re-balance but loses a little block and a tenth of the control-matrix ρ, which is what breadth weighting is worth in the fit.

**With a per-claw threshold** the joint fit is worse, even when started from the best no-threshold solution: the optimizer removes most of the KC–KC inhibition, both matrix ρ and the per-odor response rates fall, and the response-rate ratio is reached only with an extreme APL→bouton scale. What the threshold buys is the structure the loss does not contain: the structure vs wild-type somata, the natural-source alignment and the data's APL-on excess profile, where the no-threshold fits over-remove the block pairs' shared responders. Under these targets the threshold and KC–KC inhibition compete for the same KCs and the data prefer the latter. What no fit fixes: the APL output blocked (TNT) matrix's natural-source alignment and the block pairs' too-low sharing with APL off; the APL-blocked sharing diagnostic traces that to the model's input for five weakly represented odors, which no KC mechanism can repair.

### The joint fit with the structure targets

The same loss plus three structure terms (the structure vs wild-type somata, the natural-source alignment of the APL-off residual and the APL-on excess profile) selects a per-claw threshold, though which of the two is not resolved across wiring draws (see the replication below). Seeded from their earlier solutions at breadth exponent 2, the 40 Hz and 70 Hz fits reach extended losses of 2.45 and 2.38 against 2.83 without a threshold, entirely through the structure terms (1.09 and 0.94 against 1.74) and at a rising price in the fitted statistics (1.37 and 1.44 against 1.10).

None reaches the structure targets: the structure vs wild-type somata stalls at 0.54 at both thresholds (data {{data:ctrl_struct_wt ~ .2f}}; 0.46 without a threshold, which 140 evaluations did not move), the natural-source alignment reaches +0.25 at best (data {{data:tnt_vcf ~ .2f}}), and the block pairs stay slightly more over-removed than the other pairs (−0.105 against −0.087; data −0.08 against −0.10). The best fit (70 Hz) pays with ρ on odor-odor distances with the control matrix (0.68 to 0.57) and the APL output blocked (TNT) matrix (0.69 to 0.60), the per-odor response rates (0.68 / 0.81 to 0.56 / 0.71), monotonicity (0.91) and the APL-off mean distance (0.72 to 0.78; the noise-corrected data {{data:tnt_mean_dist_noise_corrected ~ .2f}}), while keeping the block (+0.07) and the response-rate ratio (2.0). Its balance puts APL mostly on the boutons (12× the baseline's APL→bouton scale, a value the bound control below shows to mean "at least about 3×", 0.4× its APL→KC scale) under a low somatic threshold, and all three fits keep KC–KC inhibition (scale 28–52) where the earlier threshold fits had let it collapse. Within this family the structure is bought, not added, which is why the next fit puts the oracle glomerulus gain inside the joint fit.

### The combined model: the oracle gain inside the fit

With the gain of 1.5 on the weak odors' eight glomeruli held fixed and the four parameters re-fitted from the structure-target optima, the block and the gain coexist, which they did not at the fixed operating points: both threshold models keep the block (+0.049 and +0.043), reach the data's natural-source alignment ({{data:tnt_vcf ~ +.2f}}) and, for the first time in any fit, the data's order of the APL-on excess profile (the block pairs less over-removed than the other between-group pairs), with monotonicity back above the floor at 70 Hz and the best control-matrix ρ of any threshold model at 40 Hz (0.66). The extended loss falls to 1.94 and 1.93. The fit does it by raising the somatic threshold, shrinking KC–KC inhibition (to 11 and 22) and moving APL further onto the boutons.

The costs are the ones the fixed points showed and one more: response rate ρ with the control flies falls to 0.35 and 0.39 (the esters' response fractions drop as the weak odors are recruited), the structure vs wild-type somata falls from 0.54 to 0.32 and 0.36, and the mean APL-on distance falls to 0.74. The gain pays for what it buys almost entirely out of those two statistics.

**What the gain's response-rate cost is.** Comparing the per-odor response fractions of the fits with and without the gain against the KC data (figure below), the cost is confined to the APL-on condition: against the APL output blocked (TNT) data the gain costs nothing at 70 Hz and helps at 40 Hz. With APL on, three esters and three weak odors carry the loss. 2-but and 2h, the two odors the control flies respond to most, and IaA fall to ranks 10 to 14 of 16, because the gain fit's stronger APL removes 3.1 to 3.6 times as many responders as it leaves (the data: 1.8 to 2.4 times). ms, paa and B-myr, the odors the gain was meant to recruit, remain the three sparsest with APL on: the fit still removes 69 to 84 % of their APL-off responders (the data remove 52 to 59 %), although with APL off the gain brings them close to the data. And 1-8ol and euc, which the data do not place among the sparse odors, are over-recruited.

Behind this is a feature of the data that no fit reproduces: blocking APL output multiplies every odor's response fraction by about the same factor, 2.2, whatever the odor (the spread across odors is at the noise of the recordings), whereas every fit's APL is odor-selective. It removes the sparse odors' responders preferentially (without the gain, a factor 1.7 for the six esters against 2.8 for the weak-input odors and 12 for B-myr) and, with the gain, 2h, IaA and 2-but as well. The section on [what drives APL](#apl-drive) traces this to the model's APL input.

### Robustness: re-drawn wiring and the APL→bouton bound

**On re-drawn wiring.** Every joint fit above rests on one hemibrain wiring. Re-running the three structure-target fits on two re-draws of the bouton wiring (each KC type's claws re-assigned among that type's boutons, the smallest perturbation the wiring-substrate experiment used) keeps part of the conclusion and withdraws part. A per-claw threshold beats no threshold in five of the six comparisons across the three wirings, but which threshold wins flips (70 Hz on the real wiring and the first re-draw, 40 Hz on the second), so the loss selects a threshold and not a particular one. The threshold's gain in natural-source alignment repeats on every wiring; its +0.08 gain in structure vs wild-type somata does not (+0.01 to +0.05 on one re-draw, −0.03 on the other, inside the substrate experiment's ±0.15), and is withdrawn.

Every re-drawn fit is 1 to 2.5 worse in loss and 0.15 to 0.25 lower in structure vs wild-type somata than its real-wiring counterpart while keeping the block and the control-matrix ρ: fitted with the structure targets, the real hemibrain wiring carries structure the re-draws do not, so the absolute structure numbers of every fit on this page are the real wiring's. The same spread sets the scale for reading losses: differences of a few tenths between fits on one wiring are inside the variability between wirings.

|---|---|---|---|---|

| per-claw threshold 40 Hz | 2.45 / 4.79 / 2.94 | 0.54 / 0.30 / 0.32 | +0.20 / +0.13 / +0.28 | +0.060 / +0.024 / +0.055 |
| per-claw threshold 70 Hz | 2.38 / 3.86 / 3.10 | 0.54 / 0.34 / 0.33 | +0.25 / +0.17 / +0.16 | +0.070 / +0.038 / +0.072 |
| per-claw threshold 70 Hz + oracle gain 1.5 | 1.94 / — / 2.50 | 0.32 / — / 0.25 | +0.36 / — / +0.30 | +0.049 / — / +0.040 |
| per-claw threshold 40 Hz + oracle gain 1.5 | 1.93 / — / 2.49 | 0.36 / — / 0.22 | +0.36 / — / +0.29 | +0.043 / — / +0.048 |

The combined model replicates on the re-draw: with the gain the loss falls by about what it fell on the real wiring (0.60 and 0.45 against 0.44 and 0.52), the block is kept (+0.040 and +0.048), the natural-source alignment rises at 70 Hz from +0.16 to +0.30 (on the real wiring from +0.25 to +0.36; at 40 Hz the re-draw's gain-free fit already sat at +0.28), ρ on odor-odor distances with the control matrix rises from 0.55 to 0.71 and from 0.54 to 0.68, the first threshold models at the level of the breadth × KC–KC models, and the APL-on excess profile takes the data's order on the re-draw as well. So the combined model's structure is the gain's own and not the wiring's, with its level about 0.06 lower away from the real hemibrain wiring; the costs, the per-odor response-rate pattern (response rate ρ 0.33 to 0.35 with APL on) and the structure vs wild-type somata, replicate too.

**Is the extreme APL→bouton scale needed?** The structure-target optima put APL on the PN boutons at 8 to 18 times the baseline's tuned scale. Re-running the 70 Hz structure fit from its optimum with that scale capped at 3× the baseline costs 0.08 of extended loss (the fit sits on the cap and gives up only a little of the response-rate ratio), tying it to the APL→KC scale lands at 2.4× for the same loss, fixing it at the baseline's value costs 0.9 (the APL-off / APL-on response-rate ratio collapses to 1.5 and the block to +0.036, because the knockout then releases only the KC-side inhibition), and forcing the APL→KC scale up to at least 0.8× the baseline is worst of all (monotonicity collapses and the blocked flies' per-odor response-rate pattern is lost). So presynaptic APL at a few times the baseline is needed and the loss is flat above it: the large factors in the fitted optima mean "at least about 3×", not a prediction of synaptic strength, and there is no KC-side route in this family. The testable statement for APL imaging is the direction (the knockout's released responders come mostly through the boutons), not the number.

### The reference model: the combined model with interpretable bounds

Re-fitting the combined model (the per-claw threshold, breadth-weighted APL, KC–KC inhibition and the 1.5× gain on the weak odors' eight glomeruli) with the APL→bouton scale capped at 3× the baseline costs nothing: both threshold fits improve on their uncapped versions (70 Hz 1.94 to 1.84, 40 Hz 1.93 to 1.73), with the bouton scale on the cap, the APL→KC scale back up, KC–KC inhibition smaller and the somatic threshold higher. Every gain of the uncapped fits is kept (the block at the data's size, the natural-source alignment at the data, the APL-on excess profile in the data's order, monotonicity 0.92 to 0.95) and the costs are smaller (response rate ρ with the control flies 0.41 / 0.47 against 0.35 / 0.39, the structure vs wild-type somata 0.35 / 0.40 against 0.32 / 0.36).

The capped 40 Hz model is therefore the reference model, with parameters that can be stated: somatic threshold 0.88× the baseline's (200 Hz), 40 Hz per-claw threshold, APL→KC 0.80×, APL→bouton 3.0×, KC–KC 18, breadth exponent 2, gain 1.5. Its loss is the lowest on this wiring, but by less than the spread between wiring draws, so it is the reference for its statistics (the block, the alignment, the excess profile, the control-matrix ρ), not for a rank among the capped fits. A gain of 1.25 does not split the trade but moves along it: it keeps the natural-source alignment and recovers most of the structure vs wild-type somata (0.49 / 0.50) while the block shrinks (+0.039 / +0.046) and the per-odor response-rate cost stays, so that cost belongs to the gain itself (a glomerulus-specific boost that lets the fit's APL remove the esters' responders, see above), not to its size.

### Which glomeruli the oracle gain needs {#gain-decomposition}

Splitting the reference model's gain by glomerulus, with every fitted scale held and the somatic threshold re-tuned to the fit's response rate, separates its two benefits. The natural-source alignment, and nearly all of what the gain does for the extended loss, come from one glomerulus, DC2: on its own it gives the alignment the full set gives (more with APL on), and without it the other seven give none and the structure against wild-type somata collapses. DC2 is not a weak-odor glomerulus. In the lab's ORN data it responds at {{lit:100–206 Hz # apl16_orn_deltas(), DC2 row: 2h, pa, 6al, t2h, 1-8ol, 1-5ol, 1-6ol}} to the alcohols, the aldehydes, 2h and pa, and weakly to the other weak-input odors, so what the structure rewards is a stronger input shared by the alcohols, the aldehydes and two of the fruity esters.

The weak odors are recruited by their own glomeruli (VA7m, DL1 and D for ms; DM1 for paa and B-myr; DP1m and VC1 for the terpenes, which they over-recruit), without the alignment, and each of those five is dispensable for the loss on its own; DM1 is not, since without it response rate ρ with APL on falls. The block needs DM5, which is also the glomerulus that lowers the structure against wild-type somata most. The low response rate ρ with APL on is not a cost of the gain: at the same fitted parameters without any gain it is the same. The 70 Hz fit shows the same pattern, except that DC2 alone loses the block there (extended loss {{E39/stats_variants.csv:thr70__only_DC2:xl_loss ~ .2f}} against {{E39/stats_variants.csv:thr70__full:xl_loss ~ .2f}} for the full set). The gain's structural part is therefore a single, strongly driven glomerulus, and the measurement question splits: the understated-input reading concerns the recruitment glomeruli, while for DC2 the question is whether its drive onto the KCs is stronger than its ORN response suggests.

Re-fitting the joint fit with the gain on fewer glomeruli confirms it. With the gain on DC2 alone the fit reaches an extended loss as low as the eight-glomerulus reference model's or lower (at 70 Hz {{E40/fits.csv:thr70_gain1.5_k2_DC2_cap3:loss ~ .2f}} against {{E39/stats_variants.csv:thr70__full:xl_loss ~ .2f}}); the three-glomerulus core (DC2, DM5, DM1) keeps the response-rate pattern; and Hallem's receptor value for ms (DL1) can replace the gain on the three ms glomeruli, recruiting ms with APL off at {{lit:about half # E40 archive, thr40_input-hallem_shared_ms_gain1.5_k2_core3_cap3_acute_off_responses: ms 0.067 against the data's 0.116}} the fly's fraction at no cost (at ten times Yang's ms concentration, so an upper bound). Every reduced set wins the loss by a different mix: less agreement with both KC distance matrices, more structure against wild-type somata and a better response-rate pattern with APL on. The recruitment glomeruli are what lifts the matrix agreement, since recruiting a weak odor raises it. The differences are within the spread between wiring draws, so the reference model keeps the eight-glomerulus set until a second draw confirms the smaller one.

Re-fitting the three gain sets on a second draw of the wiring (each KC type's claws re-assigned among that type's boutons, the draw the combined model was re-drawn on above) asks whether that ordering is the models' or the draw's. The draw is a harder wiring for every model, and what it takes away is the block, which the re-drawn fits make at only {{E42/fits.csv:thr40_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_gain1.5_k2_cap3:fit_block ~ +.3f}} to {{E42/fits.csv:thr40_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_input-hallem_shared_ms_gain1.5_k2_core3_cap3:fit_block ~ +.3f}} at 40 Hz (the real-wiring fits {{E39/stats_variants.csv:thr40__full:xl_block ~ +.3f}} to {{E40/fits.csv:thr40_input-hallem_shared_ms_gain1.5_k2_core3_cap3:fit_block ~ +.3f}}; data {{data:block ~ +.3f}}). The core with Hallem's ms input stays the lowest-loss model on both wirings at both thresholds, with the same trade as on the real wiring: lower ρ on odor-odor distances with the control matrix than the eight-glomerulus model, and a far better APL-on response-rate pattern. DC2 alone splits by threshold. At 70 Hz it is level with or below the eight-glomerulus model on the draw ({{E42/fits.csv:thr70_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_gain1.5_k2_DC2_cap3:loss ~ .2f}} from its own seed and {{E42/fits.csv:thr70_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_gain1.5_k2_DC2_cap3_start2:loss ~ .2f}} from the eight-glomerulus seed, against {{E42/fits.csv:thr70_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_gain1.5_k2_cap3:loss ~ .2f}}); at 40 Hz it is the worst fit on the draw from either seed ({{E42/fits.csv:thr40_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_gain1.5_k2_DC2_cap3:loss ~ .2f}} and {{E42/fits.csv:thr40_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_gain1.5_k2_DC2_cap3_start2:loss ~ .2f}} against {{E42/fits.csv:thr40_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_gain1.5_k2_cap3:loss ~ .2f}}), with no block. So the reference model's gain set does not shrink to DC2 alone. The reduced set whose advantage over the eight survives the draw is the core with Hallem's ms input, and since that input is an upper bound (ten times the concentration of the KC recordings), the result is a measurement request, DL1's, VA7m's and D's responses to ms at the recordings' concentration, rather than a change of the reference model.

### What drives APL: the per-odor suppression {#apl-drive}

Blocking APL output takes away the same share of every odor's responders in the data, and no model did that. Two things could make the model's APL odor-selective: the algebra of its inhibition, or what APL responds to. The divisive APL inhibition experiment tested both on the bouton model at 5 %.

**The algebra does not matter.** Replacing the subtraction with a division (the drive divided by one plus the inhibition) at the KCs, at the boutons or at both changes almost nothing. For a KC with a hard threshold that is expected: a drive divided by one plus the inhibition crosses threshold exactly when the drive minus the threshold times the inhibition does, so division acts as a shift of the threshold, as subtraction does.

**What APL responds to does.** In the bouton model, used by every model since the bouton base with claw coincidence, APL is driven by the summed input to all claws plus the bouton rates. Its activity then barely varies across odors: its peak during ms is three quarters of its peak during pa, though pa drives about a hundred times as many KCs. Every odor receives about the same threshold shift, and an odor whose few responders sit just above threshold loses most of them. Driving APL by the KCs' spikes instead makes APL follow how many KCs fire, and with the 70 Hz per-claw threshold the suppression becomes as uniform as the data's.

The statistic is the spread of the suppression: per odor, the ratio of the fraction of KCs responding with APL off to the fraction responding with APL on, and the standard deviation of its logarithm across the 16 odors (an odor with no responders counted as half a KC). The data give 0.15, where the noise of the recordings alone gives 0.13. Its ρ with the odor's APL-on fraction is negative when the sparse odors are suppressed most.

| | | | | APL on<br>ρ(control flies, model), computed on {span=2} |
| bouton model<br>5 % with APL on | spread of suppression | ρ of the suppression with the APL-on fraction | esters (fruity and small) / other / weak-input odors | response rates | odor-odor distances |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| **APL driven by KC spikes, subtractive, 70 Hz** | **0.12** | **−0.12** | **1.9 / 2.0 / 1.9** | 0.67 | 0.52 |
| APL driven by KC spikes, subtractive, no per-claw threshold | 0.51 | −0.61 | 2.0 / 2.4 / 2.4 | 0.71 | 0.59 |
| APL driven by KC spikes, divisive at the KCs, no per-claw threshold | 0.17 | −0.63 | 1.8 / 2.2 / 2.1 | 0.71 | 0.58 |

Without the per-claw threshold the bouton rates, the remaining part of APL's input that does not depend on how many KCs fire, keep the spike-driven APL partly selective. The claw base of the APL knockout baseline, whose APL is driven by KC spikes alone, suppresses uniformly without a threshold (spread 0.16). In this base the drive changes which odors keep their responders but not which odors share them: ρ on odor-odor distances with the control matrix is 0.52 to 0.59 in every variant, and there is no block, because the base has neither breadth-weighted APL nor KC–KC inhibition. With spike-driven APL, ms keeps its share of responders, which leaves its deficit entirely in the APL-off condition, the input problem the glomerulus gain addresses.

![Per-odor suppression against the APL-on fraction for the KC data and three models at 70 Hz (top left), then, for every variant, the spread of the suppression across odors, its ρ with the APL-on fraction, the per-odor response rate ρ, ρ on odor-odor distances with the two KC matrices and the mean APL-on distance. Bars left of the line: APL driven by the summed claw input; hatched, right: by KC spikes.](results/E35_divisive_apl/E35_divisive_apl.png)

**KC-spike-driven APL in the combined models.** Put into the fitted models (the structure-loss joint fits with the APL→bouton scale capped at 3×, each re-fitted from the claw-driven optimum), spike-driven APL keeps everything the combined model gained and makes the suppression more even across odors, but does not improve the per-odor response rates. The loss is lower without the oracle gain, the same at 70 Hz with it, and slightly higher at 40 Hz with it (mostly a smaller block); the block, the response-rate ratio, ρ on odor-odor distances with both KC matrices, the natural-source alignment and the structure vs wild-type somata stay where the claw-driven fits had them. The spread of the suppression falls by about half, and at 40 Hz reaches the data's; but the sparse odors still lose relatively more of their responders (the ρ of the suppression with the APL-on fraction stays strongly negative), and response rate ρ with APL on does not move.

What drives APL therefore sets how evenly it suppresses odors, not the fitted models' structure or per-odor response-rate pattern; the remaining response-rate deficit is the oracle gain's own cost and the APL-off response rates of ms, B-myr and paa, which no APL drive changes. Among the models without the oracle gain, the spike-driven fit has the lowest loss (2.24 against 2.46 for its claw-driven counterpart), with the data's mean APL-on distance (0.84) and block (+0.060).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **KC data** | | {{lit:0 # the extended loss of the data itself is 0 by construction}} | ceiling {{data:ctrl_rate_rho_ceiling ~ .2f}} | {{data:block ~ +.3f}} | {{data:supp_spread ~ .2f}} | {{data:supp_rho_on ~ .2f}} |

![The same structure-loss joint fits with APL driven by the summed claw input (grey) and by KC spikes (green): extended loss, spread of the suppression across odors, response rate ρ with APL on, the block, the APL-off / APL-on response rate and ρ on odor-odor distances with the control matrix; dotted lines are the KC data.](results/E36_spike_apl/E36_spike_apl.png)

**Spike-only APL.** The remaining selectivity could come from the bouton rates that still feed the spike-driven APL, or from KC–KC inhibition. Removing the bouton input from the three fits, with every fitted scale held and the somatic threshold re-tuned, makes the suppression much less selective at 70 Hz (less so at 40 Hz), and removing the KC–KC term as well brings the ρ of the suppression with the APL-on fraction to the data's at 70 Hz without the gain; the KC–KC term alone does not. But every manipulation that removes the selectivity also removes most of the block and lowers the response-rate ratio. Re-fitted with APL driven by KC spikes alone, the models win the block and the ratio back with an APL→KC scale up to twice as large, and the selectivity returns with them; response rate ρ with APL on ends lower than with the bouton input. The spike-only fits match the spike-driven ones at 40 Hz and are worse at 70 Hz (the spike-driven fits re-scored with the current loss targets, whose data values moved by at most 0.006 since those fits ran). In this model family the block and an even suppression across odors compete, whatever drives APL; a KC spike's drive to APL [has since been made independent of the integration step](#fn-spike-step) without changing any model here, so the balance between the two inputs is not an artifact of the step.

### Natural-source alignment with APL on and off {#apl-on-off-alignment}

Every table on this page gives the natural-source alignment three ways: with APL on, with APL off and as their difference (APL on minus off). The flies keep most of it with APL output blocked ({{data:tnt_vcf ~ .2f}}, against {{data:ctrl_vcf ~ .2f}} in control flies), so the APL-off value says what the circuit builds without APL, and the difference says what APL adds, which the APL-off value alone cannot show. This table collects the three for the models whose APL mechanism differs.

The target for each column is the same alignment computed from Yang's KC data: the KC distances averaged over recordings for the {{data:n_ref_pairs ~ .0f}} pairs of the 12 odors the APL16 panel shares with the megamat panel, their residual around the linear ORN prediction, and its ρ with the natural-source (VCF embedding) distances. That gives {{data:ctrl_vcf ~ .2f}} for control flies, the APL-on target (90 % interval over recordings {{data:ctrl_vcf.lo ~ .2f}} to {{data:ctrl_vcf.hi ~ .2f}}); {{data:tnt_vcf ~ .2f}} for APL output blocked (TNT), the APL-off target ({{data:tnt_vcf.lo ~ .2f}} to {{data:tnt_vcf.hi ~ .2f}}); and {{data:vcf_apl_effect ~ +.2f}} for their difference ({{data:vcf_apl_effect.lo ~ +.2f}} to {{data:vcf_apl_effect.hi ~ +.2f}}). These are targets, not ceilings: the natural-source distances are an outside reference, so a model can score above the flies without being closer to them, and the intervals give how precisely the recordings fix each target.

In every model here, APL off is the acute knockout described in the model section: the APL→KC and APL→bouton weights set to zero, nothing re-tuned. A model's APL-off value therefore depends only on its non-APL parts, and how its somatic threshold was chosen decides whether that value changes from one row to the next:

- **Threshold set with APL off.** The threshold is set so that twice the 5 % target respond with APL off, and the APL scale is then lowered until 5 % respond with APL on (the model's standard tuning). Models built this way on the same base share one APL-off code: the baseline, breadth-weighted APL and the doubled APL→bouton scale share the bouton model's, and the three forms of APL at 70 Hz share theirs. Their APL-off values are identical, and an APL mechanism's whole effect is in the APL-on column.
- **Threshold re-tuned with APL on.** Adding KC–KC inhibition or the glomerulus gain changes the drive, so the threshold is searched again until 5 % respond with APL on, with the APL scale held. KC–KC inhibition stays active with APL off, so these models' APL-off response rates and alignment differ from their base's.
- **Fitted jointly.** The threshold and the APL→KC, APL→bouton and KC–KC scales are fitted together against a loss that contains both conditions (5 % responding with APL on, an APL-off / APL-on response-rate ratio near the flies'). The threshold is then a compromise between the two conditions, and changing only the APL scales or their bounds (as in the APL→bouton bound control above) moves the APL-off values through the threshold and KC–KC strength the fit picks to compensate.

Read together, the two columns say what the APL-off score alone misses. The APL mechanisms change the alignment only through the APL-on column: breadth-weighted APL adds more than twice the flies' estimate, at the upper end of its interval, KC–KC inhibition raises both columns, doubling the APL→bouton scale does little, and APL driven by KC spikes or divisive at the KCs lowers it. Without the oracle gain the models get most of their alignment through APL, from an APL-off level well below the flies'. With the gain the APL-off value reaches the flies', and the model's APL then removes alignment instead of adding it: a little in the reference model, at the edge of the flies' interval, and clearly at the grid point with the gain. The flies' own APL contribution is uncertain, its interval reaching from about zero to the size of the models' contributions without the gain. No model reaches the flies' APL-on value.

## What did not matter, and what that rules out

- **Where APL acts spatially (local APL units):** 529 connectome-defined APL compartments at any coupling give the same geometry as one APL (ρ=0.997–0.999 on odor-odor distances between the two): the units carry odor-specific patterns, but each KC averages over about 6 claws spread through the calyx. Spatially local APL is not a mechanism at the KC level.
- **PN→KC wiring (the wiring substrate experiment):** no wiring (hemibrain claws, boutons, random, shuffled) reproduces the data's structure vs wild-type somata better than a 20-seed random null (0.27 ± 0.13); the real claw wiring scores below its own shuffles. The substrate is not the source.
- **How the model reads the hemibrain claw segmentation (multi-bouton claws and synapse-count claw weights):** 3.5 % of claws take synapses from two boutons of their PN; putting each in its dominant bouton instead of the lowest-numbered one, or splitting it, changes 3 % of the responding KCs and nothing else (ρ=0.997 on odor-odor distances with the default model). Weighting each claw by its number of PN→KC synapses (median 15, spread 0.43 of the mean, mostly within glomerulus) changes which KCs respond (79 % of responders) and raises ρ on odor-odor distances with the control matrix from 0.57 to 0.60 and with the APL output blocked (TNT) matrix from 0.69 to 0.73 (better; the ceilings are {{data:ctrl_ceiling ~ .2f}} / {{data:tnt_ceiling ~ .2f}}), a mild move toward fewer, stronger claws per KC, but leaves the block absent (−0.007; data {{data:block ~ +.3f}}) and adds no structure beyond the ORN input. The default construction is kept; the synapse weights are a base variant (used in the oracle glomerulus gain experiment's anatomical arm).
- **KC heterogeneity:** per-type thresholds and per-type APL weights leave the geometry unchanged; homeostatic per-KC thresholds are degenerate at 5 % on 16 odors. Thresholds that scale with a KC's claw count were reported in that experiment as changing nothing, but its runs did not apply them (every KC received the same threshold). Applied, in the input-scaled KC threshold experiment, they are not neutral: they move the release of KCs, when APL is removed, off the many-claw cells, as the flies' numbers of odors per KC ask, and the main target pays for it ([below](#input-scaled-threshold)).
- **Readout window and time constants:** model spikes peak 50–150 ms after onset and die during the pulse; no window shows the block; APL and claw time constants only move the response rate.
- **Harder inhibition of the shared-drive claws, the bouton base with claw coincidence, panel-general glomerulus scores, a per-claw cap, bouton vesicle depletion:** each moved some statistics but not the block (depletion moved every statistic the wrong way), and the APL-synapse anatomy check (APL synapses anti-correlated with the glomeruli that need them) argues against any static, glomerulus-targeted APL rule.
- **Fixes for the weak-input odors** (per-glomerulus drive normalization, a softer per-claw knee, input scaling for the terpenes and 1-8ol, a supralinear per-claw transfer): each is negative; the weak-odor section below has what they changed and why.
- **Structured centrifugal input to the calyx:** the hemibrain's calyx-projecting LHCENT cells, driven through a lateral-horn layer of 1291 neurons built from the PN → LHN synapse counts, are broadly tuned (every cell to every odor, strongest for the small esters, at most a threefold range across odors), and MB-C1 takes 59 % of its input from KCs inside the calyx, so it is a calyx feedback neuron like APL (its output goes to the gamma KCs) rather than a lateral-horn relay. Inhibition at the KC somata at any strength changes nothing. Inhibition at the boutons is a glomerulus-specific gain cut on the VA4, DM1, DM4, DM3 and DP1m boutons that silences 2-but (0.113 → 0.014 of KCs with APL output blocked; the fly 0.193), B-myr (0.020 → 0.004; fly 0.105) and half of paa, and the pair effects and higher matrix ρ it produces (control 0.57 → 0.63; APL output blocked (TNT) 0.69 → 0.77) are what silencing those odors looks like: the per-odor response-rate pattern collapses (response rate ρ 0.68 / 0.80 → 0.31 / 0.45; ceiling {{data:ctrl_rate_rho_ceiling ~ .2f}} / {{data:tnt_rate_rho_ceiling ~ .2f}}), the APL-on block-pair excess goes from −0.42 to −0.58 (data −0.08), the block (−0.02) and the natural-source alignment (−0.04) do not appear, the small-ester / aldehyde divergences never move, and B-myr diverges from the other terpenes where the data converge. The pathway is not a source of category structure; MB-C1 would belong in a KC-driven, gamma-biased feedback model, a different question.
- **The algebra of APL's inhibition (divisive APL inhibition):** dividing the drive instead of subtracting from it, at the KCs, the boutons or both, leaves the per-odor suppression as selective as before; at a hard threshold division acts as a shift of the threshold (see [what drives APL](#apl-drive)).
- **The 5 % operating point:** fitting the reference model at the control flies' response fraction instead leaves its block, its agreement with both KC matrices and its structure where they were; it changes the response fractions, chiefly the weak odors' (see [the operating point](#operating-point)).
- **The data-only confound controls:** half of the APL-block similarity change is density; the isotonic R² of 0.94 is at the measurement ceiling (0.99 in a model is not a failure); the odor-level drive effect is confounded with density change; the only robust pair-specific signature is the block.

## Where the remaining gaps come from: five weak-input odors, and single-claw recruitment

Every model with the per-claw threshold shares too little among the non-ester pairs when APL output is blocked (TNT), and no fit repairs it. Three analyses located the problem, four model-side fixes failed, and the alternative input that exists was tried, which together narrow what is left to do.

**The diagnostic.** Comparing six archived models with the APL output blocked (TNT) data pair by pair, the missing sharing sits in the 70 pairs of five odors: ms, B-myr, euc, paa and 1-8ol. In the modelled ORN input these drive few of the 36 glomeruli, weakly (ms and B-myr, both at the exact concentration, reach 50 Hz in only three glomeruli; their largest ORN response is 73–79 Hz against a median of 150 Hz), so the models recruit 0.2–8 % of KCs for them with APL output blocked (TNT) where the fly recruits 10–14 %, and odors that recruit few KCs cannot share them. The ester, fruity-ester and aldehyde pairs are at or above the data's sharing. The per-claw threshold makes it worse: its response fractions with APL output blocked (TNT) follow the number of glomeruli an odor drives above 70 Hz (ρ=0.74, against 0.49 in the data), which is also the threshold's per-odor response-rate cost.

**What the fly does with them.** In Yang's recordings ms recruits a channel of its own: the narrowest and most exclusive responders of the panel (45 % of its control responders respond to nothing else, against 9–31 % for other odors), 11.6 % of KCs with APL output blocked (TNT), co-tuned with glomerulus D's ORN profile rather than with DL1 or with its own. In the hemibrain wiring 4.7 % of KCs carry two or more claws from DL1 or D and 7.6 % from DL1, VA7m or D, so at least a tenth of the fly's ms responders, and more if not every such KC responds, are recruited through a single claw of these glomeruli or through glomeruli the ORN data do not place among ms's. paa is a milder case of the same. The terpenes and 1-8ol are different: they are recruited through ordinary broad KC populations whose co-tuning follows their ORN profiles (right glomeruli, understated drive), except that B-myr's responders co-tune with the other terpenes in a way the ORN input does not predict.

| odor | fraction of KCs responding, APL output blocked (TNT): fly | threshold model | what the analyses found |
|---|---|---|---|
| ms | 0.116 | 0.002 | own narrow, exclusive channel; part of it single-claw recruitment (glomerulus D) |
| B-myr | 0.105 | 0.007 | weak, few-glomerulus input; responders co-tune with the other terpenes |
| euc | 0.102 | 0.053 | right glomeruli, understated drive |
| 1-8ol | 0.103 | 0.048 | right glomeruli, understated drive |
| -aPine | 0.060 | 0.066 | already at the data |
| paa | — | under-recruited | milder version of ms's channel |

**Four fixes that did not work.**

| fix (bouton model with the 70 Hz per-claw threshold unless stated) | what it changed | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| per-glomerulus drive normalization: each driven glomerulus's PN→claw weights scaled toward the median peak, at half or full strength | half strength leaves the per-odor response rate ρ where the threshold put it (0.57 → 0.60; ceiling {{data:ctrl_rate_rho_ceiling ~ .2f}}); full strength inverts the pattern (0.28–0.33) by over-recruiting the boosted glomeruli's odors; at the grid's balanced point it improves ρ on odor-odor distances with both KC matrices (0.64 → 0.68, 0.68 → 0.74) but halves the block and collapses the response-rate pattern (0.65 → 0.30) | dropped: the data's recruitment follows input strength, which the threshold exaggerates and the gain removes |
| a softer per-claw knee (softplus, width 10–40 Hz) | interpolates between the hard threshold and none: response rate ρ=0.57 → 0.67 as decorrelation erodes (mean distance 0.79 → 0.76; the noise-corrected data {{data:ctrl_mean_dist_noise_corrected ~ .2f}}); ms stays at 0.2 % of KCs at every width | the knee stays hard; no transfer applied alike to all claws recruits KCs from one glomerulus |
| input scaling for the terpenes and 1-8ol (× 1, 1.5, 2, 3) | × 1.5 matches euc's and 1-8ol's fractions, overshoots -aPine twofold and leaves B-myr at half; their pairs' sharing deficit falls from +0.075 to +0.048 but the part with the fruity esters (+0.10) stays at every factor, because the extra drive recruits KCs from the scaled odors' own glomeruli, not the ester-shared ones; response rate ρ falls from 0.57 / 0.75 to 0.30 / 0.31 | keep the unscaled input; the data want terpene responders that overlap the fruity-ester responders |
| a supralinear per-claw transfer (each claw's input above the knee raised to the power 1.5, 2 or 3 relative to 50 Hz; also on the 40 Hz threshold) | no power recruits the weak odors: ms and B-myr fall from 0.2 % and 0.7 % of KCs to zero, euc and 1-8ol fall (0.053 → 0.033, 0.048 → 0.010), only paa (0.076 → 0.205; fly 0.137) and -aPine gain, and the strongly driven esters gain more; at power 3 the ester over-sharing disappears and ρ on odor-odor distances with the APL output blocked (TNT) matrix rises (0.60 → 0.66), but with the control matrix it falls (0.53 → 0.49), the response-rate pattern falls (0.57 / 0.75 → 0.48 / 0.53) and the block stays absent | the model's PN rates are compressed (no PN above 134 Hz; the esters' strongest PNs are no higher than the weak odors'), so odors differ in how many PNs clear the knee (esters 5–7, weak odors 2–3) and any transfer applied alike to every claw rewards the esters' breadth |

**What this leaves.** The per-claw-transfer family is closed: a cap, a soft knee, a convex power and the hard knee itself all leave the weak odors unrecruited, and scaling their input recruits them without the fly's fruity-ester overlap. The reason is at the PN level (table below): the ORN-to-PN transform compresses rates so that no odor has an unusually strong PN; the weak odors drive two or three PNs above the knee where the esters drive five to seven, and any rule applied alike to every claw rewards breadth. What would recruit them is glomerulus-specific: claws from their glomeruli counting for more, which the oracle gain confirmed (above), while the hemibrain's per-claw synapse counts do not supply that gain and the centrifugal pathway does not either. The input side itself remains uncertain for the five odors (concentration, glomerular profile); the next subsection tests it with the one alternative dataset.

| odor | strongest PN rate in the model (Hz) | PNs above the 70 Hz knee | fraction of KCs responding, APL output blocked (TNT): fly / threshold model |
|---|---|---|---|
| ms | 96 (VA7m) | 2 | 0.116 / 0.002 |
| paa | 124 (DM1) | 2 | 0.137 / 0.076 |
| B-myr | 91 (DM1) | 3 | 0.105 / 0.007 |
| euc | 109 (DP1m) | 4 | 0.102 / 0.053 |
| 1-8ol | 97 (DC2) | 5 | 0.103 / 0.048 |
| -aPine | 134 (VC1) | 3 | 0.060 / 0.066 |
| 2h (fruity ester) | 84 (DM3) | 6 | 0.216 / 0.098 |
| pa (fruity ester) | 111 (DM3) | 7 | 0.173 / 0.209 |
| eb (small ester) | 105 (DM2) | 5 | 0.213 / 0.187 |

### Which pairs carry the gap in ρ on odor-odor distances {#gap-pairs}

The agreement of the model's odor-odor distances with the two KC matrices is scored beside [the main target](#apl-effect), which does not see what a model gets wrong in both conditions alike, and ρ on odor-odor distances is one number over 120 pairs. Spearman's ρ is one minus a constant times the sum over pairs of the squared rank residual (a pair's rank among the 120 distances in the model minus its rank in the KC data), so the gap, one minus ρ, splits exactly into a share per pair and per odor. The per-pair decomposition of the gap did this for the reference model, the first joint fit (the best APL-on ρ of any model), the best APL-off fit (the structure-loss fit on a re-drawn wiring), the bouton baseline, the centrifugal input and the ORN input itself, against the control flies with APL on and against APL output blocked (TNT) with APL off.

![Each pair's rank in the model minus its rank in the KC data (of 120 pairs; red: the model puts the pair further apart than the flies do, blue: closer), for the reference model, the first joint fit, the best APL-off fit and the ORN input, with APL on against the control flies (top) and with APL off against APL output blocked (TNT) (bottom). The paa and B-myr rows and the 2-but column carry the saturated cells in every model; in the ORN input they are paler.](results/E61_gap_decomposition/E61_rank_residuals.png)

The gap is not where the fits were scored. The nine fruity × small-ester block pairs and the 13 within-group pairs together carry a tenth of it; the 75 pairs of one of the six weak-input odors (ms, paa, euc, -aPine, B-myr, 1-8ol) carry most of it, more than they do of the ORN input's own gap, and three odors carry two thirds: paa, B-myr and 2-but. The two conditions mis-order the same pairs (the reference model's residuals with APL on and with APL off agree at ρ 0.69), so the APL-off gap is mostly the APL-on gap carried over, with the wrong per-pair APL effect (the models' is half the data's and lands on other pairs) as the smaller remainder. Dropping the strongly driven odors' pairs (2h, 6al, the alcohols other than 1-8ol) lowers ρ: the model orders them better than the input does.

The misses have two signs. paa, B-myr and 2-but are far too close to each other in the model: the three recruit nearly the same KCs, so the model puts paa / B-myr at a cosine distance of {{E61/worst_pairs.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=on;pair=paa / B-myr:model_distance ~ .2f}} where the control flies have {{E61/worst_pairs.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=on;pair=paa / B-myr:data_distance ~ .2f}} (nearly orthogonal), and 2-but / paa and 2-but / B-myr likewise; paa / euc and paa / -aPine are too close as well. Every model does this, with or without the glomerulus gain and the per-claw threshold, and the ORN input has the same pairs too close at about half the rank residual, so the input carries the seed of it and the KC stage amplifies it: the three odors' modelled input drives two or three glomeruli strongly, with DM1 the strongest PN of both paa and B-myr (the PN table above), so they recruit the KCs that carry those glomeruli and few others. 1-8ol and ms have the opposite sign, too far from everything, in both conditions and in the input itself (their pairs with 1-6ol are missed by the input by as much), which is the APL-blocked sharing deficit above seen from the other side. With APL off, paa adds a third miss: in flies with APL output blocked paa comes close to the aldehydes t2h and 6al and the model keeps them apart. The models with the eight-glomerulus gain miss pairs of their own more than the models without it: the reference model's residuals follow the ORN input's at ρ {{E61/residual_correlations.csv:condition=on;model_a=reference 40 Hz;model_b=ORN input:spearman ~ .2f}} with APL on and {{E61/residual_correlations.csv:condition=off;model_a=reference 40 Hz;model_b=ORN input:spearman ~ .2f}} with APL off, the first joint fit's at {{E61/residual_correlations.csv:condition=on;model_a=first joint fit;model_b=ORN input:spearman ~ .2f}} and {{E61/residual_correlations.csv:condition=off;model_a=first joint fit;model_b=ORN input:spearman ~ .2f}} and the best APL-off fit's at {{E61/residual_correlations.csv:condition=on;model_a=best APL-off fit (re-drawn wiring);model_b=ORN input:spearman ~ .2f}} and {{E61/residual_correlations.csv:condition=off;model_a=best APL-off fit (re-drawn wiring);model_b=ORN input:spearman ~ .2f}}; the gain, which bought the block and the natural-source alignment, moved the disagreement with the KC matrices off the input's own misses and onto the model, consistent with the two ρ's falling along the gain axis (the gain as the carrier of a noise-robust block, above). The centrifugal input is the one model unlike the rest (its residuals agree with the reference model's at {{E61/residual_correlations.csv:condition=on;model_a=reference 40 Hz;model_b=centrifugal input:spearman ~ .2f}} / {{E61/residual_correlations.csv:condition=off;model_a=reference 40 Hz;model_b=centrifugal input:spearman ~ .2f}}) and the only one with paa / B-myr near the data, because it adds structure at the KCs that does not come from the ORN input; that is how it reaches the best APL-off ρ while losing the per-odor pattern and the block.

**The KCs behind the collapse.** In the reference model {{E61/shared_kcs/triple.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=on:all_three}} KCs respond to all three of paa, B-myr and 2-but with APL on ({{E61/shared_kcs/triple.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=on:expected_all_three ~ .1f}} expected if the three responder sets were independent) and {{E61/shared_kcs/triple.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=off:all_three}} with APL off ({{E61/shared_kcs/triple.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=off:expected_all_three ~ .1f}} expected); paa's and B-myr's responders overlap at {{E61/shared_kcs/overlap.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=on;pair=paa / B-myr:ratio ~ .1f}} times the independent expectation against {{E61/shared_kcs/overlap.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=on;pair=paa / B-myr:data_ratio ~ .1f}} in the control flies, whereas the terpenes' overlaps (B-myr with euc and -aPine, euc with -aPine) are as large in the flies as in the model. The shared KCs carry claws from DM1 ({{E61/shared_kcs/glomerulus_enrichment.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=on;set=paa & B-myr & 2-but;glomerulus=DM1:frac_set ~ .0%}} of them against {{E61/shared_kcs/glomerulus_enrichment.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=on;set=paa & B-myr & 2-but;glomerulus=DM1:frac_all ~ .0%}} of all KCs) and DP1m ({{E61/shared_kcs/glomerulus_enrichment.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=on;set=paa & B-myr & 2-but;glomerulus=DP1m:frac_set ~ .0%}} against {{E61/shared_kcs/glomerulus_enrichment.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=on;set=paa & B-myr & 2-but;glomerulus=DP1m:frac_all ~ .0%}}), the two gained glomeruli that all three odors drive above the per-claw knee, and for every shared KC one glomerulus drives it for both odors. Neither the gain nor the input's size makes the collapse: with DM1's gain removed paa / B-myr sits at a cosine distance of {{E39/model_pair_distances_long.csv:tag=thr40__loo_DM1;condition=on;odor_a=paa;odor_b=B-myr:distance ~ .2f}} with APL on and with no gain at {{E39/model_pair_distances_long.csv:tag=thr40__none;condition=on;odor_a=paa;odor_b=B-myr:distance ~ .2f}}, against {{E39/model_pair_distances_long.csv:tag=thr40__full;condition=on;odor_a=paa;odor_b=B-myr:distance ~ .2f}} with the full gain and {{E61/worst_pairs.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=on;pair=paa / B-myr:data_distance ~ .2f}} in the control flies, and the rate oracle's scales leave it where it is. DM1 is the strongest ORN glomerulus of paa and of B-myr and the third of 2-but; the per-claw threshold reduces each odor to its strongest glomeruli, and the three meet there. The flies do something else with paa: its responders overlap with the aldehydes' (6al, t2h), and paa's second glomerulus in the ORN input, VL2a, responds to nothing else in the panel but the aldehydes, so the fly's paa channel looks like VL2a's where the model's is DM1's, which the bounded-input oracle (below) can test (DM1 down and VL2a up for paa) and an ORN measurement at Yang's concentrations can settle.

![Left and middle: responder overlap among the weak-input odors and 2-but (KCs responding to both odors over the number expected if the two responder sets were independent, log2 color scale, value printed) for the reference model and for the flies, with APL on against the control flies (top) and APL off against APL output blocked (TNT) (bottom). Right: the glomeruli of the claws of the KCs that respond to all three of paa, B-myr and 2-but, as the fraction of those KCs with at least one claw from the glomerulus, beside the fraction among all KCs (* = one of the eight gained glomeruli).](results/E61_gap_decomposition/shared_kcs/E61_shared_kcs.png)

So ρ on odor-odor distances is to be gained on paa, B-myr and 2-but, whose input is the first suspect ([paa's and the terpenes' modelled input stands in at other concentrations than Yang's](#fn-concentrations)), and the per-odor rate oracle and the bounded-input oracle for the weak odors are to be read on these pairs: if scaling paa's and B-myr's input to the data's response fractions recruits more KCs from the same two or three glomeruli, the pairs stay too close and ρ barely moves, and the glomerular pattern of the input rather than the recruitment pattern is the lever. Experiments on the block bear on ρ on odor-odor distances only through their cost on the weak-input pairs.

The recruitment level was then removed as a cause (the per-odor rate oracle). Each odor's ORN input was scaled so that the reference models' per-odor response fractions equal the KC data's, once with APL on against the control flies and once with APL off against APL output blocked (TNT), at the fitted scales and thresholds, with the agreement of the odor-odor distances as the free readout. It does not move: with the recruitment pattern exactly right with APL on (response rate ρ {{E62/stats_variants.csv:tag=R_thr40_gain1.5_on:spearman_rate_on_vs_data ~ .2f}} against {{E62/stats_variants.csv:tag=R_thr40_gain1.5_untuned:spearman_rate_on_vs_data ~ .2f}} as fitted) the reference model's agreement with the control flies' matrix goes from {{E62/stats_variants.csv:tag=R_thr40_gain1.5_untuned:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman ~ .2f}} to {{E62/stats_variants.csv:tag=R_thr40_gain1.5_on:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman ~ .2f}} and with the APL-output-blocked matrix from {{E62/stats_variants.csv:tag=R_thr40_gain1.5_untuned:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman ~ .2f}} to {{E62/stats_variants.csv:tag=R_thr40_gain1.5_on:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman ~ .2f}}; the scales set on the APL-off fractions give {{E62/stats_variants.csv:tag=R_thr40_gain1.5_off:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman ~ .2f}} and {{E62/stats_variants.csv:tag=R_thr40_gain1.5_off:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman ~ .2f}}; the other two models move by the same few hundredths. The scales needed are modest (2h up by {{E62/scales.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;arm=on;odor=2h:scale ~ .2f}}, B-myr by {{E62/scales.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;arm=on;odor=B-myr:scale ~ .2f}}, ms by {{E62/scales.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;arm=on;odor=ms:scale ~ .2f}}, -aPine down to {{E62/scales.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;arm=on;odor=-aPine:scale ~ .2f}}), and the pairs that carry the gap do not open: paa / B-myr sits at cosine distance {{E62/model_pair_distances_long.csv:tag=R_thr40_gain1.5_untuned;condition=on;odor_a=paa;odor_b=B-myr:distance ~ .2f}} as fitted and {{E62/model_pair_distances_long.csv:tag=R_thr40_gain1.5_on;condition=on;odor_a=paa;odor_b=B-myr:distance ~ .2f}} with the scales, against {{E61/worst_pairs.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=on;pair=paa / B-myr:data_distance ~ .2f}} in the control flies. What the oracle does expose is the per-odor APL effect: B-myr's response fraction is {{E62/scales.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;arm=untuned;odor=B-myr:fraction_on ~ .3f}} with APL on against {{E62/scales.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;arm=untuned;odor=B-myr:data_on ~ .3f}} in the control flies, and {{E62/scales.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;arm=untuned;odor=B-myr:fraction_off ~ .3f}} with APL off against {{E62/scales.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;arm=untuned;odor=B-myr:data_off ~ .3f}} with APL output blocked, so the model's APL suppresses it two to three times too strongly (paa likewise), while it suppresses the broad esters eb, ep and 1-6ol too weakly; the breadth-weighted APL takes the few broad, gained KCs that carry the weak odors. So the gap in the agreement is which KCs an odor recruits, not how many: the glomerular pattern of the weak odors' input, and the APL's per-odor action, are what remain. The APL's per-odor action has since been set by an oracle as well (each odor's input scaled to the flies' response fraction with APL off, then its APL strength to the flies' with APL on): the two ρ's on odor-odor distances move by a few hundredths in opposite directions and [the main target stays where it was](#apl-effect-parts).

**Putting the two ρ's on odor-odor distances first in the loss does not raise them.** The two-ρ fit asked how far the present mechanisms reach when the agreement with the two KC matrices is the objective (its terms at four times their usual weight, the response-rate terms kept as constraints, the block and structure terms unweighted), starting from the structure-loss optimum of each base in the table. Little moved, and no fit raised both ρ's: the reference model went from {{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5_seed:fit_on_spearman ~ .3f}} / {{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5_seed:fit_off_spearman ~ .3f}} (APL on / off, deterministic) to {{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5_rho:fit_on_spearman ~ .3f}} / {{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.5_rho:fit_off_spearman ~ .3f}}, the 70 Hz and gain-1.25 fits about as little, the freed weight going to the response rates at the deterministic block's cost. The no-gain base moved furthest, out of its strong-KC–KC regime, and traded one ρ for the other ({{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_nogain_seed:fit_on_spearman ~ .3f}} / {{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_nogain_seed:fit_off_spearman ~ .3f}} to {{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_nogain_rho:fit_on_spearman ~ .3f}} / {{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_nogain_rho:fit_off_spearman ~ .3f}}). All four end near the ORN input's own agreement with APL on ({{data:rho_ctrl_orn ~ .2f}}), below it with APL off ({{data:rho_tnt_orn ~ .2f}}) and far from the ceilings ({{data:ctrl_ceiling ~ .2f}} / {{data:tnt_ceiling ~ .2f}}): the weighting was not the limit (one start per base), and the gap is in the input or in a mechanism not in hand. The no-gain fit recovers the APL-off response rate and keeps its block through the noise ({{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_nogain_rho:block_raw ~ +.3f}} in three-trial means; the data {{data:block ~ +.3f}}) with KC–KC at {{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_nogain_rho:kc_kc_inh ~ .0f}}, where the gain-1.25 fit at {{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.25_rho:kc_kc_inh ~ .0f}} keeps {{E63/comparison.csv:key=thr40_gain1.25_rho:block_raw ~ +.3f}}: the fragile block goes with the gain, not with a weak KC–KC scale (one fit, one noise seed). None of these fits moves the APL-effect loss (the root mean squared difference between the model's and the KC data's per-pair APL effect, each pair's APL-on minus APL-off distance): the mean APL effect stays at half the data's or less and ρ on the per-pair APL effect far below its ceiling.

**Searching the weak odors' input within a factor of two opens the collapsed pairs and raises both ρ's on odor-odor distances; it changes which pairs APL moves, not how far.** The weak-input odors' input as a bounded oracle tested what was left on the input side, each weak odor's pattern across glomeruli: on the reference model at its fitted scales and threshold, the ORN response of paa, B-myr, 2-but, euc, -aPine, ms and 1-8ol, one odor after the other, was multiplied in every glomerulus by a factor between one half and two, searched for the highest sum of the two ρ's on odor-odor distances with a penalty once the odor's response fraction left ±15 % of the KC data's; 2h and 6al, two strongly driven odors whose pairs the model already orders well, were searched the same way as the control. It is an [oracle](#fn-oracle), and an in-sample one, searched against the same two KC matrices it is scored on with nothing held out and no scale re-fitted: what a corrected input could at most buy, not a measurement. Both agreements rise, with APL on from {{E64/stats_variants.csv:tag=I_untuned:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman ~ .2f}} to {{E64/stats_variants.csv:tag=I_searched_pass1:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman ~ .2f}} (the best earlier model, the first joint fit, has {{E61/rhos.csv:short=first joint fit:rho_on ~ .2f}}) and with APL off from {{E64/stats_variants.csv:tag=I_untuned:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman ~ .2f}} to {{E64/stats_variants.csv:tag=I_searched_pass1:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman ~ .2f}} (the best earlier model, the structure-loss fit on a re-drawn wiring, has {{E61/rhos.csv:short=best APL-off fit (re-drawn wiring):rho_off ~ .2f}}): both in one model, above the ORN input's own agreement in both conditions, and still far from the ceilings (table). The collapsed pairs open, paa / B-myr from a cosine distance of {{E64/model_pair_distances_long.csv:tag=I_untuned;condition=on;odor_a=paa;odor_b=B-myr:distance ~ .2f}} to {{E64/model_pair_distances_long.csv:tag=I_searched_pass1;condition=on;odor_a=paa;odor_b=B-myr:distance ~ .2f}} with APL on ({{E61/worst_pairs.csv:short=reference 40 Hz;condition=on;pair=paa / B-myr:data_distance ~ .2f}} in the control flies) and 2-but's pairs with the two likewise, though paa still does not come close to the aldehydes with APL off. The natural-source alignment with APL on, its APL-on minus APL-off difference and the structure against the wild-type somata, none of them scored by the search, move toward the data at a small cost in the block, while response rate ρ is raised by the penalty itself; the control search raises neither agreement, so a free input within the same bounds is not what buys the gain. B-myr's search is worth the most ({{diff:E64/per_odor_gains.csv:label=weak;pass_no=1;odor=B-myr:rho_on_after -- E64/per_odor_gains.csv:label=weak;pass_no=1;odor=B-myr:rho_on_before ~ +.3f}} with APL on, {{diff:E64/per_odor_gains.csv:label=weak;pass_no=1;odor=B-myr:rho_off_after -- E64/per_odor_gains.csv:label=weak;pass_no=1;odor=B-myr:rho_off_before ~ +.3f}} with APL off), then paa's and 2-but's, the three odors the decomposition named; the other four add a hundredth or less. {{lit:99 of the 252 # counted in results/E64_input_oracle/factors_weak_pass1.csv (factors at 0.5 or at 2); the note of the experiment gives the share, 39 %}} factors end at a bound, so the factor of two binds. For paa the search doubles VL2a (as the shared-KC analysis above expected), halves DP1m, DM4, DL2v and VM7d, lowers DM6 by nearly as much (Hallem's receptor data put it above the lab's value) and leaves DM1 where it was (factor {{E64/factors_weak_pass1.csv:odor=paa;glomerulus=DM1:factor ~ .2f}}; expected down); for B-myr it halves DM1 and doubles DC1 and D. The search thus names paa's VL2a, DP1m, DM6 and DM4 for measurement at 10^-3, the KC data's concentration ([the lab's recording is at 10^-4](#fn-concentrations)); B-myr's recording is already at that concentration, so its searched input is either within the spread of the ORN recordings or a statement about how the model weighs DM1, DC1 and D. On [the main target](#apl-effect) the searched input improves the pattern and not the size: ρ on the per-pair APL effect rises from {{E64/apl_effect.csv:tag=I_untuned:rho ~ .2f}} to {{E64/apl_effect.csv:tag=I_searched_pass1:rho ~ .2f}} (ceiling {{data:apl_effect_rho_ceiling ~ .2f}}; {{E64/apl_effect.csv:tag=I_controls:rho ~ .2f}} after the control search) and the pattern part of the APL-effect loss falls from {{E64/apl_effect.csv:tag=I_untuned:pattern ~ .3f}} to {{E64/apl_effect.csv:tag=I_searched_pass1:pattern ~ .3f}}, still above the {{data:apl_effect_sd ~ .3f}} that an effect with no pattern at all would score, while the mean APL effect stays at half the data's ({{E64/apl_effect.csv:tag=I_searched_pass1:mean ~ +.3f}} against {{data:apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}}), so the loss goes only from {{E64/apl_effect.csv:tag=I_untuned:loss ~ .3f}} to {{E64/apl_effect.csv:tag=I_searched_pass1:loss ~ .3f}} (floor {{data:apl_effect_rmse_floor ~ .3f}}), inside the three bands on the response rates in every column. On the fourth band, the number of odors each KC responds to, the model with the lab's input is just outside with APL off and the searched input brings it just inside ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E64_input_oracle;tag=I_untuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} and {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E64_input_oracle;tag=I_searched_pass1:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} from the flies' distribution, against a band of {{data:tnt_odors_per_kc_tv_max ~ .3f}}), a small move that the control search does not make ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E64_input_oracle;tag=I_controls:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}}). The weak odors' input bears on which pairs APL moves apart, and through them on both matrices; how far APL moves odors apart is not on the input side.

The reference model at its fitted scales and threshold with the lab's ORN input, with the input of the seven weak odors searched (each glomerulus within a factor of two of the lab's response, a penalty on each odor's response fraction outside ±15 % of the KC data's), and with the input of 2h and 6al searched instead

### Hallem's receptor data as the weak odors' input {#hallem-input}

The lab's ORN input and Hallem & Carlson's receptor data (odors at 10^-2; 16 of the model's 36 glomeruli) agree for the esters and aldehydes and disagree for exactly the weak-input odors: ms is a one-glomerulus odor in Hallem (DL1 at 258 Hz against 70 Hz in the lab's data), paa and 1-8ol peak in different glomeruli in the two datasets (DM6 against DM1; VC3 against DC2), B-myr is weak in both, and euc is not in Hallem's set. Putting Hallem's values for ms, paa, B-myr and 1-8ol into the input (on the shared glomeruli, the lab's elsewhere) on the 70 Hz threshold model, one odor at a time, says which of the five odors' deficits the input can explain. Hallem's 10^-2 is ten times Yang's concentration for ms, paa and 1-8ol, so these inputs are upper bounds.

**By odor.** Hallem's ms recruits half the fly's ms population with APL off and Hallem's paa recruits paa at the fly's fraction, both at no cost to any other statistic; Hallem's 1-8ol over-recruits and costs the natural-source alignment; Hallem's B-myr is weaker than the lab's and changes nothing. Every ms responder recruited by DL1 has a second claw from a glomerulus ms drives weakly: one claw at 258 Hz does not fire a KC in this model, so the recruited population is narrower than the fly's with APL off (a breadth of 2.4 odors against 4.9), and the fly's single-claw ms channel, if it is one, needs a mechanism the model lacks.

**In the reference model**, which carries the 1.5× gain on DL1, VA7m and D, Hallem's ms brings ms to the fly's fraction with APL off, but APL then spares the new narrow responders (blocking APL output multiplies ms's response fraction by 1.3, against 2.1 in the fly), because breadth-weighted APL inhibits broad KCs most: a narrow channel is where that rule and the fly part.

**Level is not profile.** Scaling the lab's profiles of the four odors to Hallem's level instead over-recruits ms, paa and 1-8ol (16 glomeruli above the knee for ms instead of two) and gives the highest ρ on odor-odor distances with the APL output blocked (TNT) matrix of any model so far with the per-odor response-rate pattern destroyed: the matrix ρ rewards a weak odor's recruitment whatever its size, which is why it is read together with response rate ρ.

### The operating point: the control flies' 6.8 % instead of 5 % {#operating-point}

Every model on this page is tuned or fitted so that 5 % of KCs respond with APL on, a convention from the lab's earlier modelling, while {{data:ctrl_frac ~ .1%}} of the control flies' KCs respond (and {{data:tnt_frac ~ .1%}} with APL output blocked). Re-fitted with the response-rate target at the control flies' fraction instead, the reference model keeps everything the fit measures: the block, ρ on odor-odor distances with both KC matrices and the structure vs wild-type somata stay where they were at 5 %, response rate ρ with APL on rises slightly, and the loss without its response-rate term is no worse (the fit does it with a stronger APL↔KC loop and a weaker APL→bouton scale). Moving only the somatic threshold to the new operating point, with every other fitted scale held, costs the 40 Hz model almost half of its block, so a threshold-only change of operating point is not neutral. What the operating point does change is the response fractions: at 6.8 % the weak-input odors respond more, and the reference model, whose gain was fitted at 5 %, now over-recruits them with APL off. Its weak odors were already too strong relative to the mean at 5 % (the absolute match there came from an overall APL-off response rate below the data's), so the gain of 1.5 on eight glomeruli is more than the data's operating point needs. In the bouton bases tuned from 3.4 % to 10 %, ρ on odor-odor distances barely moves and the block stays at zero; the weak odors' share of the mean APL-off response rate rises from {{E44/comparison.csv:A_thr70_t0.050:weak_share_off ~ .2f}} at 5 % to {{E44/comparison.csv:A_thr70_t0.068:weak_share_off ~ .2f}} at 6.8 % in the 70 Hz base (data {{data:tnt_weak_share ~ .2f}}), a fifth to a quarter of the way, and the APL-off mean pair distance falls toward the data's as more KCs respond ({{E44/comparison.csv:A_thr70_t0.050:mean_dist_off ~ .2f}} to {{E44/comparison.csv:A_thr70_t0.068:mean_dist_off ~ .2f}}; data {{data:tnt_mean_dist ~ .2f}}). Fitting at the data's fraction costs nothing and compares response fractions with the data at the same mean; the gain is the parameter to re-fit there.

**The gain at the data's operating point.** Re-fitting the gain arms at the control flies' response rate settles three things. The gain is not an artefact of the 5 % convention: without it the fits keep the block but lose the APL-off to APL-on response-rate ratio and the natural-source alignment with APL off, at a much higher loss. At the 70 Hz per-claw threshold a smaller gain on the eight glomeruli (1.25) gives the best fit of all, keeping the block and the agreement with both KC matrices with more structure against wild-type somata; at 40 Hz the block still needs 1.5. And the three-glomerulus core with Hallem's ms input is the best fit at 40 Hz and the only arm whose weak odors take the data's share of the APL-off response, at its usual cost in agreement with the KC matrices. The differences are within the spread between wiring draws, so the reference model is unchanged until a second draw.

**The operating-point fits on a re-drawn wiring.** The two best fits at the control flies' response rate, the eight glomeruli at 1.25 at the 70 Hz per-claw threshold and the DC2, DM5 and DM1 core with Hallem's ms input at 1.5 at 40 Hz, led the reference model by only 0.1–0.2 of extended loss, within what a re-drawn wiring can move a fit. Re-fitted on the second within-type claw-permutation draw (the one the replication on re-drawn wiring and the reduced gain sets on a re-drawn wiring used), both orderings hold and widen: at 70 Hz the eight at 1.25 reach {{E46/comparison.csv:thr70_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_gain1.25_rate0.068_k2_cap3:loss ~ .2f}} against {{E46/comparison.csv:thr70_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:loss ~ .2f}} for the eight at 1.5, and at 40 Hz the core reaches {{E46/comparison.csv:thr40_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_input-hallem_shared_ms_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_core3_cap3:loss ~ .2f}} against {{E46/comparison.csv:thr40_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:loss ~ .2f}}. The draw again takes most of its toll on the block: the reference model keeps half of its block on the draw at 70 Hz and two thirds at 40 Hz (the latter from a second start with a lower somatic threshold and a stronger APL; from its own start it kept a third), the core two thirds, and the eight at 1.25 nearly all of it, by shifting to that lower-threshold, stronger-APL solution. Structure against wild-type somata and the natural-source alignment fall on the draw for every model, as in the replication on re-drawn wiring, while agreement with the two KC matrices does not. So the smaller gain at 70 Hz and the core at 40 Hz are not artefacts of the real wiring, and fitting at the data's response fraction left the reference model's dependence on the real wiring unchanged; what that dependence consists of is taken up [next](#wiring-feature).

### Which feature of the wiring the fitted block needs {#wiring-feature}

Every re-drawn wiring on this page is one kind of shuffle, each KC type's claws re-assigned among that type's boutons, and every such re-draw lost part of the block once the model was fitted with the structure targets. Which feature of the real wiring the block needs was asked with four kinds of shuffle of the bouton wiring: claw permutations, which keep each KC's and each glomerulus' number of claws, and Curveball shuffles, which keep each KC's number of distinct glomeruli and each glomerulus' number of KCs, each either across all KCs or within each KC type (so that each type keeps its glomerulus repertoire). The reference model at the control flies' response fraction (the eight glomeruli at 1.5, at both per-claw thresholds, and the eight at 1.25 at 70 Hz) was first run on five draws of each kind at its fitted APL and KC–KC scales, with only the somatic threshold re-tuned to the response fraction (it moved by at most 2 %), and then re-fitted on one draw of each kind.

At the fitted scales every kind of shuffle loses part of the block, and the draw-to-draw spread is as large as the differences between kinds, so no kind separates from the others: the within-type Curveball keeps the most for the eight at 1.5 and the claw permutations the least at 40 Hz, but the eight at 1.25 reverse that order. Agreement with the two KC matrices does not move; what the shuffles cost besides the block is the APL-on response-rate pattern and the natural-source alignment. Re-fitted, the block comes back, but by an amount set by the draw and not by the kind of shuffle. On the first draw the two shuffles that ignore KC type made the data's block in full, on a wiring whose every claw was re-assigned at random across all KCs, and the two within-type shuffles kept the least; the follow-up on four draws each of the plain claw permutation and the within-type Curveball (the fitted block on more shuffle draws) found the two kinds indistinguishable, their means in the opposite order and both between half and three quarters of the data's block, with single draws recovering anything from none of it to all of it, none above the real wiring's value, and the four draws with the lowest loss carrying the four largest blocks. So the fitted block does not need the hemibrain's pairing of glomeruli on KCs: a re-fit recovers it on a random wiring with these claw counts to a degree that varies from draw to draw, and the re-draws' loss of it above was the typical draw's loss (their within-type claw-permutation draws recovered what a typical draw of either kind recovers), not a sign that the block rides on the real wiring. What every shuffle loses, re-fitted or not, is the natural-source alignment with APL off, which on the real wiring sits at the data's value and on no shuffled wiring the model was re-fitted on comes close to it; APL's effect on it (on minus off) is at or below zero on the real wiring and on the within-type shuffles, where the data's is positive, and positive only on the plain claw permutation, the one statistic that told the two kinds apart. The structure against wild-type somata is kept by every shuffle for the eight at 1.5 and lost only for the eight at 1.25. The real wiring's own contribution to the fitted models is therefore the natural-source alignment, not the block; a block difference between two wirings means something only once several draws of each have been re-fitted.

Which part of the wiring carries the natural-source alignment was then asked directly (the glomerulus pairs behind the natural-source alignment). Counting, for each pair of the 54 glomeruli of the claw wiring, the KCs with claws from both glomeruli against 200 draws of each kind of shuffle finds two layers of structure beyond the margins. Fifty-five of the 1431 pairs are over-shared against every kind, and they are the food glomeruli converging on common KCs (DM1 with DM4, DM2, DM5 and DP1m, DM2 with DM4 and DM5, VM5d with VM5v and VA3; DM1 / DM4 on 92 KCs where the margins predict 50), the convergence the connectome literature reports. And 76 of the 162 glomerulus x KC-type counts are far from what a type-ignoring shuffle predicts: the food glomeruli are concentrated on the α/β KCs and depleted on the γ KCs (DM2 reaches 22 % of the α/β KCs and 8 % of the γ KCs where a type-ignoring shuffle gives 14 % of each), and a second set (DC3, VA5, VA6, VL2a, DA2, D, DL1) the other way round; this is the layer the within-type shuffles keep and the type-ignoring ones destroy. Turned into a score per odor pair (the shared-KC counts summed over glomerulus pairs, weighted by the two odors' ORN input), the type layer predicts which odor pairs the fitted real-wiring model brings closer than its shuffles do (ρ {{E52/score_correlations.csv:weighting=norm;kind=typestruct;null_class=claw_permutation;target=model_contrib_off_thr40_gain1.5:rho ~ +.2f}} with the reference model's APL-off residual minus the shuffle mean), which pairs the control flies bring closer than the ORN input predicts ({{E52/score_correlations.csv:weighting=norm;kind=typestruct;null_class=claw_permutation;target=resid_ctrl:rho ~ +.2f}}) and, less strongly, the natural-source-close pairs ({{E52/score_correlations.csv:weighting=norm;kind=typestruct;null_class=claw_permutation;target=vcf_hyp:rho ~ +.2f}}); the over-shared pairs' excess beyond type predicts chemical distance instead ({{E52/score_correlations.csv:weighting=norm;kind=excess;null_class=curveball_within_type;target=chem:rho ~ +.2f}}) and none of those ({{E52/score_correlations.csv:weighting=norm;kind=excess;null_class=curveball_within_type;target=vcf_hyp:rho ~ +.2f}} with the natural-source distance). Lesioning only the 55 pairs' excess, by claw swaps that keep every KC's and every bouton's claw count (a tenth of the claws moved), takes the reference model's alignment with APL off at its fitted scales from {{E47/comparison.csv:A_thr40_gain1.5_real_retuned:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ .2f}} to {{E52/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=lesion_targeted:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}}, where the within-type shuffles sit, while a control lesion of the same size on count-matched pairs leaves it at {{E52/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=lesion_control:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}}; the block does not move. So the alignment the models owe to the real wiring has two carriers: the KC-type organization of the glomerular input, the larger and the one organized along the natural-source axis, and the within-type convergence of the food glomeruli, the smaller and organized by chemistry. A within-type shuffle is the right null for the first and the targeted lesion for the second; the type-ignoring shuffles lose both, which is why a model re-fitted on one loses more of the alignment than any pairwise feature accounts for.
