The pairwise carrier was then tested against the gain (the gain and the wiring behind the natural-source alignment), since four of the eight gained glomeruli sit in the over-shared pairs. It works only through the gain. With the eight glomeruli's gain set to 1 at the fitted scales the reference model's alignment with APL off falls from {{E47/comparison.csv:A_thr40_gain1.5_real_retuned:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ .2f}} to {{E54/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=nogain:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} (the fit made without gain, which re-tunes every scale, reaches {{E54/comparison.csv:model=thr40_nogain;shuffle=real:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ .2f}}), and under the fits made without gain the lesion of the 55 over-shared pairs changes nothing beyond draw noise. With the gain, neither half of the over-shared pairs with ORN data moves the alignment on its own: the 15 pairs that involve a gained glomerulus leave it at {{E54/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=lesion_gained:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} (their matched control pairs {{E54/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=control_gained:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}}), the 17 that do not at {{E54/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=lesion_ungained:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} ({{E54/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=control_ungained:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}}), and the 23 over-shared pairs among glomeruli the model does not drive at {{E54/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=lesion_nonorn:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} ({{E54/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=control_nonorn:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}}); the 32 pairs with ORN data lesioned together take it to {{E54/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=lesion_orn_all:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} against their controls' {{E54/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=control_orn_all:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}}, where the lesion of all 55 had put it. So the pairwise carrier is the food-glomerulus convergence as a whole, not the sum of its parts, and it carries the alignment only when the type-biased glomeruli within it are gained; the half that involves gained glomeruli is what costs the per-odor response-rate pattern with APL on ({{E54/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=lesion_gained:spearman_rate_on_vs_data__mean ~ .2f}} against {{E54/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=control_gained:spearman_rate_on_vs_data__mean ~ .2f}} for its controls). A re-fit of the reference model on a lesioned wiring buys the block back ({{E54/comparison.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=refit_lesion_targeted-seed0:spike_counts_cosine__block_fruity_small_iso ~ +.3f}} against {{E47/comparison.csv:A_thr40_gain1.5_real_retuned:spike_counts_cosine__block_fruity_small_iso ~ +.3f}} on the real wiring) and leaves the alignment at {{E54/comparison.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=refit_lesion_targeted-seed0:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ .2f}}, with the loss at {{E54/comparison.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=refit_lesion_targeted-seed0:loss_rate_ctrl ~ .2f}} against {{E47/comparison.csv:A_thr40_gain1.5_real_retuned:loss_rate_ctrl ~ .2f}}: as on every shuffled wiring, the alignment is the statistic a re-fit cannot buy back.

Whether the two carriers are sufficient was then asked by putting the convergence back (building the natural-source alignment back). On within-type Curveball draws (the type structure kept, the convergence destroyed) and on plain Curveball draws (both destroyed), the 32 over-shared pairs with ORN data were raised to the real wiring's shared-KC counts by claw swaps that keep every KC's and every bouton's claw count (10 to 12 % of the claws moved), with the same raises on count-matched control pairs as the control, and the reference models were run at their fitted scales with the threshold re-tuned. The answer splits by APL state. With APL on the convergence brings the alignment back: on the within-type draws the reference model goes from {{E47/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} untreated to {{E57/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type_transplant:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} (the real wiring {{E47/comparison.csv:A_thr40_gain1.5_real_retuned:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ .2f}}; the control raises {{E57/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type_control:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}}), a rise of {{E57/paired_differences.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type;kind=transplant;stat=spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp:mean_diff ~ +.2f}} per draw that is positive on every draw and for all three models, to within a few hundredths of the real wiring; on the plain draws it gets about halfway ({{E57/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_transplant:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} from {{E47/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}}). With APL off it does not move: {{E57/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type_transplant:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} after the transplant against {{E47/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} untreated and the real wiring's {{E47/comparison.csv:A_thr40_gain1.5_real_retuned:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ .2f}}, on either class of draw, although the lesion of exactly these counts on the real wiring had cost it ({{E54/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=lesion_orn_all:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} against {{E54/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=control_orn_all:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} for the control lesions). So APL on minus off, which the KC data have at {{data:vcf_apl_effect ~ +.2f}} and the real-wiring models about level, moves on the within-type draws from {{E47/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type:vcf_on_minus_off__mean ~ +.2f}} to {{E57/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type_transplant:vcf_on_minus_off__mean ~ +.2f}}. The convergence as a pairwise count is what carries the APL-on side of the alignment, and with the type structure under it it is close to sufficient for that side; the APL-off side that the lesions lose is the convergence in its real-wiring arrangement, which KCs hold the pairs and what else those KCs sample, which no pairwise transplant restores. The structure against the wild-type somata rises with the transplant above the real wiring's value ({{E57/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type_transplant:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_remap_wt__mean ~ .2f}} against {{E47/comparison.csv:A_thr40_gain1.5_real_retuned:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_remap_wt ~ .2f}}), the block is not restored ({{E57/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type_transplant:spike_counts_cosine__block_fruity_small_iso__mean ~ +.3f}} against {{E47/comparison.csv:A_thr40_gain1.5_real_retuned:spike_counts_cosine__block_fruity_small_iso ~ +.3f}}), and the response rate ρ with APL on rises under the control raises ({{E57/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type_control:spearman_rate_on_vs_data__mean ~ .2f}}) nearly as much as under the transplant ({{E57/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type_transplant:spearman_rate_on_vs_data__mean ~ .2f}}), so that statistic answers to the swaps rather than to the pairs.

Whether the whole pairwise matrix suffices was then asked (the co-input-preserving shuffle). A simulated-annealing shuffle of same-type claw swaps brought every one of the 1431 glomerulus pairs' shared-KC counts, on within-type and on plain Curveball draws, to within one KC of the real wiring's, with 97 % of the claws still differing from the real wiring and the three-way co-input among the over-shared glomeruli only partly reproduced. On the type-preserving draws the match buys no APL-off alignment: the reference model's {{E59/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type_matched:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} against {{E47/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} untreated and the real wiring's {{E47/comparison.csv:A_thr40_gain1.5_real_retuned:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ .2f}}, as the 32-pair transplant bought none; on the plain draws it lifts it to {{E59/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_matched:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} from {{E47/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}}, the untreated within-type level, so the pairwise matrix and the type layer are substitutes for one another and together reach no more than either. The APL-on side comes back on both backgrounds ({{E59/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_within_type_matched:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} and {{E59/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_matched:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp__mean ~ .2f}} against the real wiring's {{E47/comparison.csv:A_thr40_gain1.5_real_retuned:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ .2f}}), as it did with the transplant, and the block is restored by nothing ({{E59/per_class.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=curveball_matched:spike_counts_cosine__block_fruity_small_iso__mean ~ +.3f}} on the matched plain draws). A re-fit of the reference model on a matched within-type wiring recovers everything the structure loss scores, the loss itself ({{E59/comparison.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=refit_curveball_within_type_matched-seed1:loss_rate_ctrl ~ .2f}} against {{E47/comparison.csv:A_thr40_gain1.5_real_retuned:loss_rate_ctrl ~ .2f}} on the real wiring), the block ({{E59/comparison.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=refit_curveball_within_type_matched-seed1:spike_counts_cosine__block_fruity_small_iso ~ +.3f}}) and the APL-on alignment ({{E59/comparison.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=refit_curveball_within_type_matched-seed1:spike_counts_cosine__on_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ .2f}}), by doubling the KC-KC scale, and not the APL-off alignment ({{E59/comparison.csv:model=thr40_gain1.5;shuffle=refit_curveball_within_type_matched-seed1:spike_counts_cosine__off_spearman_resid_vs_vcf_hyp ~ .2f}}): on every shuffled, lesioned or matched wiring so far it is the statistic a re-fit cannot buy back, and its carrier is finer than the pairwise matrix (the three-way co-input, or which KC holds which combination of glomeruli). None of these wirings moves ρ on odor-odor distances with either KC matrix beyond the draw-to-draw spread.

### The per-claw threshold at low concentration {#low-concentration}

Every constraint above comes from the APL16 panel at 10^-3 to 10^-2, where the per-claw threshold that every good model carries costs nothing. Two further KC datasets of Yang's have weak input by construction: in control flies 2h and ma at 10^-7 recruit 5–6 % of KCs and farn at 10^-3 4 %, and with APL output blocked two to three times as many. Run at their fitted parameters on those odors (ORN input within one log unit of the recorded concentration, the stronger one where the exact one is missing, so a failure to recruit is the conservative result), the fitted models recruit a half to a tenth of the KCs the flies recruit and almost none for ma, whose ORN input is exact; the matched trial noise roughly doubles the fractions without closing the gap, and with APL off the models are as far short of the flies with APL output blocked, so the deficit is in what reaches the KCs through the per-claw threshold, not in APL (whose share, the APL-off to APL-on ratio, is about the flies' for 2h and farn). The model with the lowest somatic threshold, the eight glomeruli at 1.25 at 70 Hz, comes closest. The flies' binary mixtures recruit fewer KCs than the union of their components, mildly in control flies and strongly with APL output blocked (ma + farn fewer than either component); with the components' ORN input combined as their maximum per glomerulus the models show the mild sub-additivity and not the strong one. So the per-claw threshold, a hard rectifier at 40–70 Hz of PN input, is falsified at low concentration: a sub-threshold slope or noise through the threshold has to carry these responses, and the fitted models describe KC recruitment at 10^-3 to 10^-2 only. A fit without a per-claw threshold has since been run on these odors: it recruits KCs for ma and for 2h at 10^-5, as many as the flies for 2h through the matched trial noise and about half as many for ma ([the APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings](#wiring-population)).

| distance matrices: APL on, APL off and APL on minus APL off (the KC data above the model) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/data.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E13_bouton_coincidence__bouton_base.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E16_claw_saturation__bouton_thr70.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E8_kc_kc_inhibition__breadth3_tau0.05_kckc100.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E19_thr_breadth_kckc__thr70_k2_kckc100.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E10_joint_fit__k2.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E27_glomerulus_gain__oracle_g1.5.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E25_joint_fit_structure__thr70_k2_ext.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E28_gain_combined_jointfit__thr40_gain1.5_k2.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E34_gain_bounded__thr40_gain1.5_k2_cap3.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E44_operating_point__thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E36_spike_apl__spk_thr70_k2_s2.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E67_apl_effect_fits__rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E69_kc_firing_ceiling__rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2.png) | !button[matrices](results/apl_effect/matrices/E39_gain_decomposition__thr70__only_DM1.png) |

Two columns hold the same model at two operating points: the reference model until 2026-11-08, fitted with 5 % of KCs responding with APL on (the convention of the lab's earlier modelling), and the current reference model, its re-fit at the {{data:ctrl_frac ~ .1%}} of KCs that respond in the control flies, from which the later experiments start ([the operating point](#operating-point)). The third column from the end is one of [the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits), the fits that put the APL-effect loss first in the loss: the first joint fit's family (breadth exponent 2, no per-claw threshold, no glomerulus gain) fitted at the flies' response rate with the calibrated readout in the loss, and scored here on raw spike counts like every other column. The column after it is the same fit made again with [a firing ceiling for the KCs](#firing-ceiling), a refractory period and an adaptation that bound how many spikes a KC fires to one odor, at the setting whose largest spike counts agree with whole-cell recordings of KCs and with both definitions of the model's side in the loss.

On the main target no model reaches the data, and only the fit with a firing ceiling comes near it, at about one and a half times the floor. Without a ceiling the lowest APL-effect loss in the table is more than twice the floor, and most columns sit at the bouton baseline's level: the per-claw threshold, the oracle gain, the structure targets, the interpretable bounds and KC-spike-driven APL were built for other statistics and left this one where it was, the reference model included (what its larger mean effect gains over the bouton baseline, its pattern loses). Only the first joint fit, the two APL-effect fits and the last column do clearly better with their response rates inside the three bands on them, and the fourth band, on the number of odors each KC responds to, puts the first joint fit and the last column outside and leaves the two APL-effect fits inside (next paragraph); the 70 Hz joint fit with the structure targets does worst. In every column APL moves the pairs apart less than it does in the flies: outside the last two columns the mean effect is below the lower end of the data's 90 % interval ({{data:apl_effect_mean.lo ~ +.3f}}), two thirds of the data's in the APL-effect fit without a firing ceiling and at most a little over half in the others. In all but four columns (the current reference model, the two APL-effect fits and the last) this shortfall, not the pattern, is the larger part of the loss. For the first joint fit, the APL-effect fit of its family and the 40 Hz fits with the oracle gain, most of it belongs to the comparison of raw spike counts with a calcium signal and not to the models: compressed as the indicator compresses them, their counts give a mean effect inside the flies' interval, while the others, the last two columns apart, stay below it ([the main target under a compressive readout](#readout), at the end of this section); with the firing ceiling the raw counts themselves give it ([the firing ceiling for the KCs](#firing-ceiling)). That agreement is with the recordings as measured, and the models reach it with their responders alone, where a third of the flies' mean effect comes from products with values below the response criterion (same subsection). They also reach it with more broadly responding KCs with APL off than the flies have (the row on KCs responding to eight or more odors), and in the three columns checked, the current reference model, the APL-effect fit and its re-fit with the firing ceiling, these are the many-claw KCs: about a third of the responses that removing APL adds go to the {{E72/release.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3:frac_kcs_9+ ~ .0%}} of KCs with nine or more claws. A threshold scaled to each KC's input moves the release off those cells at a cost on the main target, and a fit then makes as many broad responders from other cells ([the input-scaled KC threshold](#input-scaled-threshold), at the end of this section). The flies' own effect rests on broadly responding KCs too, of two kinds: split by the number of odors each KC responds to, most of it sits in the KCs that respond to eight or more odors when APL output is blocked and most of the rest in those that respond to four to seven, and the part below the response criterion is for the most part those KCs' weaker responses to their other odors; the models have the first kind alone ([the APL effect split by odors per KC](#apl-effect-by-class), at the end of this section). The columns with the larger mean effects all carry breadth-weighted APL (in the breadth-weighted APL experiment the mean effect grew with the breadth exponent at unchanged response rates), though not every column that carries it has one. The pattern is no closer. ρ on the per-pair APL effect stays well under its ceiling in every column; the per-claw threshold and breadth-weighted APL each lift it from the bouton baseline's, and the two 40 Hz fits with the gain on all eight glomeruli, the reference model among them, have the lowest values. Nor has a positive ρ lowered any loss yet: with the means removed, no column's pattern part is clearly below the data's own standard deviation across pairs ({{data:apl_effect_sd ~ .3f}}), which is what a model with the same effect on every pair would score, and in the columns with breadth-weighted APL and no firing ceiling it is well above it ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E34_gain_bounded;tag=thr40_gain1.5_k2_cap3:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse_centered ~ .3f}} for the reference model; {{E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse_centered ~ .3f}} with the ceiling). There the effect is spread over the pairs more unevenly than in the flies, and in every column without the ceiling some pairs move closer with APL on, which no pair does in the flies; which pairs, through which KCs, and what the breadth weighting has to do with it is taken up [at the end of this section](#apl-effect-parts). Two columns are outside the band on the response-rate ratio, both with KC–KC inhibition at 100: the inhibition that remains without APL keeps the response rate from doubling when APL is removed, as it does in the flies (the row of response-rate ratios further down), so the low loss of the breadth 3 column does not count.

The fourth band, on the number of odors each KC responds to, leaves four columns inside, and only two of them, the two APL-effect fits, are ahead on the main target. Ten of the fourteen model columns are outside it, nine with APL off and four with APL on (the realism row; the three rows under it give every column's distance from the flies' distribution and the two ends of that distribution). With APL off the models' responses sit in too few KCs: in every column about half of the KCs or more respond to none of the 16 odors, against a third in the flies with APL output blocked (TNT), and the KCs that respond to between one and seven odors are too few to match; in the columns with the glomerulus gain at 40 Hz, in the two APL-effect fits and in the last column, more KCs than in the flies respond to eight or more. Part of this is the operating point. Eleven columns are tuned to 5 % of KCs responding with APL on, which leaves fewer responses to spread than the flies have and more silent KCs in both conditions, and this band, unlike the band on the ratio, is on the distribution itself and makes no allowance for it. The two reference-model columns are the same fit at the two operating points: the re-fit at the flies' response rate takes the distance from the flies' distribution from {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E34_gain_bounded;tag=thr40_gain1.5_k2_cap3:opk_on_tv ~ .2f}} to {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:opk_on_tv ~ .2f}} with APL on, well inside the band, and from {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E34_gain_bounded;tag=thr40_gain1.5_k2_cap3:opk_off_tv ~ .2f}} to {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:opk_off_tv ~ .2f}} with APL off, just outside it. The rest is the model. At the flies' overall response rates the current reference model still leaves about half of its KCs silent with APL off and has nearly twice the flies' share responding to eight or more odors: removing APL brings in about half as many new KCs as it does in the flies and makes more of the responding ones broad. Three of the five columns inside the band with APL off have the per-claw threshold at 70 Hz and no glomerulus gain (the per-claw threshold alone, the joint fit with the structure targets, and that fit with KC-spike-driven APL), although they are tuned to 5 %; the oracle gain and KC–KC inhibition at 100 each take a 70 Hz model out. Only the last two of the three are inside all four bands, and one of them has the highest loss in the table and the other is at the bouton baseline's level. The other two are the APL-effect fits, with neither a per-claw threshold nor a gain and tuned to the flies' response rate, both inside all four bands. Without a firing ceiling the fit has the lowest loss in the table but the last column's and that of its own re-fit with the ceiling, and the lowest of the columns without a ceiling once the two highest-firing KCs are left out (the row under the loss, which was not computed for the column with the ceiling): the first progress on the main target that the table shows inside the realism bands ([the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits)). With the ceiling it has the lowest loss in the table, on raw spike counts nearly what it has under the calibrated readout, with less than half the block of the fit without it and no change in which KCs respond with APL off ([the firing ceiling for the KCs](#firing-ceiling)). Most of the saved models are outside a band as well: {{lit:563 of the 782 # counted in results/apl_effect/by_variant.csv as written 2026-11-09 23:56: rows with a non-empty realism_flag, of all rows}} scored are outside at least one band, {{lit:362 # counted in results/apl_effect/by_variant.csv as written 2026-11-09 23:56: rows whose realism_flag names only odors per KC}} of them on this band alone.

The last column holds the lowest APL-effect loss in the table among the models saved before the main target was fitted directly, and held the lowest of any model on the real wiring until then ([the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits)). It was chosen as the lowest inside the three bands on the response rates, which it meets, and the fourth band, on the number of odors each KC responds to, puts it outside. It is the 70 Hz fit of the reference model's family with the oracle gain kept on DM1 alone, every fitted scale held and the somatic threshold re-tuned to the fit's response rate, one of the variants of [the oracle gain decomposed by glomerulus](#gain-decomposition). Among the columns without a firing ceiling its mean effect is the only one inside the data's 90 % interval, at the reference model's response rates, so the larger effect is not bought with the overall response rates, and its ρ on the per-pair APL effect is the highest. Its loss is then almost all pattern (a pattern part of {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr70__only_DM1:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse_centered ~ .3f}} in a loss of {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr70__only_DM1:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}), and that part is within the range of the other columns with breadth-weighted APL, not below it. What the larger effect does come with is a response with APL off that sits in fewer KCs. The same fit with the gain removed everywhere is inside the band on odors per KC with APL off ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr70__none:opk_off_tv ~ .2f}} from the flies' distribution, against a band of {{data:tnt_odors_per_kc_tv_max ~ .2f}}), at a loss of {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr70__none:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}; the gain on DM1 lowers the loss and takes the distance to {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr70__only_DM1:opk_off_tv ~ .2f}}, with more KCs silent and more responding to eight or more odors. In the 70 Hz family and in the 40 Hz family alike, the variant with the gain on DM1 alone has both the lowest loss of the decomposition and the largest distance from the flies' distribution with APL off. A loss counts only inside the bands, so as it stands the column is not progress on the main target. The rows further down the column show what else it gives up: the lowest ρ on odor-odor distances of any column, with APL on and with APL off, a block under half the data's and a negative natural-source alignment with APL off. Its matrices (the button in the bottom row opens each column's APL-on and APL-off matrices and their difference, under the KC data's) show how a low loss and poor matrices go together: 2-but, paa and B-myr nearly coincide in both conditions, as [in the reference model](#gap-pairs), and a pair that is equally wrong with APL on and with APL off costs this loss nothing. The column is also a selection after the fact, the best of the decomposition's variants on this statistic, with a gain set by hand. The same variant of the 40 Hz fit is the lowest of its family too ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr40__only_DM1:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} against the reference model's {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E34_gain_bounded;tag=thr40_gain1.5_k2_cap3:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}), so DM1's part is not an accident of the selection, but the column is a lead for a fit, not a result: a lead on raw spike counts only (under [the compressive readout](#readout) it is the one column whose loss rises), and one whose fit would have to hold the band on odors per KC that the column itself fails. It also rests on two of the model's {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr70__only_DM1:n_kcs ~ .0f}} KCs: with the two that fire most with APL on left out of both conditions after the fact, nothing simulated again, its loss is {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr70__only_DM1:raw__top2__rmse ~ .3f}} (the row under the loss), above the first joint fit's, which the same leave-out leaves at {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E10_joint_fit;tag=k2:raw__top2__rmse ~ .3f}}. In every other column the loss stays where it was or falls with the two KCs left out, most in the three 40 Hz fits with the oracle gain, whose mean effect a few such KCs hold down ([the high-firing KCs that survive APL](#high-firing-kcs), at the end of this section). The fit that the column was a lead for has since been run, among [the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits): with the same per-claw threshold and the gain on DM1 alone, fitted on raw counts at the flies' response rate, the model reaches {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} inside all four bands, the lowest loss on raw counts of those fits, by giving up the block; it rests on a few KCs as the column does, and it loses that lead once the flies' trial-to-trial noise is put into the model. Among the models on the real wiring saved before those fits that are scored on all four bands and inside them, the lowest loss is {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr70__loo_DC2:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}, that of the same 70 Hz fit with the gain removed from DC2 alone, no lower than the first joint fit's ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E10_joint_fit;tag=k2:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}, itself outside the fourth band with APL off); without any glomerulus gain it is that of the fit with the gain removed everywhere, above; the APL-effect fit of the third column from the end is below both, inside all four bands and without a gain ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}). Breadth-weighted APL alone on the bouton baseline has a lower loss ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E12_breadth_apl;tag=spikes_k2:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}) and is outside the band with APL off, as the bouton baseline is: the weighting leaves the model with APL off as it was. A few single draws of shuffled wirings have losses inside all four bands as low as the lowest of the APL-effect fits (the lowest {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E47_wiring_features;tag=A_thr40_gain1.5_claw_permutation-seed5_retuned:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}), below the last column's, with a larger mean effect than the real wiring gives at the same parameters ([about half of that difference is a few high-firing KCs of the real wiring](#high-firing-kcs)); they are controls, not candidate models.

The two mechanisms fix complementary things: the per-claw threshold sets the decorrelation level and the APL-off structure; breadth-weighted APL with KC–KC inhibition sets the block and the control-matrix ρ. The threshold × breadth × KC–KC grid combined them and they add: its block-first point is the first model with the data's decorrelation level, control excess profile and block together, at the cost of the response-rate ratio, the per-odor response rates and monotonicity; its balanced point (threshold 40, breadth 2, KC–KC 30) keeps the per-odor response rates and monotonicity with a smaller block. The joint fit then showed the response-rate ratio is recoverable by rebalancing APL between claws and boutons, but, so far, only without the per-claw threshold.

No model yet has the APL output blocked (TNT) matrix right (ρ on odor-odor distances at most 0.72 against a ceiling of 0.97; once the threshold is on, its non-ester pairs share too little), and these, with the per-odor response-rate pattern, are the remaining gaps. They point at the input side (the five weak-input odors analysed above) and at a stronger or differently distributed APL rather than at more KC mechanisms of the kind tested so far.

### The main target under a compressive readout of the model's spike counts {#readout}

**Compressed as the calcium indicator compresses them, the best models' spike counts give the flies' mean effect.** A model's distances are cosines on raw spike counts, the flies' on a nuclear calcium signal that compresses large responses. A few high-firing KCs dominate the model's cosines with APL on and off alike (the 1 % of KCs that respond most to an odor hold {{E65/model_amplitude.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:top1_share_on ~ .0%}} of its summed squared responses in the current reference model, {{E65/data_amplitude_summary.csv:genotype=TNT3c:top1_share_all ~ .0%}} in the control flies) and hide what removing APL recruits. The compressive readout of the model's spike counts re-scored every saved model through a saturating function, n / (n + k), with k set on its APL-on counts alone so that an odor's response is spread over the responding KCs as in the control flies, and kept for APL off.

The current reference model and the first joint fit, each on its raw spike counts and on the same counts through [the calibrated saturating readout](#fn-calibrated-readout) (half-saturation k of {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=saturating_cal_pr:param ~ .1f}} and {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E10_joint_fit;tag=k2;readout=saturating_cal_pr:param ~ .1f}} spikes); the readout changes no response rate

Both models' mean effects move from below the flies' 90 % interval to inside it and their losses nearly halve. What remains is pattern ([taken apart by odor and by pair in the next subsection](#apl-effect-parts)): ρ on the per-pair APL effect stays far from its ceiling and neither pattern part is below the score of an effect with no pattern. Other calibrated readouts, a power law among them, change little (a loss between {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=power_cal_pr:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} and {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=saturating_cal_pr_all:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} for the reference model). Every column of the best-models table improves except the last, whose loss rises ({{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr70__only_DM1;readout=raw:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} to {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr70__only_DM1;readout=saturating_cal_pr:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}) as its pattern worsens, and the first joint fit is the lowest of the {{E65/readout_summary.csv:readout=saturating_cal_pr:n_realistic ~ .0f}} models saved before the APL-effect fits and inside the three bands on the response rates (its family, fitted to the main target, goes lower still: [the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits)). The fourth band, on the number of odors each KC responds to, is not applied in this subsection or the next, whose counts and medians are over models inside those three. No readout would change a model's standing on it: the readout alters how much each responding KC weighs, not which KCs respond, and both models of the table are outside it with APL off ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E10_joint_fit;tag=k2:opk_off_tv ~ .2f}} from the flies' distribution for the first joint fit and {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:opk_off_tv ~ .2f}} for the current reference model, against a band of {{data:tnt_odors_per_kc_tv_max ~ .2f}}). The readout supplies these models' mean effect; it does not give them the flies' spread of responses over the KCs with APL off. Over those models the median mean effect rises only from {{E65/readout_summary.csv:readout=raw:median_mean_effect ~ +.3f}} to {{E65/readout_summary.csv:readout=saturating_cal_pr:median_mean_effect ~ +.3f}}: the lift grows with the APL-off / APL-on response-rate ratio, which the readout leaves alone (figure).

![Each saved model inside the three bands on the response rates (the band on odors per KC is not applied): its mean APL effect against its APL-off / APL-on response-rate ratio, on raw spike counts (grey) and through the calibrated saturating readout (blue; calibrated to the control flies' participation ratio, the effective share of the responding KCs that carries an odor's response). Horizontal lines: the flies' mean effect as measured, with sub-threshold responses set to 0 and corrected for trial-to-trial noise; vertical line: the flies' response-rate ratio.](results/E65_spike_count_readout/E65_mean_effect_vs_rate_ratio.png)

**The cost is the gain models' block.** Under the readout the block of both models, and of nearly every model built on the glomerulus gain, all but disappears, while the flies' block survives binarizing the recordings ({{lit:+0.047 # the block of the two KC matrices computed from binarized responses (trial mean > 1): kc_remapping_harness.compare_on_off on yang_mean_rdm(binarize=1.0) of the control flies and of APL output blocked (TNT), 0.0474; the KC data's binarized column of the cost table in E65_spike_count_readout.md}} against {{data:block ~ +.3f}} as measured): these models make the block with the amplitude of a few hard-driven KCs ([identified at the end of this section](#high-firing-kcs)), the flies with which KCs respond. The blocks that survive are mostly those of mechanisms acting on the response set (the breadth × shared-input APL rule, panel-general glomerulus scores, the oracle co-responder suppression), whose losses stay above these two models'. The APL-off distances and the natural-source alignment fall too.

**Below the response criterion the recordings are not empty, and what is there is mostly signal.** In the recordings a KC counts as responding to an odor when its mean over the three trials is above 1 in the recordings' z-scored units (1 z); everything at or below that is sub-threshold. A model KC without a spike reads exactly 0, on raw counts and through the readout alike, while in the control flies {{E65/data_amplitude_summary.csv:genotype=TNT3c:share_subthreshold ~ .0%}} of an odor's squared response vector lies in sub-threshold trial means, {{E65/data_amplitude_summary.csv:genotype=TNT3c:share_negative ~ .0%}} in negative ones. The low end of the readout asked what those values are, whether a model's scores change when its readout is given the same low end, and whether the model holds such values under its spike threshold ([how each step was done](#fn-readout-low-end)). They are mostly signal, not measurement noise. Entries picked by their mean over two trials come back on the third: the weakly positive ones (0.3 to 1 z) at {{E68/data_cv_by_genotype.csv:genotype=TNT3c;band=(0.3, 1]:reproducible_share ~ .0%}} of that mean in the control flies and {{E68/data_cv_by_genotype.csv:genotype=TNT;band=(0.3, 1]:reproducible_share ~ .0%}} with APL output blocked (TNT), the negative ones (at or below −0.3 z) at {{E68/data_cv_by_genotype.csv:genotype=TNT3c;band=(-inf, -0.3]:reproducible_share ~ .0%}} and {{E68/data_cv_by_genotype.csv:genotype=TNT;band=(-inf, -0.3]:reproducible_share ~ .0%}}. Blocking APL output nearly doubles the share of weak values, while the share of strong ones barely rises and the responders' mean amplitude does not change (the KC data column of the table of amplitudes further down). The negative values are more frequent for the odors with more responders, in the control flies and with APL output blocked (TNT) alike (a Spearman correlation across odors of {{E68/data_neg_by_genotype.csv:genotype=TNT3c:rho_within_recording_mean ~ .2f}} and {{E68/data_neg_by_genotype.csv:genotype=TNT:rho_within_recording_mean ~ .2f}} within a recording), so they are not a mark of APL's output. A pair's cosine similarity is a sum over KCs, and each KC's part belongs to one of three terms: both of its entries responders, one a responder and the other sub-threshold, or both sub-threshold. Of the flies' mean APL effect of {{data:apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}}, {{E68/data_effect_terms.csv:both__effect_mean:observed ~ +.3f}} is in the first term, {{E68/data_effect_terms.csv:one__effect_mean:observed ~ +.3f}} in the second (90 % interval {{E68/data_effect_terms.csv:one__effect_mean:lo ~ +.3f}} to {{E68/data_effect_terms.csv:one__effect_mean:hi ~ +.3f}}) and {{E68/data_effect_terms.csv:neither__effect_mean:observed ~ +.3f}} in the third; of the effect's pattern across the 120 pairs, {{E68/data_effect_terms.csv:both__pattern_share:observed ~ .0%}} is in the first. The sub-threshold values thus carry about a third of how far APL moves odors apart in the flies, and a tenth of which pairs it moves most.

**Giving the model's readout that low end leaves its scores where they were.** Two additions to the calibrated readout were tried on every saved model. One is measurement noise: the control flies' trial-to-trial standard deviation of non-responding entries ({{data:ctrl_trial_noise_sd ~ .2f}} z per trial) added to the readout's values, in simulated recordings of three trials. The other is a one-spike floor, an offset b in (n + b) / (n + b + k) for every KC with at least one spike, so that the weight of a single spike is calibrated instead of following from k. The half-saturation k, the offset and the conversion of the readout's values to z are set together, on the model's APL-on counts through the noise, on three values of the control flies: the response rate under the recordings' criterion, the share of entries between 0.5 and 1.5 z and the participation ratio among responders. Each reading of the recordings is scored against the same reading of the model.

The additions move the reference model's loss by less than 0.01 under every reading but one, and under the target as defined they do not lower it: the noise lifts the model's mean effect a little further above the flies', the one-spike floor calibrates to a small offset ({{E68/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;config=floor;reading=graded:floor ~ .2f}} spikes) and adds nothing, and the pattern part is where it was ({{E68/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;config=cal;reading=graded:rmse_centered ~ .3f}} as defined, {{E68/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;config=floor_noise;reading=graded:rmse_centered ~ .3f}} with both). The exception is the reading with negative values set to 0, where cutting away the negative half of the added noise brings the model's mean effect down to the flies'; that is the clipping, not the model. The first joint fit moves as little under the target as defined (a loss of {{E68/key_models.csv:folder=E10_joint_fit;tag=k2;config=cal;reading=graded:rmse ~ .3f}} as defined and {{E68/key_models.csv:folder=E10_joint_fit;tag=k2;config=floor_noise;reading=graded:rmse ~ .3f}} with both). Over the {{E68/medians.csv:config=floor_noise;reading=graded:n ~ .0f}} saved models inside the three bands on the response rates in which the recordings' response rate can be reached, the median loss goes from {{E68/medians.csv:config=cal;reading=graded:loss ~ .3f}} to {{E68/medians.csv:config=floor_noise;reading=graded:loss ~ .3f}} and the order of the models by loss is largely kept (a Spearman correlation of {{E68/medians.csv:config=floor_noise;reading=graded:order_rho_vs_cal ~ .2f}} between the two orders). The readout therefore stays as defined, with no measurement noise and no one-spike floor.

**With APL off the models add strong responders where the flies add weak ones.** In the flies the products of a responder with a sub-threshold entry grow when APL output is blocked; in the reference model they do not (last row of the table below), and the membrane potential was the place to look for the missing values. Six models, the reference model and the first joint fit among them, were simulated again at their fitted parameters with every KC's membrane potential saved (the spike counts are those of the saved runs), and the readout was given two more terms, calibrated on the control flies with APL on as the rest is: for a KC without a spike, a weight times its mean depolarization during the odor; for every non-responding KC, a weight times its mean hyperpolarization, subtracted. The weights are set so that the model with APL on has the control flies' shares of weakly positive entries (0.25 to 1 z) and of negative ones (below −0.25 z).

The amplitudes of the trial means in z, with APL on / APL off: the KC data (control flies / APL output blocked (TNT)) and the current reference model read through the calibrated readout with the one-spike floor and measurement noise, without and with the two membrane-potential terms. With APL on, the model's response rate, its share between 0.5 and 1.5 z and, with the potential terms, its share below −0.25 z are set by the calibration; every APL-off value is a prediction

With APL on, the reference model read through the noise is close to the flies' amplitudes above 0.5 z (figure below). With APL off it is not. What blocking APL output adds in the flies is weak: the share between 0.5 and 1.5 z nearly doubles, and the share above 4 z and the responders' mean amplitude hardly move. What removing APL adds in the model is strong: about five times the flies' share above 4 z, and a mean amplitude more than a third higher than with APL on. The potential terms do not supply the weak values. The depolarization term calibrates to almost nothing in four of the six models, the reference model among them: the measurement noise alone already puts more of the model's APL-on entries between 0.25 and 1 z ({{E68/partB_amplitude.csv:model=ref40;config=floor_noise:share_025_1_on ~ .1%}}) than the control flies have ({{data:ctrl_share_z_025_1 ~ .1%}}), which leaves the term nothing to supply. The hyperpolarization term gives the model the flies' negative values with APL on and, with APL off, more of them where the flies have fewer: in five of the six models removing APL makes more KCs fire, and their KC–KC inhibition hyperpolarizes the rest. The cosine term that grows in the flies turns negative (last row), and the terms add strongly negative values that the flies do not have. Where a loss falls with the potential terms it falls with the mean effect, and in every model the same terms do as well or better with the potential shuffled across the non-spiking KCs or with the APL-on potential used for both conditions, so nothing in the potential's own pattern helps. The missing low end is therefore not hidden under the model's spike threshold. It is missing from what the model recruits when APL is removed, in all six models: strong responses where the flies have weak ones. What the weak values carry of the flies' APL effect sits for the most part in broadly responding KCs, as their weaker responses to their other odors, not in cells that respond weakly throughout ([the APL effect split by odors per KC](#apl-effect-by-class), at the end of this section). The realism bands hold a model to which KCs respond, not to how strongly, and do not see this; it is taken up with the pending work at the end of the page.

??? The amplitude histograms of the recordings and of two models, with APL on and with APL off

Left: APL on against the control flies. Right: APL off against APL output blocked (TNT). With APL on the models are close to the flies above 0.5 z and have about half their negative values; with APL off they have fewer entries between 0.5 and 1.5 z and several times more above 4 z.](results/E68_readout_low_end/E68_amplitude_histograms.png)
???

**The target's definition is open, and the readout's match of the mean depends on it.** How the recordings are read moves the flies' mean effect about as much as the readout moves a model's: over the six readings of the table of readings above it runs from {{data:apl_effect_thresholded_mean ~ +.3f}} with sub-threshold values set to 0 to {{data:apl_effect_noise_corrected_mean ~ +.3f}} corrected for trial-to-trial noise, with the {{data:apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}} of the recordings as measured between them. The model's mean effect does not follow: setting the sub-threshold values to 0 lowers the flies' mean effect by a quarter and leaves the reference model's where it was, because the model makes its mean effect with its responders alone. Through the calibrated readout the products of two responding entries give it {{diff:E68/variant_amplitude.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;config=cal:term_both_off -- E68/variant_amplitude.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;config=cal:term_both_on ~ +.3f}}, against {{E68/data_effect_terms.csv:both__effect_mean:observed ~ +.3f}} in the flies, and the products of a responder with a sub-threshold entry {{diff:E68/variant_amplitude.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;config=cal:term_one_off -- E68/variant_amplitude.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;config=cal:term_one_on ~ +.3f}}, against {{E68/data_effect_terms.csv:one__effect_mean:observed ~ +.3f}}; none of the nine models examined most closely gets any of its mean effect from that second term. The calibrated readout's agreement with the flies' mean effect therefore holds for the readings that keep the sub-threshold values, the recordings as measured among them, and not for the two that drop them, and where it holds it is reached through the wrong term: with the sub-threshold values set to 0 on both sides, or with both sides binarized, the reference model's mean effect is above the upper end of the flies' 90 % interval for the reading ({{data:apl_effect_thresholded_mean.hi ~ +.3f}} and {{data:apl_effect_binary_mean.hi ~ +.3f}}) and its loss is higher (second and third rows of that table). Whether a model is held to raw counts or to a calibrated readout, and against which reading of the recordings, is still to be decided; until then both are reported, and the best-models table and the progress plot stay on raw counts and the recordings as measured. With a firing ceiling in the model the first of these choices matters little, since its raw counts and the calibrated readout then give nearly the same scores ([the firing ceiling for the KCs](#firing-ceiling), at the end of this section); the reading of the recordings stays open.

### The pattern part of the main target, by odor and by pair {#apl-effect-parts}

**The pattern part is a per-odor error and a pair-specific error, and the models are at about twice the data's floor, or more, on each.** Once the readout has supplied the mean, what is left of the best models' loss is its pattern part, and the per-odor profile of the APL effect took that part apart. Part of a per-pair effect is the sum of two per-odor terms, one for each odor of the pair (an odor's share of the effect in every pair it is in); the rest belongs to the pair alone. In the flies the per-odor terms hold {{E66/data_floor_parts.csv:data=graded;stat=measured_additive_r2:mean ~ .0%}} of the effect's variance across the 120 pairs, and an odor's share follows how much its response fraction rises when APL output is blocked (Pearson r {{E66/data_share_correlates.csv:data=graded;correlate=gain:pearson ~ .2f}} across the 16 odors). A model's pattern error [is split the same way](#fn-apl-effect-parts): the per-odor part is the error that 16 per-odor terms account for, the pair-specific part is the rest, and their squares add up to the squared pattern part. Each has a floor of its own, from recordings drawn again with replacement, as the loss has.

The pattern part of the APL-effect loss and its two parts (lower is better), on raw spike counts / through the calibrated saturating readout; for the KC data, the floor of each with its 90 % interval over the resampled recordings in parentheses

| | | raw spike counts / calibrated readout {span=4} |
| statistic | KC data | bouton baseline | the first joint fit | the current reference model | median of the saved models<br>inside the three bands<br>on the response rates |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

No saved model reaches either floor, and none comes close on both. The per-odor part is about the same in the bouton baseline, in the reference model and in the median saved model, a little over twice its floor, and somewhat lower in the first joint fit; the pair-specific part is the larger of the two in the models built on the joint fits and is what differs between models. Across the {{E66/parts_summary.csv:readout=raw:n_realistic ~ .0f}} saved models inside the three bands on the response rates the two parts are nearly unrelated (a Spearman correlation of {{E66/parts_summary.csv:readout=raw:spearman_per_odor_vs_pair_specific ~ .2f}} on raw spike counts and {{E66/parts_summary.csv:readout=saturating_cal_pr:spearman_per_odor_vs_pair_specific ~ .2f}} under the calibrated readout), so being good on one says nothing about the other: the lowest per-odor part on raw counts ({{E66/parts_summary.csv:readout=raw:best_apl_effect_per_odor_value ~ .3f}}) is that of the 70 Hz joint fit with the gain on DC2 alone, one of the reduced gain sets, and the lowest pair-specific part ({{E66/parts_summary.csv:readout=raw:best_apl_effect_pair_specific_value ~ .3f}}) that of a claw-permuted wiring from the PN→KC wiring substrate experiment, a control and not a candidate model.

??? The per-odor shares of the APL effect in the flies and in three models
![Each odor's share of the per-pair APL effect (a pair's effect is approximated by the sum of its two odors' shares), in the KC data (black, with the 90 % interval over resampled recordings) and in the current reference model, the first joint fit and the reference model with the searched weak-odor input (an oracle), on raw spike counts (left) and through the calibrated saturating readout (right). On raw counts the models' shares fall short of the flies' for most odors and are near zero for ms, paa and B-myr; through the readout most are within the flies' interval, with 1-5ol's too large in all three models and the terpenes' in the reference model.](results/E66_per_odor_apl_profile/E66_shares_key_models.png)

**Setting every odor's response fraction on the flies' does not lower the loss.** If the per-odor part were the strength of APL's action on each odor, giving every odor the flies' response fraction in both conditions should remove it. [The per-odor rate oracle](#gap-pairs) had done that through the inputs, one condition at a time; here it was done through APL itself, by a second [oracle](#fn-apl-effect-parts): each odor's ORN input was scaled until its response fraction with APL off equals the flies' with APL output blocked (TNT), and that odor's APL→KC and APL→bouton scales were then multiplied by a factor of its own until its response fraction with APL on equals the control flies' (second model column). The per-odor response rates are then the flies' in both conditions, and the loss is where it was, on raw spike counts and under the calibrated readout. Under the readout the per-odor part falls by about a quarter, still well above its floor, and the pair-specific part rises enough to leave the pattern part where it was; on raw counts neither moves. The loss does not move either in the two 70 Hz fits of the reference model's family, in the first joint fit (where the oracle is partial: its recruitment with APL off saturates before most odors reach the flies' fraction) or in the reference model with the bounded oracle's searched weak-odor input, nor when the rate oracle's two sets of input scales are read together, one for each condition. How many KCs APL removes for each odor is therefore not what sets the pattern of the APL effect; which KCs two odors share is. The factors do say what an APL that gave the flies' per-odor response rates would do differently from the model's: {{E66/multipliers.csv:base=thr40_gain1.5;variant=both;odor=eb:multiplier ~ .1f}} times the inhibition on eb and {{E66/multipliers.csv:base=thr40_gain1.5;variant=both;odor=ep:multiplier ~ .1f}} times on ep, half or less on paa, B-myr, euc and 1-5ol and {{E66/multipliers.csv:base=thr40_gain1.5;variant=both;odor=-aPine:multiplier ~ .2f}} times on -aPine in the reference model, less wherever an odor's response with APL off is concentrated in few KCs (a Spearman correlation of {{E66/multiplier_correlates.csv:base=thr40_gain1.5;variant=both;property=off_spike_share_top1:spearman ~ .2f}} across the 16 odors between the factor and the share of the odor's APL-off spikes in its top 1 % of KCs). Imposing that rule does not help the main target, so a per-odor APL rule fitted to the response rates is not pursued; paa's, euc's and -aPine's factors are in part corrections for [an input at another concentration](#fn-concentrations).

**The pair-specific part comes with breadth-weighted APL.** On raw spike counts it is about twice the bouton baseline's in the first joint fit and in the reference model (first table), and in the breadth-weighted APL experiment it rose with the exponent of the spike-total breadth at unchanged response rates (from {{E66/by_variant_parts.csv:folder=E12_breadth_apl;tag=k0;readout=raw:apl_effect_pair_specific ~ .3f}} without the weighting to {{E66/by_variant_parts.csv:folder=E12_breadth_apl;tag=spikes_k3;readout=raw:apl_effect_pair_specific ~ .3f}} at exponent 3). The largest pair-specific errors of the reference model are of two kinds, both through KCs that two odors share by way of one strongly driven glomerulus. The small esters eb and ep share with the alcohols 1-8ol and 1-6ol a few KCs, most of them driven through DM5, that keep firing with APL on and then dominate both odors' response vectors: the model's APL brings these pairs closer, where the flies' APL moves them apart by more than it does most pairs (an APL effect of {{E66/worst_pairs.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=raw;odor_a=eb;odor_b=1-8ol:model_effect ~ +.2f}} for eb / 1-8ol against the flies' {{E66/worst_pairs.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=raw;odor_a=eb;odor_b=1-8ol:data_effect ~ +.2f}}, half of it carried by {{E66/worst_pairs.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=raw;odor_a=eb;odor_b=1-8ol:n_kcs_half ~ .0f}} KCs). The same esters share with paa and B-myr KCs driven through DM1 and DP1m that respond only with APL off; removing APL adds them to both odors at once, and the effect is too large ({{E66/worst_pairs.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=raw;odor_a=eb;odor_b=paa:model_effect ~ +.2f}} against {{E66/worst_pairs.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=raw;odor_a=eb;odor_b=paa:data_effect ~ +.2f}} for eb / paa). Under the calibrated readout the same pairs lead, with more KCs behind each. DM5, DM1 and DP1m carry the oracle gain, but the first kind of error leads in the first joint fit as well, which has no gain (a pair-specific error of {{E66/worst_pairs.csv:folder=E10_joint_fit;tag=k2;readout=raw;odor_a=eb;odor_b=1-8ol:pair_specific ~ +.2f}} for eb / 1-8ol against the reference model's {{E66/worst_pairs.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=raw;odor_a=eb;odor_b=1-8ol:pair_specific ~ +.2f}}): it comes with the breadth weighting, and the gain on DM5 enlarges it (among the variants of [the oracle gain decomposed by glomerulus](#gain-decomposition) for the 40 Hz fit, the pair-specific part on raw counts is {{E66/by_variant_parts.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr40__full;readout=raw:apl_effect_pair_specific ~ .3f}} with the gain on all eight glomeruli, {{E66/by_variant_parts.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr40__loo_DM5;readout=raw:apl_effect_pair_specific ~ .3f}} with DM5's removed and {{E66/by_variant_parts.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr40__none;readout=raw:apl_effect_pair_specific ~ .3f}} with none).

**Lowering the breadth exponent trades the pair-specific part against the mean effect and the block.** The breadth exponent is the power of a KC's breadth (its spike total over the 16 odors with APL off) by which breadth-weighted APL scales that KC's APL→KC weight; the reference model was fitted with 2. It was changed in the reference model at otherwise fitted parameters, the APL→KC scale re-tuned to the fitted response rate with APL on, so that the model with APL off is the same in every column (last three columns of the table). Without the weighting the pair-specific part falls to near the bouton baseline's level, by half on raw spike counts, and on raw counts the mean APL effect and the block all but disappear with it: keeping APL off the high-firing KCs is what moves odors apart overall and makes the block, and where two odors share such KCs it is also the pair-specific error. On raw counts the trade has no good point. Under the calibrated readout, which supplies the mean by itself, exponent 1 gives the reference model a lower loss than the fitted exponent 2, the lowest of the series, with most of the block on raw counts and the response rates unchanged; it is a one-parameter change of the fitted model and not a fit, and the two 70 Hz fits of the family gain nothing from it (figure). Taking a KC's breadth as the number of odors it responds to, instead of its spike total, raises the pair-specific part less for the same mean effect and gives the lowest loss of the series on raw counts, with a smaller block. Removing the weighting, or weighting by the number of odors, also concentrates the response with APL on in fewer KCs, which the bands on the response rates do not see; the band on odors per KC counts responders and not their amplitudes, and with APL on every column passes it but the one without the weighting ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E66_per_odor_apl_profile;tag=C_thr40_gain1.5_spikes_k0:opk_on_tv ~ .2f}} from the control flies' distribution, against a band of {{data:ctrl_odors_per_kc_tv_max ~ .2f}}; a participation ratio among responders of {{E66/parts_bc_table.csv:C_thr40_gain1.5_spikes_k0:participation_ratio_resp_on ~ .1%}} without the weighting, against {{E66/parts_bc_table.csv:O_thr40_gain1.5_untuned:participation_ratio_resp_on ~ .1%}} as fitted and at exponent 1, and {{data:ctrl_participation_ratio_resp ~ .1%}} in the control flies), and that ratio is what the readout is calibrated on.

![The breadth exponent of the APL→KC weights in the three fits of the reference model's family (blue: the current reference model; orange: its 70 Hz fit; green: the 70 Hz fit with the gain at 1.25), with a KC's breadth taken as its spike total with APL off (solid) or as the number of odors it responds to (dashed), and the APL→KC scale re-tuned to each fit's response rate with APL on. Columns: the APL-effect loss, the mean APL effect, the per-odor part and the pair-specific part of the loss; top row on raw spike counts, bottom row through the calibrated saturating readout. Dotted lines: the floor of the loss and the flies' mean effect.](results/E66_per_odor_apl_profile/E66_breadth_exponent.png)

### The main target fitted directly, on raw spike counts and under the readout {#apl-effect-fits}

**Fitted directly, the main target is matched in its mean under the readout but not in its pattern; on raw counts the lower losses are bought with the block, and through the trial noise the fit that keeps the block is the lowest, narrowly.** The APL-effect fits put the APL-effect loss first in the joint fit's loss, the response rates held as constraints, and fit its four parameters from five bases at the flies' response rate, each once under [the calibrated readout](#fn-calibrated-readout) and once on raw spike counts.

Three of the five bases, each fitted under both definitions, and every fit scored under both (raw: on raw spike counts; calibrated: through the calibrated saturating readout). Three of the fits were run through the trial noise

Under the readout four bases end between {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k3_cap3:cal_loss ~ .3f}} and {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3_DM1:cal_loss ~ .3f}} (the current reference model at {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:cal_loss ~ .3f}}, from {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=saturating_cal_pr:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}), their mean effects inside the flies' 90 % interval and their losses nearly all pattern; two of them, the reference model's fit and the first joint fit's family at breadth exponent 3, are outside the band on odors per KC with APL off. The same family at breadth exponent 2, with no per-claw threshold and no glomerulus gain, reaches {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:cal_loss ~ .3f}}, against a floor of {{data:apl_effect_rmse_floor ~ .3f}} and the {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E10_joint_fit;tag=k2;readout=saturating_cal_pr:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} of the first joint fit itself, fitted at 5 % to other targets. Its mean effect is the flies', but its pattern part, most of it pair-specific, is no lower than what an effect with no pattern would score ({{data:apl_effect_sd ~ .3f}}), although ρ on the per-pair APL effect is {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:cal_rho ~ .2f}}.

In the breadth-2 family the definition changes the number, not the model: fitted on raw counts, the same start ends at nearly the same parameters and scores (first two columns). The other bases come apart: fitted on raw counts they give up most or all of their KC–KC inhibition and the block for a larger mean effect, and two then go below the breadth-2 fit on raw counts, the breadth-3 family and the 70 Hz base with the gain on DM1 alone (fourth and last columns). Through [the matched trial noise](#fn-trial-noise) the order changes. The 70 Hz base's loss rises, and it rests on a few of its KCs ([the next subsection](#high-firing-kcs)). The breadth-3 fit holds, with part of a block coming back, and the breadth-2 fit's loss falls with its block kept, so that it is the lowest of the three, but narrowly: its margin over the breadth-3 fit is about the difference between the two noise levels, on one seed of the noise.

Under the readout the family's lead needs the per-claw threshold absent and a breadth exponent near 2: it ends at {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k1.5_cap3_c2:cal_loss ~ .3f}} and {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k1_cap3_c2:cal_loss ~ .3f}} at exponents 1.5 and 1, and the 40 Hz model without the gain, whose structure differs from it only in the per-claw threshold, ends at {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=thr40_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:cal_loss ~ .3f}}, where the reference model does.

Three things remain in the breadth-2 fit: the pair-specific part of the pattern; the block under the readout ({{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:cal_block ~ +.3f}} against the flies' {{data:block ~ +.3f}}), which a block term in the loss does not raise; and the recruitment with APL off, where {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:opk_off_0 ~ .0%}} of its KCs stay silent against the flies' {{data:tnt_odors_per_kc_0 ~ .0%}}, and a term on odors per KC in the loss moves the distance from the flies' distribution only from {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:opk_off_dist ~ .3f}} to {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=rate0.068_aplcalopk_k2_cap3:opk_off_dist ~ .3f}} (which KCs are released when APL is removed, and what moving the release costs, is in [the input-scaled KC threshold](#input-scaled-threshold), at the end of this section). The fit is inside all four realism bands, at the flies' response rates and their ratio; its response rate ρ with APL on is {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:rate_rho_on ~ .2f}}, against the first joint fit's {{E10:k2:spearman_rate_on_vs_data ~ .2f}}, and its other statistics are in its column of the best-models table. Its somatic threshold ({{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:thr_factor ~ .2f}} of the baseline's) and its APL→bouton scale, near the bound, are hard to defend; each fit is a local search from one start, and the pairs with paa, euc and -aPine rest on [ORN input at another concentration](#fn-concentrations). Fitted again with a firing ceiling in the model, the same family scores on raw counts nearly as it does under the readout, lower than any fit of this subsection does on raw counts ([the firing ceiling for the KCs](#firing-ceiling)). Fitted again on seven re-drawn wirings, the family ends around the loss it has on the real wiring, and its mean over the wirings is lower ([the APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings](#wiring-population), at the end of this section).

### The high-firing KCs that survive APL, and what rests on them {#high-firing-kcs}

**On raw spike counts about twenty KCs of the real wiring hold the reference model's mean APL effect down.** The shortfall of the mean APL effect is the larger part of most losses in the best-models table, and at the same fitted scales the reference model's mean effect is larger on shuffled wirings than on the real one. The pairwise co-input examined in [the wiring experiments](#wiring-feature) is not the reason: with the excess of the over-shared glomerulus pairs removed from the real wiring the mean effect stays at {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E52_glomerulus_pairs;tag=C_thr40_gain1.5_lesion_targeted-seed1_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} to {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E52_glomerulus_pairs;tag=C_thr40_gain1.5_lesion_targeted-seed2_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} over three draws, against {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} untouched, and on the six shuffled draws matched to the real wiring on every glomerulus pair's count of shared KCs it is {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E59_coinput_shuffle;tag=M_thr40_gain1.5_curveball_within_type_matched-seed3_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} to {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E59_coinput_shuffle;tag=M_thr40_gain1.5_curveball_within_type_matched-seed2_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} (the flies' {{data:apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}}). The experiment on the high-firing KCs that survive APL looked for the reason in single cells. Two sets were fixed before any simulation: a model's high-firing set, its KCs with twenty or more spikes to some odor with APL off, and its survivors, those of them that also reach twenty spikes to some odor with APL on. The current reference model has {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:n_high_firing ~ .0f}} KCs in the first set and {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:n_survivors ~ .0f}} survivors, of {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:n_kcs ~ .0f}} KCs. With APL on the survivors respond to {{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=survivor:n_odors_on ~ .1f}} of the 16 odors each, at {{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=survivor:max_count_on ~ .0f}} spikes to their best odor on average, where the rest of the high-firing set responds to {{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=high_firing_other:n_odors_on ~ .1f}} odors at {{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=high_firing_other:max_count_on ~ .0f}} spikes; they hold {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:survivor_share_sq_on ~ .0%}} of an odor's summed squared responses with APL on, the quantity a cosine is built from, so with APL on they are the KCs that two odors have in common. Taken apart by class of KC, the model's mean effect of {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} is {{E70/class_parts.csv:key=ref40;readout=raw:effect__survivor ~ +.3f}} from the survivors, {{E70/class_parts.csv:key=ref40;readout=raw:effect__high_firing_other ~ +.3f}} from the rest of the high-firing set and {{E70/class_parts.csv:key=ref40;readout=raw:effect__responder ~ +.3f}} from the other responding KCs.

Eight saved models with KCs left out of both conditions after the fact, nothing simulated again: the two KCs with the largest spike counts with APL on, or the model's survivors. In the rows of the loss and of the mean effect the two values are on raw spike counts / through the calibrated saturating readout, its half-saturation set again on the KCs that are kept; the response rates and the bands are those of the model as saved
