**Left out after the fact, the survivors give back about half of what the real wiring lacks on raw counts; under the calibrated readout the same cells keep the mean effect near the flies'.** With its survivors left out the reference model's mean effect on raw counts rises about half of the way to its shuffled wirings' and its loss falls a little (first model column of the table above); the same leave-out on nine shuffled draws of the model (three each of the claw permutation, the within-type claw permutation and the within-type Curveball), where the survivors hold less, moves the mean effect by between {{diff:E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E47_wiring_features;tag=A_thr40_gain1.5_claw_permutation-seed2_retuned:raw__survivors__mean -- E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E47_wiring_features;tag=A_thr40_gain1.5_claw_permutation-seed2_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} and {{diff:E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E47_wiring_features;tag=A_thr40_gain1.5_curveball_within_type-seed1_retuned:raw__survivors__mean -- E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E47_wiring_features;tag=A_thr40_gain1.5_curveball_within_type-seed1_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}}. With the whole high-firing set left out the real wiring and the shuffles no longer differ (a loss of {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:raw__hf_all__rmse ~ .3f}} against {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E47_wiring_features;tag=A_thr40_gain1.5_curveball_within_type-seed3_retuned:raw__hf_all__rmse ~ .3f}} to {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E47_wiring_features;tag=A_thr40_gain1.5_curveball_within_type-seed1_retuned:raw__hf_all__rmse ~ .3f}} on the nine draws). Under the calibrated readout the reference model's mean effect is already above the flies'; leaving the survivors out raises it further and the loss with it. The same cells are a fault of the model on raw counts and a support under the readout.

![How few KCs the APL-effect loss rests on. Seven saved models with their highest-firing KCs (by spike count with APL on) left out of both conditions after the fact, against the number left out; stars: the model's survivors left out. Top: the APL-effect loss (lower is better); bottom: the mean APL effect; left: raw spike counts; right: the calibrated saturating readout. Dashed lines: the floor of the loss and the flies' mean effect. The model with the gain on DM1 alone is the 70 Hz reference fit with the oracle gain kept on DM1 only, tuned to 5 %.](results/E70_high_firing_kcs/E70_leave_out.png)

**Re-drawing the survivors' claws raises the mean effect in simulation, and the controls do not.** In the reference model each claw of the survivors was exchanged with a claw of a KC of the same type outside the set, so that every KC keeps its number of claws, every glomerulus its number of claws and every KC type its repertoire ({{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed1_retuned:n_swaps ~ .0f}} exchanges; the glomerulus pairs' counts of shared KCs stay correlated with the real wiring's at {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed1_retuned:coinput_corr_vs_real ~ .2f}}). The fitted APL and KC–KC scales were held and the somatic threshold re-tuned to the response rate, three draws per arm; the real wiring run the same way reproduced the saved model exactly. The mean effect on raw counts rose in each of the three draws, on average by about half of the difference between the real wiring and its within-type claw permutations (first row of the table below). Re-drawing as many high-firing KCs that do not survive, matched to the survivors in KC type and number of claws, left it where it was, as did re-drawing as many matched KCs from outside the high-firing set ({{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_ctl_outside-seed1_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_ctl_outside-seed2_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_ctl_outside-seed3_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}}), and putting the survivors' real claws back into the three claw permutations lowered their mean effect by about as much as the re-draw had raised the real wiring's. Few survivors form again on the re-drawn wirings, so it is the wiring of these cells, and not the inhibition at the fitted scales, that makes them. Re-drawing the whole high-firing set goes a little further on average. In the 70 Hz fit of the same model, where the difference to the shuffles is smaller, re-drawing its {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr70_gain1.5_real_fit:n_survivors ~ .0f}} survivors takes the mean effect from {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr70_gain1.5_real_fit:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} to {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr70_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed1_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr70_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed2_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr70_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed3_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} and leaves the loss where it was ({{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr70_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed1_retuned:raw__rmse ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr70_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed2_retuned:raw__rmse ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr70_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed3_retuned:raw__rmse ~ .3f}} from {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr70_gain1.5_real_fit:raw__rmse ~ .3f}}; all inside the realism bands).

The current reference model at its fitted scales, the somatic threshold re-tuned to the response rate: on the real wiring, on the real wiring with the claws of a set of KCs re-drawn, and on three within-type claw permutations without and with the survivors' real claws put back. Three draws per arm, given in the order of the draws (the put-back arm on the same three permutations as the untreated one). Outside a realism band: the real wiring, its control and one of the three draws with the survivors re-drawn, on the number of odors per KC with APL off, as the reference model is; and two of the three claw permutations, with or without the survivors' claws, on the response rate ρ with APL on

| odors per KC with APL off: distance of the model's distribution from the flies' (lower is better) | band {{data:tnt_odors_per_kc_tv_max ~ .3f}} | {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_real_fit:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} | {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed1_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed2_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed3_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} | {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_ctl_hf_nonsurv-seed1_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_ctl_hf_nonsurv-seed2_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_ctl_hf_nonsurv-seed3_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} | {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_hf_redraw-seed1_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_hf_redraw-seed2_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_hf_redraw-seed3_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} | {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E47_wiring_features;tag=A_thr40_gain1.5_claw_permutation_within_type-seed1_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} / {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E47_wiring_features;tag=A_thr40_gain1.5_claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} / {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E47_wiring_features;tag=A_thr40_gain1.5_claw_permutation_within_type-seed3_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} | {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_restore-seed1_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_restore-seed2_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_restore-seed3_retuned:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} |

**The gain is all in the mean part, and the block goes with the cells.** The loss barely moves: what the mean part gains, the pattern part loses (second and third rows of the table above), and under the calibrated readout, where the real wiring's mean effect is already above the flies', the loss rises in every draw. The fruity × small-ester block, which the reference model has at the data's size on the real wiring, falls to half or less with the survivors re-drawn and keeps most of its size in all but one draw of the controls ({{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_ctl_outside-seed1_retuned:raw__block ~ +.3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_ctl_outside-seed2_retuned:raw__block ~ +.3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_ctl_outside-seed3_retuned:raw__block ~ +.3f}} with the matched KCs from outside the high-firing set re-drawn); put back into the claw permutations, the survivors' claws bring part of it back in two of the three. APL's effect on the natural-source alignment (APL on minus APL off), of the wrong sign on the real wiring, moves further from the flies' when the survivors are re-drawn and toward it when their claws are put back. The cells that hold the mean effect down on raw counts are therefore the ones that carry the reference model's block, and on raw counts the two statistics pull against each other through the same twenty KCs. None of this costs a realism band: the re-drawn models stay at the flies' response rates, and the distance of the odors-per-KC distribution from the flies' with APL off, just outside its band on the real wiring, shrinks.

**More inhibition on these cells does not silence them.** With the wiring left as it is and the survivors' APL→KC weights multiplied by two and by four on top of the breadth weighting (an oracle, at weights no connectome supports), {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_surv_apl_x2:n_survivors ~ .0f}} and {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_surv_apl_x4:n_survivors ~ .0f}} of the {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_real_fit:n_survivors ~ .0f}} still reach twenty spikes with APL on, and the mean effect does not rise ({{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_surv_apl_x2:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} and {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_surv_apl_x4:raw__mean ~ +.3f}}, from {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_real_fit:raw__mean ~ +.3f}}; both runs, like the reference model itself, are just outside the band on the number of odors per KC with APL off). Their drive is too far above threshold for inhibition aimed at them to be the remedy; a mechanism that removes them has to cap their output or lower their drive.

**Most of the reference model's survivors are the cells through which the oracle gain lifts euc and -aPine, and they are not realistic KCs.** On the real wiring the survivors' claws are concentrated on fewer glomeruli than those of the rest of the high-firing set ({{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=survivor:max_claws_one_glomerulus ~ .2f}} claws from their most-sampled glomerulus against {{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=high_firing_other:max_claws_one_glomerulus ~ .2f}}, {{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=survivor:n_glomeruli ~ .1f}} distinct glomeruli against {{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=high_firing_other:n_glomeruli ~ .1f}}); they have twice the claws from DP1m ({{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=survivor:claws_DP1m ~ .2f}} against {{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=high_firing_other:claws_DP1m ~ .2f}}), more from the eight glomeruli that carry the oracle gain ({{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=survivor:claws_gained8 ~ .2f}} against {{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=high_firing_other:claws_gained8 ~ .2f}}) and less APL→KC weight from the connectome ({{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=survivor:apl_weight_connectome ~ .2f}} against {{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=high_firing_other:apl_weight_connectome ~ .2f}} of the mean weight), and the KC–KC inhibition they receive is no different from the other KCs'. {{lit:14 # survivors of the reference model whose largest APL-on count is to euc (10) or -aPine (4), counted in results/E70_high_firing_kcs/survivors.csv, key ref40, column top_odor_on}} of the {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:n_survivors ~ .0f}} fire most to euc or -aPine ({{lit:21 # survivors of the 70 Hz reference fit whose largest APL-on count is to euc (14) or -aPine (7), counted in results/E70_high_firing_kcs/survivors.csv, key ref70, column top_odor_on}} of {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:n_survivors ~ .0f}} in the 70 Hz fit), and all of the survivors of the two fits with the gain on DM1 alone fire most to paa. euc and paa are among the weak-input odors that the oracle gain was introduced to lift, and -aPine is carried by the same gained glomeruli as euc (DP1m and VC1); the survivors are how the gain lifts them, through a few over-driven KCs where the flies have many moderately driven ones. euc, -aPine and paa are also the three odors whose ORN input is taken from another concentration than the KC recordings' ([the concentrations](#fn-concentrations): euc's and -aPine's from a higher one, paa's from a lower one), so how many such cells a model has depends on an input that is not the recordings'. The cells themselves are far from recorded KCs: with APL on the reference model's survivors fire up to {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:max_count_on ~ .0f}} spikes to one odor, and with APL off {{E70/wiring_by_class.csv:key=ref40;kc_class=survivor:max_count_off ~ .0f}} on average and up to {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:max_count_off ~ .0f}}, against a bound of about {{lit:14 # the bound the firing ceiling for the KCs (E69) takes for a KC's response with APL on: the mean plus three standard deviations of the 4.9 ± 3.0 spikes per 0.5 s response of α′/β′ KCs in Turner, Bazhenov and Laurent (2008), as quoted in E70_high_firing_kcs.md, Realism; not re-derived for this page}} spikes that [the firing ceiling for the KCs](#firing-ceiling) takes from recordings of KCs (the mean plus three standard deviations of the counts in Turner, Bazhenov and Laurent, 2008). The realism bands hold a model to which KCs respond, not to how hard they fire, and do not see them.

**The lowest losses on raw spike counts rest on two to six KCs of the real wiring; the lowest under the calibrated readout rests on none in particular.** The 70 Hz reference fit with the oracle gain kept on DM1 alone, which held the lowest loss on raw counts of the models on the real wiring before the APL-effect fits ([the oracle gain decomposed by glomerulus](#gain-decomposition)), has {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr70__only_DM1:n_survivors ~ .0f}} survivors, and with its two highest-firing KCs left out its loss on raw counts goes from {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr70__only_DM1:raw__rmse ~ .3f}} to {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr70__only_DM1:raw__top2__rmse ~ .3f}}, above the first joint fit's, which the same leave-out barely moves (first table of this subsection; the best-models table carries the same leave-out as a row for every column without a firing ceiling). [The APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits) arrived at the same kind of model as their best fit on raw counts, the 70 Hz base with the gain on DM1 alone fitted on raw counts at the flies' response rate, and it rests on its {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1:n_survivors ~ .0f}} survivors in the same way (last column of the first table). Re-drawing those survivors' claws in simulation, at the fit's scales and with three draws per arm as above, takes its mean effect from {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_real_fit:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} past the flies' to {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_surv_redraw-seed1_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_surv_redraw-seed2_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_surv_redraw-seed3_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} and its loss from {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_real_fit:raw__rmse ~ .3f}} to {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_surv_redraw-seed1_retuned:raw__rmse ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_surv_redraw-seed2_retuned:raw__rmse ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_surv_redraw-seed3_retuned:raw__rmse ~ .3f}}, where re-drawing as many matched high-firing KCs that do not survive leaves the loss at {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_ctl_hf_nonsurv-seed1_retuned:raw__rmse ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_ctl_hf_nonsurv-seed2_retuned:raw__rmse ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_ctl_hf_nonsurv-seed3_retuned:raw__rmse ~ .3f}}. On three untreated within-type claw permutations the fit's scales give a mean effect of {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_cpwt_untreated-seed1_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_cpwt_untreated-seed2_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_cpwt_untreated-seed3_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} and a loss of {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_cpwt_untreated-seed1_retuned:raw__rmse ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_cpwt_untreated-seed2_retuned:raw__rmse ~ .3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=C_e67-thr70_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3_DM1_cpwt_untreated-seed3_retuned:raw__rmse ~ .3f}}. The fit found scales at which a handful of paa cells of this wiring hold the mean effect at the flies' level; without those cells the same scales overshoot, and on the real wiring the loss is then about the first joint fit's. Every run of these arms is inside the realism bands. The best fit under the calibrated readout, the first joint fit's family at breadth exponent 2, with neither the gain nor the per-claw threshold, has about as many survivors as the reference model and does not rest on them: with up to twenty of its highest-firing KCs left out its loss moves by at most {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:raw__max_change_k20 ~ .3f}} on raw counts and {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:cal__max_change_k20 ~ .3f}} under the readout.

**Strong KC–KC inhibition makes more of them.** The fit with the block term on the batched proxy, which raised the KC–KC scale and lowered the somatic threshold, has more than twice its seed's survivors, holding half of an odor's summed squared responses with APL on, and with KC–KC inhibition held at 45 there are several times more (the three columns of that family in the first table). With them left out, the mean effect of the fit on the proxy is above its seed's as saved. In this family the inhibition that spreads the responses over more KCs with APL off, and brings the number of odors per KC to the flies', also leaves a class of KCs that APL does not stop, and the regime's cost on the main target runs through them. Over the {{E70/archive_relations.csv:operating_point=6.8 %;x=survivor_share_sq_on;y=raw__mean:n ~ .0f}} saved models tuned to the flies' response rate, shuffled wirings and controls included, the larger the survivors' share of the APL-on responses, the smaller the mean effect on raw counts (a Spearman correlation of {{E70/archive_relations.csv:operating_point=6.8 %;x=survivor_share_sq_on;y=raw__mean:spearman ~ +.2f}}) and the larger the loss ({{E70/archive_relations.csv:operating_point=6.8 %;x=survivor_share_sq_on;y=raw__rmse:spearman ~ +.2f}}); under the calibrated readout the two correlations are {{E70/archive_relations.csv:operating_point=6.8 %;x=survivor_share_sq_on;y=cal__mean:spearman ~ +.2f}} and {{E70/archive_relations.csv:operating_point=6.8 %;x=survivor_share_sq_on;y=cal__rmse:spearman ~ +.2f}}. What separates the KCs that stay silent with APL off from those that respond to eight or more odors is input and not inhibition: the number of claws on the eight gained glomeruli tells the two groups apart almost completely (the chance that a KC of the second group has more such claws than one of the first is {{E70/opk_features.csv:key=ref40;feature=claws_gained8:auc_8plus_vs_silent ~ .2f}} in the reference model, where one half is no separation), the KC–KC inhibition received not at all ({{E70/opk_features.csv:key=ref40;feature=kckc_received_off:auc_8plus_vs_silent ~ .2f}}).

**One fly is about as far from the other flies as the best models are from the data.** Scored as a model is, one control recording paired with one recording with APL output blocked (TNT), against the mean of the remaining recordings, has an APL-effect loss of {{lit:0.111 # median of column rmse over the 88 pairs of one control recording and one recording with APL output blocked (TNT) in results/E70_high_firing_kcs/yardstick.csv}} (the median over the pairs; {{lit:0.074 # minimum of column rmse in results/E70_high_firing_kcs/yardstick.csv}} to {{lit:0.263 # maximum of column rmse in results/E70_high_firing_kcs/yardstick.csv}}) and a pattern part of {{lit:0.092 # median of column rmse_centered in results/E70_high_firing_kcs/yardstick.csv}}, measurement noise included; the floor of {{data:apl_effect_rmse_floor ~ .3f}} is that of the mean over all recordings. The best fits on one wiring score no worse than a typical single pair of recordings does. Averaging a model over shuffled draws of its wiring lowers its pattern part only modestly (for the reference model on five claw permutations, {{E70/yardstick_models.csv:model=ref40;what=mean over the claw permutation draws;readout=raw:per_draw_pattern_mean ~ .3f}} per draw on average and {{E70/yardstick_models.csv:model=ref40;what=mean over the claw permutation draws;readout=raw:rmse_centered ~ .3f}} for the mean over the draws), so most of a model's pattern error is common to the draws and not one wiring's accident. The model's own single pseudo-recordings through the matched trial noise could not be scored the same way here (the pipeline saved only their means); scored since, a single pseudo-recording pair of the breadth-2 fit is closer to the data's mean than a single pair of recordings is ([the input-scaled KC threshold](#input-scaled-threshold), at the end of this section).

**What follows for the definition of the target, the block and the realism of the models.** On raw spike counts the shortfall of the mean effect in the models with the oracle gain is in good part the amplitude of about one in a hundred of their KCs, the least realistic cells they have. The calibrated readout takes that amplitude away without choosing cells, which is how it supplied these models' mean effect ([above](#readout)), and a firing ceiling in the model takes it away at the source ([the next subsection](#firing-ceiling)); under the readout the same cells support the mean effect, so in a model without a ceiling whether they count against it depends on the definition that is still open. Three things do not depend on it. The order of the best models on raw counts turns on two to six cells of one wiring, and the order under the calibrated readout does not, which favours the first joint fit's family over the fits with the gain on DM1 alone under either definition. The reference model's block is carried by its survivors, so whatever removes them, re-wiring here and the readout's compression before, removes the block with them; a model that is to have the block together with the flies' mean effect needs a carrier of the block that is not a few over-driven cells, which is what the mechanisms acting on the set of responding KCs are. And the weak-input odors have to reach their response rates through many moderately driven KCs: the ORN input of euc, -aPine and paa scaled toward the recordings' concentrations, or a gain kept off the glomeruli that give the survivors most of their claws, is the place to start.

### A firing ceiling for the KCs, and the two definitions of the main target {#firing-ceiling}

**With a ceiling on how many spikes a KC fires to an odor, the model's raw spike counts score on the main target as the calibrated readout does, inside the realism bands; the block is lost as it is under the readout, and which KCs respond with APL off does not change.** Until this experiment the model's KCs had neither a refractory period nor adaptation: after a spike the potential was reset and integration went on at once. The hardest-driven KC of the current reference model fires {{E69/stats_variants.csv:ref40_base:max_count_on ~ .0f}} spikes to one 0.5 s odor with APL on and {{E69/stats_variants.csv:ref40_base:max_count_off ~ .0f}} with APL off, where a reading of whole-cell recordings of KCs puts the bound at about {{lit:14 # the bound on a KC's spike count to one odor with APL on that the firing ceiling for the KCs takes from whole-cell recordings: the mean plus three standard deviations (4.9 + 3 x 3.0 = 13.9) of the spikes per response of α′/β′ KCs to a 500 ms odor in Turner, Bazhenov and Laurent (2008); E69_kc_firing_ceiling.md, Design (part B) and Caveats}} spikes (the mean plus three standard deviations of the counts of the α′/β′ KCs in Turner, Bazhenov and Laurent, 2008). The firing ceiling for the KCs adds both to the model ([how it works and how its settings were chosen](#fn-firing-ceiling)): a refractory period of 3 ms, and an adaptation that raises a KC's threshold by a fixed fraction of itself at every spike and decays over 1 s, longer than the odor, so that within one odor it acts as a budget of spikes. It was applied first at fixed scales, to the current reference model and to the best APL-effect fit under each definition of the model's side ([the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits)), with both response rates held at each model's own by re-tuning the somatic threshold and the APL scales, so that nothing is bought with the response rates. The breadth-2 fit was then fitted again under the APL-effect loss, with the ceiling fixed and both definitions in the loss, at two strengths of the adaptation: the literature setting ({{E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2:kc_adapt_inc ~ .0%}} of the threshold per spike), at which its largest count with APL on is nearest the recordings' bound, and a stronger setting ({{E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.1114t1000_k2:kc_adapt_inc ~ .0%}}), the weakest at which the calibrated readout has nothing left to compress. The reference model was fitted again at the stronger setting, where its own largest count with APL on is at the recordings' bound.

The breadth-2 APL-effect fit and the current reference model, each without a firing ceiling and fitted again with one. Where a row gives two values without naming conditions, they are on raw spike counts / through the calibrated saturating readout, its half-saturation set again on each model's own APL-on counts; at the stronger setting it runs to the upper end of its range, where the readout changes nothing. The fit without a ceiling and the two fits of its family with one went through the matched trial noise

| realism bands | — | {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E69_kc_firing_ceiling;tag=e67cal_base:realism}} | {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E69_kc_firing_ceiling;tag=rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2:realism}} | {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E69_kc_firing_ceiling;tag=rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.1114t1000_k2:realism}} | {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E69_kc_firing_ceiling;tag=ref40_base:realism}} | {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E69_kc_firing_ceiling;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.1114t1000_k2:realism}} |

**The raw counts now give what the readout gave.** On raw counts the loss of the breadth-2 family falls by a third at the literature setting and by nearly half at the stronger one, and the reference model's halves (first row). The mean APL effect, below the flies' 90 % interval on raw counts without a ceiling, is inside it in every fit with one, and the two definitions come together: at the stronger setting they give one number, and at the literature setting they are about a tenth as far apart as without a ceiling. The calibrated readout was standing in for firing that the model did not bound, and with a ceiling of this kind the choice between raw counts and the readout matters little. Most of this is there before any fitting: at fixed scales, with both response rates held, the breadth-2 fit goes from {{E69/stats_variants.csv:e67cal_base:apl_raw_rmse ~ .3f}} on raw counts to {{E69/stats_variants.csv:e67cal_r3_a1_t1000:apl_raw_rmse ~ .3f}} at the literature setting and {{E69/stats_variants.csv:e67cal_r3_a2_t1000:apl_raw_rmse ~ .3f}} at the stronger one. The best fit on raw counts without a ceiling, the 70 Hz base with the gain on DM1 alone, gets worse with one ({{E69/stats_variants.csv:e67raw_base:apl_raw_rmse ~ .3f}} to {{E69/stats_variants.csv:e67raw_r3_a1_t1000:apl_raw_rmse ~ .3f}} and {{E69/stats_variants.csv:e67raw_r3_a2_t1000:apl_raw_rmse ~ .3f}} at the two settings) and was not fitted again; its lead rested on a few high-firing KCs ([above](#high-firing-kcs)). The data are not reached. The fit at the stronger setting has the lowest loss of any model on the real wiring inside all four realism bands, under either definition, and is still a third above the floor, nearly all of it pattern: ρ on the per-pair APL effect stays well under its ceiling, and the per-odor and the pair-specific part of the loss are {{E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.1114t1000_k2:apl_raw_per_odor ~ .3f}} and {{E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.1114t1000_k2:apl_raw_pair_specific ~ .3f}}, against floors of {{E66/data_floor_parts.csv:data=graded;stat=apl_effect_per_odor:mean ~ .3f}} and {{E66/data_floor_parts.csv:data=graded;stat=apl_effect_pair_specific:mean ~ .3f}}. Through [the matched trial noise](#fn-trial-noise) both fits with the ceiling stay below every APL-effect fit without one on raw counts (the fourth row here and the same row of the table of [the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits)), and the stronger setting's lead over the literature setting is gone, so the setting that agrees with the recordings loses nothing.

**The ceiling costs what the readout costs, and it does not change which KCs respond.** In the breadth-2 family the block falls to about two thirds of the flies' at the literature setting and a third at the stronger one, and the reference model's is all but gone; the natural-source alignment with APL on falls at the stronger setting, and the mean distances fall below the flies' in both conditions. The APL-effect loss scores none of these, and the calibrated readout costs the same, as expected if the two do the same job: on raw counts the block and the alignment were carried by the strong responders ([the high-firing KCs that survive APL](#high-firing-kcs)). With the ceiling no KC of the reference model reaches twenty spikes with APL on, so none of its survivors is left, and its mean effect on raw counts comes to the flies', further than re-drawing the survivors' claws had brought it ({{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed1_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed2_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} / {{E70/partB_scores.csv:tag=B_thr40_gain1.5_surv_redraw-seed3_retuned:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} over the three draws): the shortfall of the mean on raw counts was the strong firing of many KCs, not of those few alone. What the ceiling gains in realism is the spread of an odor's response over its KCs, which comes to the flies' (the row on the top 1 % of KCs), with APL on at the literature setting and with APL off at the stronger one. What it leaves is which KCs respond. The distance of the odors-per-KC distribution from the flies' with APL off hardly moves, about half of the KCs stay silent to all 16 odors with APL off against a third in the flies, and the reference model stays just outside that band with the ceiling as without it, also when a term on odors per KC is added to the loss of its fit ({{E69/stats_variants.csv:thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplbothopk_refr3_adapt0.1114t1000_k2:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} against a band of {{data:tnt_odors_per_kc_tv_max ~ .3f}}): the breadth of its recruitment with APL off belongs to the gain and the 40 Hz per-claw threshold, not to the firing. Nor do the amplitudes come to the flies' in both conditions. Read in the recordings' units as in the table of amplitudes [above](#readout), the responders of the fit at the literature setting average {{E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2:z_floor_noise_resp_amp_mean_on ~ .2f}} z with APL on and {{E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2:z_floor_noise_resp_amp_mean_acute_off ~ .2f}} z with APL off, where the flies' average {{data:ctrl_resp_amp_mean_z ~ .2f}} and {{data:tnt_resp_amp_mean_z ~ .2f}}: the ceiling lowers both together. It sets how much the strong responders fire; which KCs are recruited when APL is removed needs a mechanism of its own, and the one tried changes them at a cost on the main target ([the input-scaled KC threshold](#input-scaled-threshold), the next subsection).

**Other wirings, and a strength that is a reading.** Under the readout, one draw of the wiring moves this family's loss by more than the fits with a ceiling differ from the fit without one. The same family fitted without a ceiling on seven re-drawn wirings (four within-type claw permutations and three shuffles matched to the real wiring on every glomerulus pair's count of shared KCs; [the APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings](#wiring-population), at the end of this section) ends between {{E72/stats_variants.csv:wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed3_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3:cal_loss ~ .3f}} and {{E72/stats_variants.csv:wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed1_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3:cal_loss ~ .3f}} under the readout, the real wiring's {{E72/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3:cal_loss ~ .3f}} among them, and between {{E72/stats_variants.csv:wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed3_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3:raw_loss ~ .3f}} and {{E72/stats_variants.csv:wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3:raw_loss ~ .3f}} on raw counts. Under the readout the fits with a ceiling are therefore as good as the fit without one, not better; on raw counts they are below the fit of every one of those wirings, and the gain holds through the trial noise. The ceiling at the literature setting has since been fitted on three of those wirings as well, each beside the fit without a ceiling on the same wiring: on raw counts the loss changes by between {{E72/partG_paired.csv:wiring=P2:raw_loss_diff ~ +.3f}} and {{E72/partG_paired.csv:wiring=P1:raw_loss_diff ~ +.3f}} on the three, as by {{E72/partG_paired.csv:wiring=real:raw_loss_diff ~ +.3f}} on the real wiring, and under the readout by no more than {{E72/partG_paired.csv:wiring=P2:cal_loss_diff ~ .3f}} on any of the four, so the gain on raw counts is the family's and not the hemibrain wiring's. The stronger setting has been fitted on the real wiring only. Its strength is a reading of the recordings, not a measurement: they give the mean and the standard deviation of the spike counts per response ({{lit:4.9 ± 3.0 # spikes per response of α′/β′ KCs to a 500 ms odor (mean ± standard deviation, whole-cell recordings, APL intact), Turner, Bazhenov and Laurent (2008), as quoted in E69_kc_firing_ceiling.md, Design (part B), Realism and Caveats}} for α′/β′ KCs and {{lit:2.2 ± 1.2 # spikes per response of α/β KCs to a 500 ms odor (mean ± standard deviation, whole-cell recordings, APL intact), Turner, Bazhenov and Laurent (2008), as quoted in E69_kc_firing_ceiling.md, Realism and Caveats}} for α/β KCs, to other odors and concentrations than the KC data's) and no largest count, and the stronger setting, at which no KC of the breadth-2 fit fires more than {{E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.1114t1000_k2:max_count_on ~ .0f}} spikes with APL on, allows the α′/β′ KCs less than they show. Both fits of the breadth-2 family also ended with more KCs responding with APL off than they started with (a response rate {{E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2:rate_ratio_off_over_on ~ .2f}} and {{E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.1114t1000_k2:rate_ratio_off_over_on ~ .2f}} times that with APL on, from {{E69/stats_variants.csv:e67cal_base:rate_ratio_off_over_on ~ .2f}}; the flies' {{data:rate_ratio_fraction ~ .2f}}, 90 % interval {{data:rate_ratio_fraction.lo ~ .2f}} to {{data:rate_ratio_fraction.hi ~ .2f}}), which by itself raises the mean effect, so part of what the fits add to the runs at fixed scales may be bought that way; the ratio was not held in the fits. As everywhere, the pairs with paa, euc and -aPine rest on [ORN input at another concentration](#fn-concentrations).

### The input-scaled KC threshold: which KCs are released when APL is removed, and what moving the release costs {#input-scaled-threshold}

**A somatic threshold that scales with each KC's input moves the release of KCs, when APL is removed, off the many-claw cells, as the flies' numbers of odors per KC ask, and in every family the main target pays for it; fitted again, each family gets the mean effect back with as many broadly responding KCs as before, drawn from other cells, and loses on the pattern.** In the model every KC has the same somatic threshold, apart from a term proportional to its spontaneous input, so a KC with many claws crosses it for more odors than a KC with few. Breadth-weighted APL inhibits the broadly responding KCs most, so when APL is removed they are the ones released. In the breadth-2 APL-effect fit ([the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits)) the KCs that respond to eight or more odors with APL off have {{E70/opk_features.csv:key=e67__rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3;feature=n_claws:mean_8plus_off ~ .1f}} claws on average and the silent ones {{E70/opk_features.csv:key=e67__rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3;feature=n_claws:mean_silent_off ~ .1f}}, and the {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_base:rel_g9plus_n_kcs ~ .0f}} KCs with nine or more claws, of {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_base:n_kcs_on ~ .0f}}, take a third of the responses that removing APL adds. More of its KCs stay silent than in the flies, more respond to eight or more odors, and the number of odors per KC is spread more widely than in any recording. With every KC × odor entry counted only as a response or none (binarized: two or more spikes in the model, a trial mean above 1 in the recordings), removing APL then adds more overlap between odors than it does in the flies, although under the calibrated readout the fit's mean APL effect is the flies' (first model column of the table below). The low end of the readout found the same from the amplitudes ([above](#readout)): with APL off the models add strong responses where the flies add weak ones, and what the weak ones carry of the flies' APL effect is for the most part the weaker responses of their broadly responding KCs to their other odors ([the APL effect split by odors per KC](#apl-effect-by-class), the last subsection of this section). [The firing ceiling for the KCs](#firing-ceiling) changes how much the released KCs fire and not which KCs they are, and KC–KC inhibition strong enough to spread the release brings the silent share to the flies' ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E53_kckc_held_block;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_kckc45_k2_cap3:opk_off_0 ~ .0%}} in the 70 Hz model with the gain at 1.25 and KC–KC held at 45) at a mean APL effect of {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E53_kckc_held_block;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_kckc45_k2_cap3:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} on raw counts and {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E53_kckc_held_block;tag=thr70_gain1.25_rate0.068_kckc45_k2_cap3:cal__mean ~ +.3f}} under the readout. The one earlier test of thresholds by claw count, in the KC heterogeneity experiment, had not applied them.

The release through the many-claw KCs belongs to the family and not to the hemibrain wiring. Fitted on seven re-drawn wirings ([the APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings](#wiring-population), the next subsection), the breadth-2 family gives the KCs with nine or more claws, {{E72/release.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3:frac_kcs_9+ ~ .0%}} of the KCs, between {{E72/release.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed4_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3:share_added_9+ ~ .0%}} and {{E72/release.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-curveball_within_type_matched-seed2_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3:share_added_9+ ~ .0%}} of the added responses, against {{E72/release.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3:share_added_9+ ~ .0%}} on the real wiring, and over the eight wirings the squared coefficient of variation of the number of odors per KC with APL off is between {{E72/release.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3:opk_cv2_off ~ .2f}} and {{E72/release.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-curveball_within_type_matched-seed3_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3:opk_cv2_off ~ .2f}}, above every recording's (the fourth row of the table below). With the per-claw threshold at 40 Hz and no glomerulus gain the share is as large, between {{E72/release.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=thr40_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:share_added_9+ ~ .0%}} and {{E72/release.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=thr40_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:share_added_9+ ~ .0%}} over five wirings.

The input-scaled KC threshold multiplies each KC's threshold by a power of the KC's summed claw weight relative to the mean over KCs, the same with APL on and with APL off, so that it is a property of the cell and not of APL; with the power at zero it is the model as it was. Through the spontaneous-input term the threshold of the breadth-2 fit already grows with a KC's summed claw weight, with a log-log slope of {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_base:thr_loglog_slope_W ~ .2f}}; a power of 0.5 brings the slope to {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_a0.5:thr_loglog_slope_W ~ .2f}}, a threshold about proportional to the input. The power was swept at fixed scales, from 0.25 to 1 and at −0.5 as a control of the sign, on three models: the breadth-2 fit, the same fit with the firing ceiling at its literature setting, and the current reference model. At every setting both response rates were held at the model's own by re-tuning the somatic threshold on the APL-off response rate and then the APL→KC and APL→bouton scales together on the APL-on one, so that no change is bought with the response rates, and the per-KC breadth behind the APL→KC weights was computed again with the scaled thresholds, the weights forming on the cells as they then are. For each model the power kept is the one that brings the number of odors per KC with APL off closest to the flies' among the settings that stay inside the bands on odors per KC and on the response rate ρ with APL on, a choice that involves no distance between odors. Each of the three was then fitted again under the APL-effect loss with that power fixed, both definitions in the loss and the usual four parameters free.

The three models at fixed scales, both response rates held: without the scaling and at the power kept for each. Where a row gives three values without naming conditions they are binarized / through the calibrated saturating readout / on raw spike counts, the binarized ones scored against the binarized KC data

**At fixed scales the release moves the way the flies' numbers ask.** With the power at 0.5 the few-claw KCs of the breadth-2 fit take over the responses that removing APL adds (first row), and with APL off a KC with nine or more claws then responds to fewer odors than a KC with one to four ({{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_a0.5:rel_g9plus_opk_off ~ .1f}} against {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_a0.5:rel_g1_4_opk_off ~ .1f}} on average, from {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_base:rel_g9plus_opk_off ~ .1f}} against {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_base:rel_g1_4_opk_off ~ .1f}}). The distance of the odors-per-KC distribution from the flies' with APL off falls by more than a quarter, and the binarized mean APL effect and its loss come down toward the data's. The other two models move the same way, and the reference model, just outside the band on odors per KC with APL off as it stands, is well inside it with the scaling, where no model with that set of parameters on the real wiring had been. The flies' distribution is not reached: about four KCs in ten still respond to none of the odors with APL off, against a third in the flies, and in the breadth-2 fit, with or without the ceiling, the spread of the number of odors per KC stays above every recording's (the reference model's comes inside their range). The control of the sign moves the release the other way: at a power of −0.5 the KCs with nine or more claws take {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_a-0.5:rel_g9plus_share_added ~ .0%}} of the added responses, the distance with APL off rises to {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_a-0.5:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}}, outside the band, and the binarized mean APL effect rises to {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_a-0.5:apl_bin_mean ~ +.3f}}. Beyond the power kept the few-claw KCs become the over-driven ones: at a power of 1 the largest spike count of the breadth-2 fit with APL on is {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_a1:max_count_on ~ .0f}} and the distance with APL off is back at {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_a1:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}}.

**The graded APL effect falls with it.** Under the calibrated readout the mean effect of the breadth-2 fit, the flies' without the scaling, falls by about a fifth and its loss rises by a third; on raw counts the mean effect falls to less than half and the loss nearly doubles. In the other two models the mean effect and the loss move the same way under both definitions, and only the binarized loss gains. The breadth weighting computed again on the scaled cells holds back about half of the fall: with the breadth left as fitted, the mean effect of the breadth-2 fit under the readout is {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_a0.5_kb:apl_cal_mean ~ +.3f}} at the same power. ρ on odor-odor distances with APL on falls in the breadth-2 fit as well.

The three families fitted again under the APL-effect loss with the scaling fixed, each beside the same family without it. Two values without named conditions are on raw spike counts / through the calibrated saturating readout, its half-saturation set again on each model's own APL-on counts. The columns without the scaling are the breadth-2 APL-effect fit, the fit with the firing ceiling at its literature setting, and the current reference model, which was fitted to other targets (the fits of its set under the APL-effect loss are given in the text)

**Fitted again, every family gets the mean effect back and loses on the pattern.** In the two families of the first joint fit the mean APL effect is where it was without the scaling under both definitions (second row), and in the reference model's set it is at the level of the set's fits under the APL-effect loss ({{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3:raw_mean ~ +.3f}} on raw counts in its fit on raw counts and {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:cal_mean ~ +.3f}} under the readout in its fit under the readout, [the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits)). The loss is higher in all three, through the pattern part (third row). Under the readout the breadth-2 family at a power of 0.5 ends {{diff:E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_thrin0.5_k2:fit_apl_cal_rmse -- E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_base:apl_cal_rmse ~ .3f}} above the fit without the scaling and the family with the firing ceiling {{diff:E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:fit_apl_cal_rmse -- E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2:apl_cal_rmse ~ .3f}} above the same ceiling's fit; on raw counts the two end {{diff:E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_thrin0.5_k2:fit_apl_raw_rmse -- E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_base:apl_raw_rmse ~ .3f}} and {{diff:E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:fit_apl_raw_rmse -- E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2:apl_raw_rmse ~ .3f}} above. The reference model's set ends {{diff:E71/fits.csv:thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplboth_thrin0.75_k2:fit_apl_cal_rmse -- E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:cal_loss ~ .3f}} above its fit under the readout ({{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:cal_loss ~ .3f}}) and above the reference model as it stands; on raw counts it is about where the set's fit on raw counts is ({{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3:raw_loss ~ .3f}}). Under the readout the costs are two to four times the standard deviation of the breadth-2 family's loss over the eight wirings it has been fitted on ({{E72/spread.csv:readout=cal:sd_single ~ .3f}}), so they are not one wiring's accident, although the scaling itself was run on the real wiring only; on raw counts, where that standard deviation is {{E72/spread.csv:readout=raw:sd_single ~ .3f}}, the two costs are little more than one. The fits reach the mean effect with less KC–KC inhibition, all but off in the breadth-2 family and in the reference model's set, with a higher common threshold in its place (last row), and with more KCs responding with APL off: the response rate with APL off is {{E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_thrin0.5_k2:rate_ratio_off_over_on ~ .2f}}, {{E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:rate_ratio_off_over_on ~ .2f}} and {{E71/fits.csv:thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplboth_thrin0.75_k2:rate_ratio_off_over_on ~ .2f}} times that with APL on in the three fits, in the order of the table, from {{E69/stats_variants.csv:e67cal_base:rate_ratio_off_over_on ~ .2f}}, {{E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2:rate_ratio_off_over_on ~ .2f}} and {{E69/stats_variants.csv:ref40_base:rate_ratio_off_over_on ~ .2f}} without the scaling (the flies' {{data:rate_ratio_fraction ~ .2f}}, 90 % interval {{data:rate_ratio_fraction.lo ~ .2f}} to {{data:rate_ratio_fraction.hi ~ .2f}}), which by itself raises the mean effect; the ratio was not held in the fits. With the scaling the block on raw counts is about two thirds of the flies' in the breadth-2 family and half in the other two, the natural-source alignment with APL on is lower than without the scaling in all three, and ρ on odor-odor distances is lower in the two families of the first joint fit and about unchanged in the reference model's set.

**The release stays off the many-claw KCs, and the broad responders come back from other cells.** In all three fits the KCs with nine or more claws take a fifth or less of the responses that removing APL adds, where they took a third (the row on who is released), and the spread of the number of odors per KC with APL off comes inside the recordings' range (the row under it). But between {{E71/fits.csv:thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplboth_thrin0.75_k2:fit_opk_off_8plus ~ .0%}} and {{E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:fit_opk_off_8plus ~ .0%}} of the KCs again respond to eight or more odors with APL off, about as many as without the scaling and against the flies' {{data:tnt_odors_per_kc_8plus ~ .0%}}, and with the power at 0.5 or 0.75 they are now KCs with few claws: the KCs of the breadth-2 family that respond to eight or more odors with APL off average {{lit:5.0 # computed from the archived response matrices of this fit (data/model_outputs/E71_input_scaled_threshold/, tag rate0.068_aplboth_thrin0.5_k2) and the claw counts of data/model_outputs/inputs/wPNKC_bouton_base.parquet, as release_stats in E71_input_scaled_threshold.py does for part A (the same computation reproduces part A's cells of input_readouts.csv); unrounded 4.96; not in a results table}} claws, from {{E70/opk_features.csv:key=e67__rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3;feature=n_claws:mean_8plus_off ~ .1f}} without the scaling, and those of the reference model's set {{lit:5.1 # computed from the archived response matrices of this fit (data/model_outputs/E71_input_scaled_threshold/, tag thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplboth_thrin0.75_k2) and the claw counts of data/model_outputs/inputs/wPNKC_bouton_base.parquet, as release_stats in E71_input_scaled_threshold.py does for part A (the same computation reproduces part A's cells of input_readouts.csv); unrounded 5.09; not in a results table}}, from {{E70/opk_features.csv:key=ref40;feature=n_claws:mean_8plus_off ~ .1f}}. The binarized mean APL effect returns with them, to between {{E71/fits.csv:thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplboth_thrin0.75_k2:responses_cosine__apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}} and {{E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:responses_cosine__apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}} against the flies' {{data:apl_effect_binary_mean ~ +.3f}}, where the settings at fixed scales had brought it to between {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_a0.5:apl_bin_mean ~ +.3f}} and {{E71/input_readouts.csv:ref40_a0.75:apl_bin_mean ~ +.3f}}. The breadth-2 family's distance from the flies' distribution of odors per KC with APL off is therefore back where it was (sixth row), with other cells as the broad responders. The other two families also leave fewer KCs silent and stay well inside the band, the reference model's set for the first time on the real wiring: neither its fits under the APL-effect loss ({{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplraw_k2_cap3:opk_off_dist ~ .3f}} and {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:opk_off_dist ~ .3f}} from the flies' distribution) nor its fits with a firing ceiling ({{E69/stats_variants.csv:thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.1114t1000_k2:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} and {{E69/stats_variants.csv:thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_aplbothopk_refr3_adapt0.1114t1000_k2:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}}) had been inside it. In this family the flies' graded mean effect is reached through a response-set term that overshoots, carried by KCs that turn into broad responders when APL is removed; the scaling changes which cells those are, and a fit of the main target makes them again. The flies' own effect is not of another kind. Split by the number of odors each KC responds to ([the APL effect split by odors per KC](#apl-effect-by-class), the last subsection of this section), it also rests on KCs that respond broadly when APL output is blocked, and the third of the mean effect that comes from products with values below the response criterion ([above](#readout)) is those KCs' weaker responses to their other odors. The difference is that the flies have two kinds of such KCs, a few that respond to eight or more odors and many that respond to four to seven, where the models have the first kind alone: what a model lacks is the contribution of the moderately broad KCs, not a route through amplitudes in place of broad responders.

**Read as the recordings are, the scaling is nearly free in the family with the firing ceiling.** The experiment on the per-KC normalization of the recordings, still running, reads every saved model as the KC data are produced: each KC's signal is put in units of its own standard deviation over the whole recording, and an odor's response is taken relative to the frames before it, so that a KC that responds to many odors has every one of its responses scaled down. Read that way (the two rows of the second table that carry this reading) the breadth-2 family and the reference model's set lose as much with the scaling as under the calibrated readout, or more; under the recordings' own response criterion the reference model is itself inside the band on odors per KC with APL off, and its set with the scaling is further inside. The family with the firing ceiling loses {{diff:E73/by_variant.csv:folder=E71_input_scaled_threshold;tag=rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2;reading_def=zcell_saturating;reading=graded:rmse -- E73/by_variant.csv:folder=E69_kc_firing_ceiling;tag=rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2;reading_def=zcell_saturating;reading=graded:rmse ~ .3f}}, a small part of what the loss varies by over wirings under the same reading ({{E73/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed3_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3;reading_def=zcell_saturating;reading=graded:rmse ~ .3f}} to {{E73/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3;reading_def=zcell_saturating;reading=graded:rmse ~ .3f}} over the seven re-drawn wirings of the breadth-2 family, fitted without a ceiling), and its distance from the flies' distribution of odors per KC with APL off is the smallest in the table. If that reading becomes the definition of the model's side of the target, the firing ceiling together with the input-scaled threshold at a small power is the combination to fit under it. That fit has been made ([the APL effect split by odors per KC](#apl-effect-by-class)): under the reading it ends level with the fit without the scaling, but it gets there by weakening APL and KC–KC inhibition until its response rate at two or more spikes with APL on is {{E74/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:rate_on ~ .3f}}, twice the flies' response rate and outside the band, so it is a measurement and not a candidate base; the question is open again with the fits' operating point held on two spikes.

**A single pseudo-recording of the breadth-2 fit is closer to the data's mean than a single pair of recordings is.** The comparison that [the high-firing KCs that survive APL](#high-firing-kcs) left open was made here, the trial-noise pipeline now saving every pseudo-recording. One pseudo-recording with APL on paired with one with APL off (three trials each through [the matched trial noise](#fn-trial-noise), {{E71/partC_summary.csv:reading=matched_each:n ~ .0f}} pairs), scored against the KC data's mean per-pair effect as a model is, has a median APL-effect loss of {{E71/partC_summary.csv:reading=matched_each:rmse_median ~ .3f}} ({{E71/partC_summary.csv:reading=matched_each:rmse_min ~ .3f}} to {{E71/partC_summary.csv:reading=matched_each:rmse_max ~ .3f}}) and a median mean effect of {{E71/partC_summary.csv:reading=matched_each:mean_median ~ +.3f}}. One control recording paired with one recording with APL output blocked (TNT), scored against the mean of the remaining recordings, has {{E71/partC_summary.csv:model=KC data (E70 yardstick):rmse_median ~ .3f}} ({{E71/partC_summary.csv:model=KC data (E70 yardstick):rmse_min ~ .3f}} to {{E71/partC_summary.csv:model=KC data (E70 yardstick):rmse_max ~ .3f}}) and {{E71/partC_summary.csv:model=KC data (E70 yardstick):mean_median ~ +.3f}}. The model's pairs vary far less than the flies': they have the trial noise, but one wiring and no differences between flies. The best fit on one wiring is therefore already as close to the data's mean as a single pair of recordings is, and what separates it from the floor is no more than single recordings show. Averaged over four wirings and read through the same noise, as the data's mean is an average over flies and trials, the breadth-2 family has a loss on raw counts of {{E72/partE_noise.csv:group=population;level=match_on;transform=graded;distances=raw:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} with the noise matched to the control flies and {{E72/partE_noise.csv:group=population;level=match_off;transform=graded;distances=raw:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} with the noise matched to APL output blocked (TNT), against {{E71/input_readouts.csv:c2_base:apl_raw_rmse ~ .3f}} for the one wiring without noise ([the APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings](#wiring-population), the next subsection). The fit with the scaling was not put through the noise, being worse than the fit without it under both definitions.

**What the scaling gains is realism, and what it costs is the main target.** Every setting kept and every fit with the scaling is inside all four realism bands, the reference model's set is inside the band on odors per KC with APL off for the first time on the real wiring, and the spread of the number of odors per KC with APL off is inside the recordings' range in all three fits, which none of the three families is without the scaling. The fit of the reference model's set has a common somatic threshold and APL scales within about a fifth of the baseline's and KC–KC inhibition all but off (last row of the second table). The fit with the firing ceiling keeps a common somatic threshold under half the baseline's, as the breadth-2 fit has, which is hard to defend, and at a power of 0.5 the scaling itself asks for thresholds from {{E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_thrin0.5_k2:kc_thr_input_factor_min ~ .2f}} to {{E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_thrin0.5_k2:kc_thr_input_factor_max ~ .2f}} times the common value; whether the thresholds of KCs vary that much with their claw input is a question for the physiology. Without a firing ceiling the scaling at fixed scales makes few-claw KCs the highest-firing cells of the breadth-2 fit (the row of largest spike counts in the first table), which the fits undo (the same row of the second). Each fit is a local search from one start on the real wiring, the scaling has one functional form, a power of the summed claw weight, and as everywhere the pairs with paa, euc and -aPine rest on [ORN input at another concentration](#fn-concentrations).

**What follows.** The input-scaled threshold does not join the base model under the present definitions of the target: it puts the release with APL off on other cells, as the flies' numbers ask, at two to four times what a draw of the wiring moves the loss under the readout. Its fit of the reference model's set is kept as that set's realism reference, not as a best model. The best-models table is to be read with this result: the columns that match the flies' mean effect, under the readout or with the firing ceiling, do it with more broadly responding KCs with APL off than the flies have, their many-claw KCs, and when the release is moved off those cells a fit of the main target makes as many broad responders from others. What is taken up from here is with the pending work at the end of the page.

### The APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings: the model as a population of flies {#wiring-population}

**The breadth-2 APL-effect fit owes its standing to its family and not to the hemibrain wiring: fitted again on seven re-drawn wirings it ends around the same loss. One wiring's loss carries a draw term as large as the margins between recent fits, and the mean over wirings, the counterpart of data that are a mean over flies, has a lower loss, the whole gain in the pattern part. What is left is the same on every wiring: too little APL effect among the esters, where the flies have the block.** Every loss by which the models above are ranked is one wiring's. A model has the hemibrain wiring with APL on and with APL off, while the data's per-pair APL effect is the mean over {{data:n_recordings_ctrl ~ .0f}} control recordings minus the mean over {{data:n_recordings_tnt ~ .0f}} recordings with APL output blocked (TNT), from different flies, so that what is particular to one fly's wiring is averaged out of the data and kept whole in the model. The APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings fitted the best family of [the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits) (breadth exponent 2, no per-claw threshold, no glomerulus gain; both definitions of the model's side in the loss, which on the real wiring scores as the fit under the readout does) again on seven re-drawn wirings, each from the real wiring's fitted parameters: four within-type claw permutations, and three shuffles matched to the real wiring on every glomerulus pair's count of shared KCs ([the wiring experiments](#wiring-feature)). The model's counterpart of the data's mean over flies is a mean over wirings: the pair distances are averaged over the wirings in each condition, and the APL effect and its loss are taken from the averages.

![The APL-effect loss of the breadth-2 family fitted on each of eight wirings, under the calibrated readout (left) and on raw spike counts (right): the real wiring (black), four within-type claw permutations (P, blue) and three shuffles matched to the real wiring on every glomerulus pair's count of shared KCs (M, orange). Solid line: the mean over the eight wirings. Dashed and dash-dotted lines: one shared parameter set, as the mean over the four wirings it was fitted to and over the four it never saw. Green diamonds: the same family with a 40 Hz per-claw threshold, fitted on the same wiring. Dotted line: the floor set by the recordings' own variability.](results/E72_wiring_population/E72_wirings.png)

The breadth-2 family on the real wiring, as the mean over all eight wirings, and on the four wirings held out of the fit of one shared parameter set (two claw permutations and two matched shuffles), each of those four with its own fit and with the shared set, nothing fitted. Two values without named conditions are on raw spike counts / through the calibrated saturating readout, its half-saturation set on each wiring's own APL-on counts. A mean over wirings averages the pair distances over the wirings in each condition before the APL effect is taken, and the response statistics over the wirings

**The standing is the family's, and one wiring is a noisy reading of it.** The eight wirings end between {{E72/spread.csv:readout=cal:min_single ~ .3f}} and {{E72/spread.csv:readout=cal:max_single ~ .3f}} under the readout, the real wiring ({{E72/spread.csv:readout=cal:real_single ~ .3f}}) in the middle, and between {{E72/spread.csv:readout=raw:min_single ~ .3f}} and {{E72/spread.csv:readout=raw:max_single ~ .3f}} on raw counts, where the real wiring ({{E72/spread.csv:readout=raw:real_single ~ .3f}}) is among the higher ones (the figure, and the first column of the second table). The hemibrain's exact wiring holds no special share of the match, so the family is the model to build on. The standard deviation of the loss over the eight wirings, the draw term in what follows, is {{E72/spread.csv:readout=cal:sd_single ~ .3f}} under the readout and {{E72/spread.csv:readout=raw:sd_single ~ .3f}} on raw counts, as large as the margins by which the recent fits were ranked on the real wiring. A difference of about that size between two fits on one wiring does not tell them apart: such comparisons need both fits on the same re-drawn wirings, as the two comparisons further down were made.

**Averaged over wirings the loss is lower, and the gain is in the pattern.** Under the readout one wiring scores {{E72/curve.csv:n=1;readout=cal;stat=loss:mean ~ .3f}} on average, two wirings averaged {{E72/curve.csv:n=2;readout=cal;stat=loss:mean ~ .3f}}, four {{E72/curve.csv:n=4;readout=cal;stat=loss:mean ~ .3f}} and all eight {{E72/curve.csv:n=8;readout=cal;stat=loss:mean ~ .3f}}, which only one of the single wirings is below; on raw counts the same steps are {{E72/curve.csv:n=1;readout=raw;stat=loss:mean ~ .3f}}, {{E72/curve.csv:n=2;readout=raw;stat=loss:mean ~ .3f}}, {{E72/curve.csv:n=4;readout=raw;stat=loss:mean ~ .3f}} and {{E72/curve.csv:n=8;readout=raw;stat=loss:mean ~ .3f}}, below every single wiring. Most of the gain comes with the first few wirings. The mean APL effect does not change with the averaging, so the whole gain is in the pattern part, and most of it in the pair-specific part: under the readout that part falls from {{E72/curve.csv:n=1;readout=cal;stat=pair_specific:mean ~ .3f}} for one wiring to {{E72/curve.csv:n=8;readout=cal;stat=pair_specific:mean ~ .3f}} for the eight and the per-odor part from {{E72/curve.csv:n=1;readout=cal;stat=per_odor:mean ~ .3f}} to {{E72/curve.csv:n=8;readout=cal;stat=per_odor:mean ~ .3f}}, and ρ on the per-pair APL effect rises (fifth row). The data's mean is also a mean over trials. Four of the wirings (the real one, two claw permutations and one matched shuffle) read through [the matched trial noise](#fn-trial-noise) and then averaged have a loss on raw counts of {{E72/partE_noise.csv:group=population;level=match_on;transform=graded;distances=raw:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} with the noise matched to the control flies and {{E72/partE_noise.csv:group=population;level=match_off;transform=graded;distances=raw:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} with the noise matched to APL output blocked (TNT), against {{E72/partE_noise.csv:group=population;level=match_on;transform=graded;distances=det:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} for the same four without noise and {{E72/spread.csv:readout=raw:real_single ~ .3f}} for the real wiring alone, the number the family had been judged by (last column of the second table). The two steps add: the noise raises the mean effect (from {{E72/partE_noise.csv:group=population;level=match_on;transform=graded;distances=det:apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}} to {{E72/partE_noise.csv:group=population;level=match_on;transform=graded;distances=raw:apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}} and {{E72/partE_noise.csv:group=population;level=match_off;transform=graded;distances=raw:apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}} at the two noise levels; the flies' {{data:apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}}) and the averaging lowers the pattern part. The mean over wirings is still not the data's design in one respect: each model wiring has both conditions, while the flies with APL on and those with APL off are different animals, and APL on from one wiring paired with APL off from another scores worse than a single wiring does.

![Left: the error common to the eight wirings, per pair: the per-pair APL effect of the mean over the wirings minus the KC data's, both means removed, under the calibrated readout (blue: the model's effect is too small; red: too large). Right: the correlation over the 120 pairs between each wiring's per-pair error and the common error.](results/E72_wiring_population/E72_common_error.png)

**What remains is common to the wirings, and it sits where the block is.** The pattern error of the mean over the eight wirings is what every wiring shares, and each wiring adds a smaller remainder of its own. Under the readout the per-pair errors of any two wirings correlate between {{E72/error_corr_cal.csv:P1:P3 ~ .2f}} and {{E72/error_corr_cal.csv:M2:M3 ~ .2f}} over the 120 pairs (on raw counts between {{E72/error_corr_raw.csv:P3:P4 ~ .2f}} and {{E72/error_corr_raw.csv:real:M1 ~ .2f}}). In the common error the model's APL effect is too small on every pair among 2h, IaA, pa, eb, ep and ms (the largest, 2h with IaA, by {{E72/common_error_pairs.csv:odor_a=2h;odor_b=IaA:abs_common ~ .3f}} once the means are removed) and on eb with 1-8ol and 1-6ol, and too large on every pair among 1-8ol, 1-5ol, 1-6ol and 6al. These are the pairs of the block and around it: under the readout the flies' APL separates these esters from one another more than the model's does on every one of the eight wirings. The block itself is smaller in the mean over wirings than on the real wiring (sixth row of the first table), whose block is the second largest of the eight; six of the seven re-drawn wirings have less, as shuffled wirings had in [the wiring experiments](#wiring-feature). No draw of the wiring removes this error, and averaging over wirings cannot: it is what a further mechanism has to supply.

**One parameter set serves every wiring.** One set of the four fitted parameters (the somatic threshold, the APL→KC and APL→bouton scales and the KC–KC scale) was fitted to the mean over four wirings (the real one, two claw permutations and one matched shuffle) and then run, nothing fitted, on the other four. On the wirings it never saw it costs {{diff:E72/partD_populations.csv:group=held-out wirings;variant=one shared set:raw_loss -- E72/partD_populations.csv:group=held-out wirings;variant=each wiring fitted alone:raw_loss ~ .3f}} on raw counts and {{diff:E72/partD_populations.csv:group=held-out wirings;variant=one shared set:cal_loss -- E72/partD_populations.csv:group=held-out wirings;variant=each wiring fitted alone:cal_loss ~ .3f}} under the readout against a fit of each wiring (last two columns of the first table), and on the wirings it was fitted to {{diff:E72/partD_populations.csv:group=fitted wirings;variant=one shared set:raw_loss -- E72/partD_populations.csv:group=fitted wirings;variant=each wiring fitted alone:raw_loss ~ .3f}} and {{diff:E72/partD_populations.csv:group=fitted wirings;variant=one shared set:cal_loss -- E72/partD_populations.csv:group=fitted wirings;variant=each wiring fitted alone:cal_loss ~ .3f}}: less than half of what one draw of the wiring moves the loss, inside all four realism bands. Three of its parameters lie inside the range of the eight per-wiring fits and the APL→bouton scale just above it. The spread of the per-wiring fits, the KC–KC scale from {{E72/stats_variants.csv:wiring_label=P2:kc_kc_inh ~ .0f}} to {{E72/stats_variants.csv:wiring_label=P4:kc_kc_inh ~ .0f}} and the somatic threshold from {{E72/stats_variants.csv:wiring_label=P3:thr_factor ~ .2f}} to {{E72/stats_variants.csv:wiring_label=M2:thr_factor ~ .2f}} of the baseline's, is therefore how far a parameter can move on one wiring at nearly the same loss, not a different physiology for each wiring.

The APL-effect loss of the family on each wiring, on raw spike counts / through the calibrated saturating readout (lower is better): as fitted above, with a per-claw threshold of 40 Hz, and with the firing ceiling at its literature setting, each fitted on the same wiring and beside its difference from the fit without it. Last column: the fits of the first column on raw counts through the matched trial noise, the noise matched to the control flies / to APL output blocked (TNT). P and M are the claw permutations and the matched shuffles of the figure; a mean over wirings is the loss of the mean over those wirings

**The per-claw threshold costs on every wiring.** Among [the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits) the family's lead needed the per-claw threshold absent, on the real wiring. The same family with a 40 Hz per-claw threshold, fitted on the four claw permutations as well, has a higher loss than the family without it on all five wirings and under both definitions, on each by more than the draw term, and in the mean over the five (second and third columns; the 40 Hz fits had the readout alone in their loss, so the like-for-like difference is the one under the readout). What the threshold buys is the number of odors per KC, which moves toward the flies' in both conditions: in the mean over the five wirings the distance from the flies' distribution is {{E72/partC_population.csv:family=40:opk_on_dist ~ .3f}} with APL on and {{E72/partC_population.csv:family=40:opk_off_dist ~ .3f}} with APL off, against {{E72/partC_population.csv:family=none:opk_on_dist ~ .3f}} and {{E72/partC_population.csv:family=none:opk_off_dist ~ .3f}} without it. It comes with a lower response rate ρ with APL on ({{E72/partC_population.csv:family=40:rate_rho_on ~ .2f}} against {{E72/partC_population.csv:family=none:rate_rho_on ~ .2f}}) and without the block on raw counts ({{E72/partC_population.csv:family=40:raw_block ~ +.3f}} against {{E72/partC_population.csv:family=none:raw_block ~ +.3f}}). Read as the recordings are produced (the per-KC normalization of the recordings, still running), the cost is less uniform: the threshold costs on four of the five wirings, by up to {{diff:E73/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=thr40_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed3_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3;reading_def=zcell_saturating;reading=graded:rmse -- E73/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed3_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3;reading_def=zcell_saturating;reading=graded:rmse ~ .3f}}, and on one the fit with it is lower by {{diff:E73/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3;reading_def=zcell_saturating;reading=graded:rmse -- E73/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=thr40_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3;reading_def=zcell_saturating;reading=graded:rmse ~ .3f}}.

**The firing ceiling's gain on raw counts holds on every wiring.** [The firing ceiling for the KCs](#firing-ceiling) had been fitted on the real wiring only. Fitted at its literature setting on three of the re-drawn wirings, each from that wiring's own fit, it leaves the loss under the readout where it was, to within the draw term, and lowers the loss on raw counts by about twice the draw term or more, on all four wirings, so that raw counts and the readout give nearly the same scores on each (fourth and fifth columns). The gain is the family's and not the hemibrain wiring's. The mean over the four wirings with the ceiling scores on raw counts what it scores under the readout, and without any noise is below the mean of the same four without a ceiling read through the trial noise (last row). As on the real wiring, the fits with the ceiling end with more KCs responding with APL off (a response rate {{E72/partG_population.csv:family=40:ratio ~ .2f}} times that with APL on in the mean over the four, from {{E72/partG_population.csv:family=none:ratio ~ .2f}}; the flies' {{data:rate_ratio_fraction ~ .2f}}), which by itself raises the mean effect, and with about half the block on raw counts ({{E72/partG_population.csv:family=40:raw_block ~ +.3f}} against {{E72/partG_population.csv:family=none:raw_block ~ +.3f}}).

Response fractions at low concentration: the breadth-2 APL-effect fit on the real wiring at its fitted parameters, without noise and through its matched trial noise (a response there being a mean over three trials of at least one spike, as the recordings are scored), and the same family with a 40 Hz per-claw threshold, without noise. The ORN input of ma @ -7 and 2h @ -5 is at the recorded concentration; 2h @ -7 and farn @ -3 are run with the input at ten times the recorded concentration
