**Without a per-claw threshold the family responds to the weakest input.** The models with a per-claw threshold recruited a half to a tenth of the KCs the flies recruit at low concentration, and almost none for ma, whose ORN input is at the recorded concentration ([the per-claw threshold at low concentration](#low-concentration)). The breadth-2 fit, run on the same odors at its fitted parameters, recruits KCs for ma: about a third of the control flies' fraction without noise and half through the matched trial noise, three times what the same family has with the 40 Hz per-claw threshold, and with APL off about half of the fraction of the flies with APL output blocked (TNT) without noise and three quarters through it. For 2h at 10^-5, the other odor with input at the recorded concentration, it has about half of the flies' fraction without noise and the flies' fraction through the noise. For 2h at 10^-7 and farn at 10^-3, whose input is from a concentration ten times higher, it stays at a third to a half of the flies' fractions without noise, no better than the best of the models with a per-claw threshold. The silence at the weakest input therefore belonged to the per-claw threshold, and neither the match to the 16-odor panel nor a response at low concentration needs one. What is left there is a shortfall of recruitment, by a factor of two to three for ma, which a change to the per-claw transfer would have to make up by adding drive at low input, not by softening a threshold.

**Nothing here is bought with realism, and what the bands do not see is the same on every wiring.** Every fit of the experiment, on every wiring, and every mean over wirings is inside all four realism bands (the lower rows of the first table): averaging over wirings changes the comparison and not the model, and the shared parameter set is as realistic as the per-wiring fits. Beyond the bands the wirings agree with the real one. On each of the eight the KCs with nine or more claws take between a quarter and a third of the responses that removing APL adds, the number of odors per KC with APL off is spread more widely than in any recording ([the input-scaled KC threshold](#input-scaled-threshold)), and more KCs stay silent and more respond to eight or more odors than in the flies (last row of the first table). The somatic threshold is under half the baseline's on every wiring and the APL→bouton scale near its bound, which are hard to defend, and as everywhere the pairs with paa, euc and -aPine rest on [ORN input at another concentration](#fn-concentrations). The release through the many-claw KCs is thus the family's and survives every draw, and a stricter reading of realism, one that also held the spread of the number of odors per KC to the recordings' range, would put every one of the eight wirings outside. What is taken up from here is with the pending work at the end of the page.

### Which KCs carry the APL effect: the effect split by the number of odors each KC responds to {#apl-effect-by-class}

**Split by how many of the 16 odors a KC responds to, the flies' APL effect rests on broadly responding KCs of two kinds, the few that respond to eight or more odors when APL output is blocked and the many that respond to four to seven. The models make theirs with the first kind alone, in every best fit and on every wiring. The difference is real, and it is not where the pattern error is: across the saved models a split closer to the flies' does not go with a lower pattern part, and a fit that is asked for the flies' split pays for it on the total.** [The input-scaled KC threshold](#input-scaled-threshold) left open which KCs carry the effect, in the flies and in the models. A pair's cosine similarity is a sum over KCs (the sum that [the low end of the readout](#readout) divided by whether its two entries are responses), so the per-pair APL effect can be divided among groups of KCs. The APL effect split by odors per KC put every KC, in each condition, in one of the five groups of the realism band on odors per KC, by the number of the 16 odors it responds to in that condition (none, one, two or three, four to seven, eight or more; one to three are shown together below), and took each group's part of every pair's similarity. A class's APL effect is its part of the similarity with APL off minus its part with APL on, and the classes' effects add up to the pair's APL effect. A KC can change class between the conditions, so the split says how much of each condition's similarity sits in KCs of a given tuning width; it needs no pairing of cells between the conditions, which the recordings cannot give, the control flies and the flies with APL output blocked (TNT) being different animals. In the recordings a response is a trial mean above 1 z, each genotype's class parts are averaged over its recordings, and the 90 % intervals are over recordings drawn again with replacement. Every archived model with both conditions saved ({{E74/rank_correlations.csv:reading=zcell;subset=all variants:n ~ .0f}} variants) was read in two ways: through [the calibrated readout](#fn-calibrated-readout) with a response at two or more spikes, and as the recordings are produced, each KC in units of its own standard deviation over the session (the reading introduced [above](#input-scaled-threshold), the per-KC reading from here on), with the recordings' criterion and through that reading's measurement noise, as ten pseudo-recordings of three trials, so that a model KC enters a class as a recorded KC does.

The per-pair APL effect of the KC data split by the number of odors a KC responds to in each condition. A class's share of the variance is the covariance of its effect with the whole effect across the 120 pairs over the variance of the whole effect, so the shares add up to 1; the similarity a class carries is its part of the cosine similarity between two odors, averaged over the 120 pairs

**The flies' APL effect rests on broadly responding KCs of two kinds.** The KCs that respond to eight or more odors, {{data:tnt_odors_per_kc_8plus ~ .1%}} of the KCs with APL output blocked (TNT) and almost none in the control flies, carry most of the mean effect and about half of its variance across pairs. Those that respond to four to seven odors, {{data:tnt_odors_per_kc_4_7 ~ .1%}} of the KCs with APL output blocked (TNT) against {{data:ctrl_odors_per_kc_4_7 ~ .1%}} in the control flies, carry the rest and a third of the variance. The KCs that respond to one to three odors, two fifths of the KCs in either genotype, add nothing, and those that respond to none take a little away. The third of the mean effect that [the low end of the readout](#readout) found in products of a responder with a sub-threshold entry sits in the same broad KCs (third row): it is their weaker responses to their other odors, not the responses of weakly responding cells. With the response criterion at 0.75 or 1.25 z in place of 1 z the class at four to seven carries {{data:apl_class_effect_4_7_z0.75 ~ +.3f}} or {{data:apl_class_effect_4_7_z1.25 ~ +.3f}} and the class at eight or more {{data:apl_class_effect_8plus_z0.75 ~ +.3f}} or {{data:apl_class_effect_8plus_z1.25 ~ +.3f}}: how the effect divides between the two kinds moves with the criterion and neither changes sign, so a model is compared with the flies at a matched criterion only. The flies therefore do not make the effect through the amplitudes of weak responses in place of broad responders, as the input-scaled KC threshold had read them: flies and models both make it through broad responders, the flies through two kinds.

A model's split is scored by the class-resolved loss: the root mean square, over the five classes and the 120 pairs, of the model's per-class effect minus the flies' (lower is better). Its floor, a resampled set of recordings scored against the measured one, is {{data:apl_class_rmse_floor ~ .3f}} ({{data:apl_class_rmse_floor.lo ~ .3f}} to {{data:apl_class_rmse_floor.hi ~ .3f}}), and one control recording paired with one recording with APL output blocked (TNT), scored against the mean of the others, has a median of {{data:apl_class_rmse_yardstick ~ .3f}}.

**Every best fit lacks the middle class, on every wiring.** In every model of the table but the one with the input-scaled threshold, the KCs at four to seven odors carry between a quarter and three fifths of what they carry in the flies, below the lower end of the data's interval; the KCs at eight or more odors carry the model's whole effect and more, and the KCs at one to three odors take away from it, where in the flies they add nothing. About half of the variance of the fits' pattern error across the pairs is in the class at four to seven, less in the reference model (the row on the pattern error, and the right panel of the figure). Through the calibrated readout, with a response at two or more spikes, the same holds more strongly: in the breadth-2 fit the class at four to seven has an effect of {{E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3;reading=cal2:eff_4_7 ~ +.3f}} and the class at eight or more {{E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3;reading=cal2:eff_8plus ~ +.3f}}, at a class-resolved loss of {{E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3;reading=cal2:class_rmse ~ .3f}} (left panel of the figure). The shortfall belongs to the family and not to the hemibrain wiring: fitted on each of the eight wirings of [the APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings](#wiring-population), the breadth-2 family has the class at four to seven between {{E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-curveball_within_type_matched-seed1_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3;reading=zcell:eff_4_7 ~ +.3f}} and {{E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3;reading=zcell:eff_4_7 ~ +.3f}} under the per-KC reading, and at {{E74/wirings.csv:group=c2;reading=zcell:eff_4_7 ~ +.3f}} in the mean over the wirings.

**The models' moderately broad KCs are too broad already with APL on.** Under the per-KC reading the similarity that the class at four to seven carries with APL off is within a tenth of the flies' in every model of the table but the one with the input-scaled threshold, and with APL on it is two fifths to three fifths above the control flies', whose 90 % interval is {{E74/data_by_class.csv:criterion=1.0;quantity=level_on;class=4_7;term=total:lo ~ .3f}} to {{E74/data_by_class.csv:criterion=1.0;quantity=level_on;class=4_7;term=total:hi ~ .3f}} (the row on the similarity carried). The class's shortfall in the APL effect is thus an excess with APL on: the KCs that respond to four to seven odors while APL is working carry more of the overlap between odors in the models than in the flies, so that removing APL adds less to them. The class at one to three odors is over with APL on in the same way. Through the calibrated readout the excess with APL on is there as well, and the class is short with APL off besides.

**A split closer to the flies' does not go with a lower pattern part.** If the missing class were what the pattern part of the APL-effect loss is made of, the archived models with a split nearer the flies' would be the ones with a lower pattern part. They are not: across the {{E74/rank_correlations.csv:reading=zcell;subset=all variants:n ~ .0f}} variants the Spearman correlation between the class-resolved loss and the pattern part is {{E74/rank_correlations.csv:reading=cal2;subset=all variants:rho_class_vs_pattern ~ +.2f}} through the calibrated readout and {{E74/rank_correlations.csv:reading=zcell;subset=all variants:rho_class_vs_pattern ~ +.2f}} under the per-KC reading, and across the {{E74/rank_correlations.csv:reading=zcell;subset=inside the two-spike bands:n ~ .0f}} of them inside the realism bands {{E74/rank_correlations.csv:reading=cal2;subset=inside the two-spike bands:rho_class_vs_pattern ~ +.2f}} and {{E74/rank_correlations.csv:reading=zcell;subset=inside the two-spike bands:rho_class_vs_pattern ~ +.2f}}. In the whole archive, under the per-KC reading, the class at four to seven reaches the flies' interval only in models with a per-claw threshold, the claw coincidence rule or the input-scaled KC threshold, and where such a model can be set beside the same family without it, the pattern does not come with the class. The breadth-2 family with a 40 Hz per-claw threshold was fitted on the same five wirings as the family without it ([above](#wiring-population)). Under the per-KC reading it has the class at four to seven higher on every one of them, between {{E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=thr40_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3;reading=zcell:eff_4_7 ~ +.3f}} and {{E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=thr40_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed4_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3;reading=zcell:eff_4_7 ~ +.3f}} against {{E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed1_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3;reading=zcell:eff_4_7 ~ +.3f}} to {{E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed2_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3;reading=zcell:eff_4_7 ~ +.3f}}, through less of the class's similarity with APL on, and a higher pattern part on every one, between {{E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=thr40_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed3_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3;reading=zcell:apl_pattern ~ .3f}} and {{E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=thr40_wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed1_rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3;reading=zcell:apl_pattern ~ .3f}} against {{E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed3_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3;reading=zcell:apl_pattern ~ .3f}} to {{E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E72_wiring_population;tag=wiring-claw_permutation_within_type-seed1_rate0.068_aplboth_k2_cap3;reading=zcell:apl_pattern ~ .3f}}.

**Asked for the flies' split, the family pays on the total.** The family with the firing ceiling was fitted again from its optimum under the per-KC reading (third model column) with the class-resolved loss added to its loss, weighted so that a fall in it counts about as much as an equal rise in the APL-effect loss (fourth column). The class-resolved loss falls by a fifth, mostly because the class at eight or more odors comes down toward the flies'; the class at four to seven hardly moves, the mean APL effect moves away from the flies', the APL-effect loss rises by {{diff:E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E74_apl_effect_by_odors_per_kc;tag=zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcellclslin_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2_cap3;reading=zcell:apl_rmse -- E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E73_per_kc_normalization;tag=zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2_cap3;reading=zcell:apl_rmse ~ .3f}} and its pattern part stays where it was. No evaluation along the fit lowers the class-resolved loss without raising the APL-effect loss. The family's good totals rest on class errors of opposite sign, too much effect in the KCs at eight or more odors and a loss of effect in those at one to three, that cancel pair by pair, and moving one class toward the flies' unbalances the other. The rise is about what one draw of the wiring moves the loss ({{E72/spread.csv:readout=cal:sd_single ~ .3f}} under the readout) and the fit was made on the real wiring only, so its size is a single reading; that the pattern part does not fall is what the archive says as well.

**With the input-scaled threshold the family reaches the middle class only by leaving its operating point.** The same experiment made the fit that the input-scaled KC threshold had left as the one to try ([above](#input-scaled-threshold)): the family with the firing ceiling and the threshold scaled at a power of 0.25, fitted under the per-KC reading (fifth model column). Under that reading its APL-effect loss ends {{diff:E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E73_per_kc_normalization;tag=zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2_cap3;reading=zcell:apl_rmse -- E74/by_variant.csv:folder=E74_apl_effect_by_odors_per_kc;tag=zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2;reading=zcell:apl_rmse ~ .3f}} below the fit without the scaling, within a draw of the wiring; its class at four to seven is inside the flies' interval; the KCs with nine or more claws take {{E74/released_by_claws.csv:model=fit S;reading=zcell;group=9+:share_added ~ .0%}} of the responses that removing APL adds, where they take {{E74/released_by_claws.csv:model=reading ceiling;reading=zcell;group=9+:share_added ~ .0%}} without the scaling; and under the recordings' criterion its distribution of odors per KC with APL off is {{E74/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:fit_zcell_opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} from the flies', against {{E73/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2_cap3:fit_zcell_opk_off_tv ~ .3f}}. It gets there by leaving the model's operating point. In the fits under the per-KC reading the response-rate terms of the loss were computed under the recordings' criterion, and the reading is calibrated at every evaluation to the flies' response rate with APL on, so the number of KCs that fire was free. This fit used the freedom. It weakened the inhibition, APL→KC from {{E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:apl_factor_vs_e3 ~ .2f}} to {{E74/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:apl_factor_vs_e3 ~ .2f}} and APL→bouton from {{E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:aplpn_factor_vs_e3 ~ .2f}} to {{E74/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:aplpn_factor_vs_e3 ~ .2f}} times the baseline's and the KC–KC scale from {{E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:kc_kc_inh ~ .1f}} to {{E74/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:kc_kc_inh ~ .1f}}, at a common somatic threshold that stayed near {{E74/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:thr_factor ~ .2f}} of the baseline's, until its response rate at two or more spikes with APL on is {{E74/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:rate_on ~ .3f}}, from {{E71/fits.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:rate_on ~ .3f}} at its start and against a band of 0.045 to 0.085. Through the calibrated readout and on raw counts it is {{diff:E74/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:fit_apl_cal_rmse -- E73/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2_cap3:fit_apl_cal_rmse ~ .3f}} and {{diff:E74/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_thrin0.25_k2:fit_apl_raw_rmse -- E73/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcell_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2_cap3:fit_apl_raw_rmse ~ .3f}} worse than the fit without the scaling, its class-resolved loss is the highest of the table, and its class at four to seven is the difference between two levels that are both above the flies' (the row on the similarity carried). It is a measurement of what that freedom allows, not a candidate base. Fits now keep their response-rate terms on the two-spike criterion, and the per-KC reading is reported beside the realism bands without deciding them ([the footnote on baseline-noise units](#fn-baseline-noise-units)), so the fit that was asked for the flies' split, made under the same rule though inside the bands at its optimum, is likewise a measurement and not a candidate for the best-models table.

**Realism.** The split itself changes no model. Of the two fits made here, the one asked for the flies' split is inside all four bands at its optimum (the last two rows of the table), with a common somatic threshold of {{E74/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcellclslin_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2_cap3:thr_factor ~ .2f}} and an APL→bouton scale of {{E74/fits.csv:zcaplrate_rate0.068_aplzcellclslin_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2_cap3:aplpn_factor_vs_e3 ~ .2f}} times the baseline's, as hard to defend as in the fits it starts from; the one with the input-scaled threshold is outside three of the bands, as described. The only fit that reached the flies' middle class thus did it outside the bands, and the fit that was asked for the split stayed inside them and paid on the main target. The split counts the flies' responses by the recordings' own criterion of 1 z, and the models' under the per-KC reading by the same criterion, not in the [baseline-noise units](#fn-baseline-noise-units) in which the realism bands count the flies' responses; as everywhere the pairs with paa, euc and -aPine rest on [ORN input at another concentration](#fn-concentrations).

**What follows.** Which KCs carry the effect becomes a statistic of its own, reported for later fits beside the realism bands: the flies' effect sits in two classes of broadly responding KCs, and a model that has the total right with one of them has it by cancellation. It is not a route to a lower pattern part. What is left of the pattern is looked for pair by pair, in the errors that remain whatever the classes do, and the class at four to seven waits for a mechanism that makes moderately broad KCs less broad with APL on at no cost on the total, which nothing in the archive does. What is taken up from here is with the pending work at the end of the page.

## How close the best models have come to the KC data, over the run

The plot below tracks, for nine of the statistics used throughout this page, how far the best model found so far had closed the gap between the first measured model and the KC data, against the time each experiment finished. Two of the lines are the main target: the APL-effect loss, for which lower is better and 100 % is the data's floor ({{data:apl_effect_rmse_floor ~ .3f}}, the loss a model with the flies' true effect would still show, not zero), and ρ on the per-pair APL effect (ceiling {{data:apl_effect_rho_ceiling ~ .2f}}). These two lines count only variants inside the realism bands (a response rate with APL on of 0.045 to 0.085, an APL-off / APL-on response rate ratio of 1.6 to 2.9, a response rate ρ with APL on of at least 0.37, and a distribution of the number of odors each KC responds to within {{data:ctrl_odors_per_kc_tv_max ~ .2f}} of the control flies' with APL on and within {{data:tnt_odors_per_kc_tv_max ~ .2f}} of that of the flies with APL output blocked (TNT) with APL off; a variant whose responses were not saved is held to the first three), so that a loss lowered through the response rates, or by gathering the responses in few KCs, does not show as progress. The fourth band sets which steps the loss line takes. Before the APL-effect fits, the variants with lower losses, breadth-weighted APL alone on the bouton baseline among them ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E12_breadth_apl;tag=spikes_k2:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}), were outside that band with APL off, and the line stood at {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E10_joint_fit;tag=k1:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}, the joint fit to both matrices run at breadth exponent 1. That fit is one of the variants whose responses were not saved, and the joint fits at exponents 2 and 3 are both outside the band with APL off, so its step is provisional; the last step before it taken by a model scored on all four bands is the point of the threshold × breadth × KC–KC grid with the per-claw threshold at 70 Hz, breadth exponent 2 and no KC–KC inhibition ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E19_thr_breadth_kckc;tag=thr70_k2_kckc0:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}). The APL-effect fits took the line to {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=rate0.068_aplraw_k3_cap3:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}, the lowest loss on raw counts among [the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits) without a glomerulus gain: the first joint fit's family at breadth exponent 3, fitted on raw counts at the flies' response rate and scored on all four bands, just inside the one on odors per KC with APL off ({{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=rate0.068_aplraw_k3_cap3:opk_off_tv ~ .3f}} from the flies' distribution, against a band of {{data:tnt_odors_per_kc_tv_max ~ .3f}}). It is one of the fits that give up KC–KC inhibition and the block for a larger mean effect, with APL→bouton at {{E67/comparison.csv:kind=fit;tag=rate0.068_aplraw_k3_cap3:aplpn_factor_vs_e3 ~ .2f}} times the baseline's against a bound of three. The line is on deterministic runs: there the breadth-2 fit of the best-models table, which keeps both, stands at {{apl_effect/by_variant.csv:folder=E67_apl_effect_fits;tag=rate0.068_aplcal_k2_cap3:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}, and through the matched trial noise the breadth-3 fit holds its loss while the breadth-2 fit falls narrowly below it ([the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits)). The line now ends at {{E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.1114t1000_k2:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}, the breadth-2 fit made again with a firing ceiling at its stronger setting, inside all four bands ([the firing ceiling for the KCs](#firing-ceiling)). The best-models table carries the fit at the literature setting ({{E69/stats_variants.csv:rate0.068_aplboth_refr3_adapt0.04142t1000_k2:spike_counts_cosine__apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}), the one whose spike counts agree with whole-cell recordings of KCs; through the matched trial noise the two settings score alike, both below every fit without a ceiling. For the other seven, 100 % is the data's value, or its measurement ceiling where the data set one (the agreement between two halves of the recordings): ρ on odor-odor distances between the model and the KC data with APL on (the control matrix, ceiling {{data:ctrl_ceiling ~ .2f}}) and with APL off (the APL output blocked (TNT) matrix, ceiling {{data:tnt_ceiling ~ .2f}}), the fruity × small-ester block ({{data:block ~ +.3f}}), the per-odor response rate ρ with the control flies (ceiling {{data:ctrl_rate_rho_ceiling ~ .2f}}), the structure vs wild-type somata ({{data:ctrl_struct_wt ~ .2f}}), and the natural-source alignment of the APL-on residual ({{data:ctrl_vcf ~ .2f}}) and of the APL-off residual ({{data:tnt_vcf ~ .2f}}); the APL-off / APL-on response-rate ratio is left out because the first measured model already sat at the data's {{data:rate_ratio_fraction ~ .1f}}, as most tuned acute-knockout models do. Each experiment contributes its best variant on each statistic separately (so the points on different lines are usually different models), among variants tuned near the 5 % operating point and excluding the oracle constructions, the random or shuffled wirings, the level-matched input controls and the control of the sign of the input-scaled KC threshold (a power of −0.5); the last column of the best-models table carries the oracle gain, so it is not on the plot. The staircase is the best so far; hover a point for the experiment and the model behind it, click a legend entry to hide a line.

%%% html results/summary_progress/progress_plot.html

Two things the lines say that the sections above say in words: the block and the two structure statistics were closed early and by different models (the block by breadth-weighted APL with KC–KC inhibition, the structure by the coincidence rule and the per-claw threshold), while ρ on odor-odor distances with the two matrices and response rate ρ have moved by a fifth of their gap at most, and since the joint fits the lines have hardly moved, because the models that close one gap reopen another, until the APL-effect fits put the APL-effect loss first in the loss and lowered it, and the firing ceiling for the KCs lowered it again; the best single model on all statistics at once is in the table of the section before.

## Experiments running or not yet run, and what each would tell us

Pending work is judged first on the main target: what an experiment is expected to do to [the APL-effect loss](#apl-effect), to its mean part and to its pattern part, inside the realism bands, comes before its bearing on any other statistic. The band on the number of odors each KC responds to makes that a harder condition than it was: before [the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits), no model on the real wiring with a loss below the first joint fit's was inside it. Those fits are inside it at the flies' response rate (a model tuned to 5 % starts at a disadvantage on it), so a new mechanism is compared with them, at that response rate and under both definitions of the model's side.

- **The open measurement question** is the ORN responses of the oracle glomeruli to ms, paa, B-myr, euc and 1-8ol at Yang's concentrations; after the Hallem-input test it is two glomeruli first, DL1's response to ms and DM6's to paa at 10^-3, which would decide whether those two odors' deficit is in the input, while B-myr's and euc's stay open. The decomposition of the gain adds that its structural part needs a measurement of a different kind: DC2's drive onto the KCs relative to its ORN response ([above](#gain-decomposition)). The bounded-input oracle adds, for paa at 10^-3, VL2a (searched up), DP1m and DM4 (searched down) and DM6, which the search lowers where Hallem's data raise it, so that one glomerulus tests the two against each other; for B-myr, whose recording is already at Yang's concentration, the question is whether DM1 at half and DC1 and D at twice the recorded response are within the spread of the ORN recordings ([above](#gap-pairs)).
- **Model and readout changes still worth making:** a saturating APL unit nonlinearity, a calcium readout with the indicator's kinetics and the recordings' per-KC normalization (the static saturating readout is done, [above](#readout)), measured ORN time courses, real weights for the KCs of unknown type, and, from the weak-odor analyses, better-constrained ORN input for ms, B-myr, euc, paa and 1-8ol (concentrations and glomerular profiles; Hallem's receptor data, [tried above](#hallem-input), cover ms and paa but not B-myr or euc, and at ten times Yang's concentration). From the compressive readout: a mechanism that makes the block through which KCs respond, joined to the best models' mean effect and scored under the compressive and the binarized readout; and, as a constraint that no readout touches and that is now one of [the realism bands](#apl-effect), the number of odors each KC responds to with APL off (the reference model leaves {{E65/model_amplitude.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:odors_0_off ~ .0%}} of its KCs silent and has {{E65/model_amplitude.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:odors_8plus_off ~ .0%}} responding to eight or more of the 16 odors, against {{E65/data_amplitude_summary.csv:genotype=TNT:odors_0 ~ .0%}} and {{E65/data_amplitude_summary.csv:genotype=TNT:odors_8plus ~ .0%}} in the flies with APL output blocked (TNT), at nearly the same overall response rate). From the per-odor profile of the APL effect ([above](#apl-effect-parts)): the number-of-odors breadth in a fit of the main target (the exponent of the spike-total breadth has been fitted in the first joint fit's family, where the loss is lowest near 2, [the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits)); for the pair-specific part, gain sets without DM5 fitted and scored on it, and a form of the breadth weighting that keeps APL off the high-firing KCs without leaving the distance between two odors to the few KCs they share through one glomerulus; and no per-odor APL rule fitted to the response rates, which the per-odor APL oracle shows would not move the main target. From the low end of the readout ([above](#readout)): the amplitudes of the responses with APL off as a second constraint for later fits of the main target, beside the number of odors per KC and like it independent of how the target is defined (with APL output blocked (TNT) {{data:tnt_share_z_above_4 ~ .1%}} of the flies' KC × odor entries are above 4 z and their responders' mean amplitude is {{data:tnt_resp_amp_mean_z ~ .2f}} z, about as in the control flies; the reference model with APL off, read as in the table of amplitudes there, has {{E68/partB_amplitude.csv:model=ref40;config=floor_noise:share_above_4_off ~ .1%}} and {{E68/partB_amplitude.csv:model=ref40;config=floor_noise:resp_amp_mean_off ~ .2f}} z); and, for the recordings, how the trial means were z-scored (the baseline window, the neuropil correction, the order of the odors within a repeat), since the negative values, which repeat across trials, make up a fifth of the control flies' entries ({{data:ctrl_share_z_below_m025 ~ .0%}} below −0.25 z). From [the APL-effect fits](#apl-effect-fits): the three fits that went through the trial noise run again on further seeds of the noise, since on raw counts the breadth-2 fit's margin over the breadth-3 fit is about the difference between the two noise levels (if the margin holds over seeds, the breadth-2 family is the lowest under both definitions; if it does not, raw counts do not separate a model that has the block from one that gave it up, and the block has to be held as a target of its own); the breadth-2 fit repeated on shuffled draws of the wiring, the replication the earlier joint fits were given, has been run as the APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings ([above](#wiring-population)): its loss returns on the draws, so the family is the model to build on (what follows from it is listed further down); a mechanism that spreads the recruitment with APL off over more KCs, which neither a term on odors per KC in the loss nor the fitted parameters do, was the next step and has been run as the input-scaled KC threshold ([above](#input-scaled-threshold); what follows from it is listed further down); and, for the block under the readout, the mechanism acting on which KCs respond that is listed above, now with the breadth-2 fit as its base, since a block term in the loss does not raise that fit's block under the readout. From the high-firing KCs that survive APL ([above](#high-firing-kcs)): a carrier of the block that is not a few over-driven KCs, which the mechanisms acting on the set of responding KCs are, joined to a model without such cells; the single pseudo-recordings of a model through the matched trial noise, scored as single pairs of recordings were, have been run since, and the breadth-2 fit is already as close to the data's mean as a single pair of recordings is ([above](#input-scaled-threshold)). The route through the weak-input odors' input is not taken up, because the archived models that already take it change the survivors and the mean effect little: in the reference model as fitted at 5 %, leaving DP1m's gain out leaves {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr40__loo_DP1m:n_survivors ~ .0f}} survivors against {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr40__full:n_survivors ~ .0f}} with all eight glomeruli gained and a mean APL effect on raw counts of {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr40__loo_DP1m:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} against {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E39_gain_decomposition;tag=thr40__full:raw__mean ~ +.3f}}; with every odor's input scaled until its response rate with APL on is the flies', the reference model has {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E62_rate_oracle;tag=R_thr40_gain1.5_on:n_survivors ~ .0f}} against {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:n_survivors ~ .0f}} and {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E62_rate_oracle;tag=R_thr40_gain1.5_on:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} against {{E70/per_variant.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:raw__mean ~ +.3f}} (the flies' {{data:apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}}). No further re-draws of single cells' claws are planned. From the firing ceiling for the KCs ([above](#firing-ceiling)): whether the ceiling joins the base model, and at which strength, which needs one number, the largest spike count of a fly KC to a 0.5 s odor (the bound used is a reading of the recordings' means and standard deviations; through the trial noise the literature setting is as good as the stronger one, so it is the setting to adopt if the ceiling joins); the literature setting fitted on re-drawn wirings has been run ([above](#wiring-population)): the gain on raw counts returns on each of the three wirings tried, and the mean over wirings with the ceiling scores on raw counts what it scores under the readout, so what is left of that check is the same mean read through the trial noise; the block and the natural-source alignment on a base with the ceiling, which takes them away with the strong responders as the readout does, so that the carrier of the block listed above has to work on that base; the recruitment with APL off, which the ceiling leaves as it was and the input-scaled KC threshold moves off the many-claw KCs at a cost on the main target ([above](#input-scaled-threshold)); and, if the ceiling is adopted, a less schematic adaptation (two time constants, or synaptic depression at the claws) and a check that its effect does not need a time constant longer than the odor. From the input-scaled KC threshold ([above](#input-scaled-threshold)): which KCs carry the APL effect, which has been run as the APL effect split by odors per KC ([above](#apl-effect-by-class); what follows from it is listed further down); the family with the firing ceiling and the scaling at a small power under the reading of the per-KC normalization of the recordings, which the same experiment fitted and where the scaling cost nothing only because the fit left the two-spike operating point, so that the fit is to be made again with its response-rate terms on the two-spike criterion, as fits now are, if that reading is taken up for fits (if the scaling then costs less than a draw of the wiring moves the loss, the ceiling with the scaling is the candidate base and is compared on re-drawn wirings, where it has not been run; if it costs more, it stays out under every reading); a fit with the scaling and a term on the number of odors per KC in the loss, to see what the main target costs when the broad responders are held at the flies' share; other forms of the scaling (a threshold set from each KC's drive over a reference set of odors, a normalization of the drive per glomerulus), which were not tried and matter only if a fit with the scaling is taken up; and, for the realism bands, whether the band on the number of odors per KC should also hold the spread of that number, which in the breadth-2 family is above every recording's on all eight wirings it has been fitted on and which the band as defined does not see. From the APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings ([above](#wiring-population)): whether the main target is defined on a mean over wirings read through the trial noise, as the data's mean is one over flies and trials, which is a choice about the target like the readout (until it is made, that mean is given beside the single-wiring number, and two fits closer than about the draw term are compared on the real wiring and at least two re-drawn ones); the error common to all wirings as the target of the next mechanisms, the too-small APL effect among the esters and on the pairs of eb, 1-8ol, IaA and ep above all, read beside the APL effect split by odors per KC, which is reported for each fit and not fitted ([above](#apl-effect-by-class)) (a mechanism that lowers that error on the real wiring and on re-drawn ones moves the family toward the floor, while one that lowers the loss on the real wiring alone has fitted that wiring's own remainder); recruitment at the weakest input in the family without a per-claw threshold, ma at 10^-7 first, through a per-claw transfer that adds drive at low input in place of a softened threshold (if it brings ma to the flies' fraction at no cost on the 16-odor panel, the low-concentration recordings become constraints the family meets; if it costs there, they stay a regime of their own); and the two checks on wirings not yet run, the mean over wirings with the firing ceiling read through the trial noise, and the input-scaled KC threshold on re-drawn wirings beside the ceiling fits made there. From the APL effect split by odors per KC ([above](#apl-effect-by-class)): the split of the APL effect over the classes of KCs is reported for every later fit beside the realism bands, as a statistic of its own and not as a term of a loss, since the family that is asked for it pays on the total and its pattern part does not move; the pattern part is looked for in the per-pair errors that remain whatever the classes do, which the glomerulus profile of APL inhibition, below, takes up; and what would bring the class at four to seven odors to the flies' is a mechanism that makes the KCs of that class carry less of the overlap between odors with APL on, which is where the models differ from the flies in it (in the archive a per-claw threshold and the input-scaled threshold do that, each with a higher pattern part; if a mechanism does it on re-drawn wirings without that cost, the model comes nearer the flies in which KCs carry the effect at no price on the target, and if none does, the class stays a known difference that the total does not need). From the APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings: the error that every wiring shares, a too-small APL effect among 2h, IaA, pa, eb, ep and ms and a too-large one among 1-8ol, 1-5ol, 1-6ol and 6al, is the target of the glomerulus profile of APL inhibition. Because no draw of the wiring removes that error, the only wiring-independent way to say which KCs APL should act on is by the glomeruli they sample; the experiment maps how the per-pair APL effect changes when APL's weight on one glomerulus is raised (on the cells that sample it, on its claws, or on its boutons), builds from that map a profile over glomeruli that cancels the shared error, checks it on odors and recordings it was not built from, and re-fits the best family with it on several wirings. If the profile lowers the loss on wirings it never saw, the remaining error is one of where APL acts, and the connectomes' APL synapse densities per glomerulus say whether it is anatomy the model lacks or a prediction for imaging APL in the calyx. If it helps on the real wiring only, a static weight per glomerulus is not the carrier. If no profile exists, APL's targeting is ruled out for this family and the error lies in the geometry that the two conditions share, on the input side. The same experiment puts the firing-ceiling family on four wirings through the trial noise, the comparison with flies and trials both in the model. The readout itself stays as it is, with no measurement noise, one-spike floor or membrane-potential term.
- **Data that would discriminate the mechanisms:** APL imaging under the same odors (is APL's output to broadly tuned KCs larger, as breadth-weighted APL requires?), KC–KC interaction tests (KC responses with neighbouring KCs silenced, the direct test of KC–KC inhibition), per-trial KC data for a reliability readout (and for the noise floor of the mean distances), and, for the single-claw question, imaging of ms responders with their glomerular input identified.


## Parameter glossary with mechanisms

- `claw_thr` (per-claw rectified offset; replaces the coincidence count `n_claws_active_to_spike`): claw-level nonlinearity, subthreshold K⁺ shunting, release-rate threshold, or tonic APL-independent inhibition; see the per-claw threshold section.
- Breadth-weighted `_wAPLKC` (exponent k): activity-dependent scaling of APL↔KC synapses; see the block section.
- `kc_kc_inh`, `kc_kc_tau` (KC–KC inhibition, connectome claw–claw weights): direct KC→KC transmission via inhibitory muscarinic receptors, toxin-insensitive local APL loops, ephaptic / K⁺ coupling, or shared presynaptic depletion; see the block section.
- APL→bouton weights (`wAPLPN`, `wPNAPL`): presynaptic GABA-B inhibition of PN terminals; see the bouton section; the joint fit raises them 2–3× to get the response-rate ratio.
- `fixed_thr` (somatic threshold, tuned to 5 %): KC intrinsic excitability under homeostatic control; the tuning procedure is a homeostatic sparseness set-point, and "APL off = 2× response rate" is what that set-point implies.
- `wAPLKC`, `wKCAPL` scales: global gains of the APL↔KC synapses (the data want APL to roughly halve the responder count).
- `n_spikes_for_response = 2`: the imaging readout (nuclear GCaMP6s needs more than one spike); the readout-window experiment found the window itself is not a knob.
- `claw_tau`, `apl_taum` (50 ms): synaptic / membrane filtering at claws and in APL; only the response rate depends on them.
- Glomerulus-specific PN → claw gain (an oracle: the wiring columns of VA7m, DL1, D, DM1, DP1m, VC1, DC2, DM5 scaled by 1.5): the claws of the weak odors' glomeruli counting for more; realizations in the glomerulus-specific claw strength section (bouton size or release sites per glomerulus, ORN → PN gain, claw-level scaling, or understated ORN input).
- `claw_pow`, `claw_pow_ref` (convex per-claw transfer after the threshold): a regenerative claw (dendritic spike, NMDA-like supralinearity); tested and negative, because it rewards the esters' breadth rather than the weak odors' few claws; see the weak-input section.
- `ext_inh_kc`, `ext_inh_bouton`, `ext_inh_scale` (odor-specific external inhibition of KC somata and of bouton output during the odor): the lateral-horn centrifugal and MB-C1 pathway built from hemibrain synapse counts; tested and negative (too flat across odors at the soma; a glomerulus-specific gain cut at the boutons).
- `claw_sat` (per-claw cap), `bouton_ves_p` / `bouton_tau_r` (bouton depletion): compressive transfers; both go the wrong way. Biophysically real (receptor saturation, short-term depression at PN terminals), but they cannot be what makes the KC code sparse in the data's way; whatever they do in the fly must sit under a per-claw threshold.
- `apl_unit_coupling`, APL units: spatially local APL; no effect at the KC level.
- `pn_inhadd` / `pn_inhsc`: antennal-lobe lateral inhibition (PN contrast); no help.
- `kc_refractory_period`, `kc_adapt_inc`, `kc_adapt_tau` (the firing ceiling; off by default): the refractory period after a spike, and spike-frequency adaptation as an increment on the KC's threshold at each spike that decays with a time constant (calcium-activated potassium currents, or synaptic depression at the claws); with a time constant longer than the odor it bounds the number of spikes per odor, which does for the model's raw spike counts what the calibrated readout does after the fact; see the firing-ceiling subsection.

## Footnotes {#footnotes}

### How the monotone (isotonic) fit is done, and why {#fn-isotonic}

**What is fitted.** The 120 pair distances of the control matrix are regressed on the 120 pair distances of the APL output blocked (TNT) matrix with isotonic regression: the fitted curve is the non-decreasing step function that is closest to the control values in the least-squares sense (the pool-adjacent-violators algorithm; scikit-learn's IsotonicRegression with increasing=True). "Isotonic" means order-preserving: the curve may bend any way as long as it never goes down, so a pair that is farther apart with APL output blocked is predicted to be at least as far apart in control flies. The isotonic R² is the fraction of the control variance the curve explains (0.94); a pair's residual is its control distance minus the curve's value at its APL output blocked (TNT) distance; and the block is the mean residual of the nine fruity × small-ester pairs ({{data:block ~ +.3f}}), with its confidence interval from resampling recordings.

**Why this fit.** The hypothesis being tested is that APL's output is a uniform sparsening: every pair moves in the same direction by an amount that depends only on how far apart the pair already is. Any such effect is a monotone map from the APL output blocked (TNT) matrix to the control matrix, and isotonic regression is the most general monotone map, so its residuals contain only what no uniform effect can produce, the pair-specific part. It assumes nothing about the shape of the map (the data's map is strongly compressive at the top, where KC distances saturate near 1), it has no tuning parameter, and its R² can be compared directly with the measurement ceiling computed the same way between halves of the recordings.

**The alternatives, and why not.** A linear fit of control on APL output blocked (TNT) assumes the change is proportional; with the saturation near 1 it explains less of the control variance (R² 0.85 against 0.94) and leaves curved residuals, so its block estimate (+0.098) mixes the pair-specific effect with the curvature the line cannot follow. A parametric saturating curve (a power or logistic function) has fewer degrees of freedom but imposes a shape the data would have to meet, and the block estimate then depends on that choice. Rank correlation alone (Spearman 0.87) tests monotonicity but gives no per-pair residual, so it cannot locate the block. Comparing the two matrices with the expectation from response density (the confound controls) asks a different, complementary question (whether two odors share more KCs than their response fractions predict) and is reported separately as the excess shared responders. The isotonic fit's own limitation is that a step function through 120 points can follow local noise, so its R² is an upper bound for any smooth monotone curve; that is why the residual is read for a block of nine pairs rather than for single pairs, and why the measurement ceiling is computed with the same fit.

### Odor concentrations in each dataset {#fn-concentrations}

The 16 odors of Yang's APL16 KC panel and the ORN data that stand in for them in the model (log10 of the v/v dilution). Twelve odors are at the concentrations of the lab's megamat ORN panel. For the four odors the APL16 panel shares with the validation2 ORN panel, Yang used different concentrations: no ORN data exist at paa -3, euc -2 or -aPine -2, so ORN data within one log10 unit stand in (the rule adopted for this work: a substitution within one log10 unit, inclusive, is allowed, and every result that involves the substituted odors' model input is reported as approximate). B-myr matches. The ORN input for paa is therefore from a tenfold weaker stimulus than the KCs saw, and for euc and -aPine from a somewhat stronger one, which bears on the weak-input-odor analyses: a shortfall of modelled drive for euc or -aPine cannot be blamed on the panel concentration, while for paa it could be.

| odor | APL16 KC data (Yang) | ORN data used | ORN dataset | difference (ORN minus KC, log10 units) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2h, IaA, pa, 2-but, eb, ep, 6al, t2h, 1-8ol, 1-5ol, 1-6ol, ms | -3 | -3 | megamat | 0 |
| paa | -3 | -4 | validation2 | -1 (ORN weaker) |
| -aPine | -2 | -1.5 | validation2 | +0.5 (ORN stronger) |
| euc | -2 | -1.75 | validation2 | +0.25 (ORN stronger) |
| B-myr | -2 | -2 | validation2 | 0 |

### How "structure beyond the ORN input" is computed {#fn-orn-residual}

1. **Subset odor distances to megamat.** Since Yang's APL16 panel only includes 12 of the 17 odors in Remy's megamat panel (its other 4 odors come from validation2), we subset the ORN / KC / embedded-VCF odor-odor distances down to these 12 odors, leaving us {{data:n_ref_pairs ~ .0f}} pairs of the 120 in Yang's full APL16 panel.
2. **Linear prediction.** For KC odor-odor distances, from one model or the mean over per-recording distances, fit KC distance = a + b × mean ORN distance across the {{data:n_ref_pairs ~ .0f}} pairs by ordinary least squares. Each set of KC distances gets its own a and b. For the control flies, b = {{data:ctrl_orn_fit_slope ~ .2f}}, a = {{data:ctrl_orn_fit_intercept ~ .2f}}, and R² = {{data:ctrl_orn_fit_r2 ~ .2f}}.
3. **Residual.** Each pair's KC distance minus the line's prediction. Because a and b are fitted to each set of KC distances, any part of those distances that is a straight-line function of the mean ORN distances goes into the line. That includes a uniform offset, such as a higher overall level of decorrelation, and a uniform stretch. The residual keeps only how individual pairs depart from that line, and by construction it is uncorrelated with the mean ORN distances.
4. **Comparison.** ρ between the residual and either the residual of Remy's wild-type KC soma matrix, computed the same way (structure vs wild-type somata: {{data:ctrl_struct_wt ~ .2f}} for the control flies), or the hyperbolic natural-source distances themselves, including the {{data:n_vcf_filled ~ .0f}} pairs whose raw natural-source value is missing and is filled by the embedding (natural-source alignment: {{data:tnt_vcf ~ .2f}} with APL output blocked (TNT)).

A dependence on the ORN distances that is curved but monotone, as a threshold produces, also leaves a systematic residual, which this statistic counts as structure beyond the ORN input: in the left panel, the within-group pairs with small ORN distances all sit below the line.

![Left: the control flies' KC distances against the mean ORN distances for the 66 megamat pairs, with the least-squares line and each pair's residual (red above the line, blue below). Middle: the control residual against the same residual of the wild-type KC somata. Right: the residual with APL output blocked (TNT) against the natural-source distances; hollow points are the pairs whose natural-source value comes from the embedding.](results/summary_background/orn_residual_example.png)

### How Yang's KC distances compare with Remy's megamat KC data {#fn-yang-remy}

Two of the page's statistics (the structure vs wild-type somata, and the 66-pair residual that the natural-source alignment is computed from) rest on Remy's wild-type megamat KC data: trial-mean responses of KC somata imaged in 18 flies (4 from 2022 and 14 from 2018–19 with partial odor panels), 17 megamat odors at 10^-3, distances as one minus the Pearson correlation across KCs. Yang's APL16 data share 12 of those odors ({{data:n_ref_pairs ~ .0f}} pairs), at the same concentrations, with distances as one minus the cosine similarity across KCs, per recording and averaged. On those pairs the two labs' wild-type matrices agree at ρ={{data:rho_ctrl_remy_soma ~ .2f}} on odor-odor distances (90 % interval over Yang's recordings {{data:rho_ctrl_remy_soma.lo ~ .2f}} to {{data:rho_ctrl_remy_soma.hi ~ .2f}}; Pearson r {{data:pearson_ctrl_remy_soma ~ .2f}} between the two sets of distances; {{data:rho_ctrl_remy_soma_2022 ~ .2f}} against Remy's 2022 flies alone), against a measurement ceiling of {{data:ctrl_ceiling_66 ~ .2f}} for Yang's control matrix on these pairs; a single control recording agrees with Remy's mean at ρ={{data:ctrl_rec_rho_remy_soma ~ .2f}} and with the mean of the other ten control recordings at {{data:ctrl_rec_rho_loo ~ .2f}}. Remy's KC claws agree less ({{data:rho_ctrl_remy_claw ~ .2f}}). The ranking is what agrees; the levels are on different scales (correlation and cosine distance), with Remy's somata at a mean of {{data:remy_soma_mean_66 ~ .2f}} and Yang's control flies at {{data:ctrl_mean_66 ~ .2f}} on the same pairs.

Remy's wild-type somata sit closer to the ORN input than Yang's control KCs do (ρ on odor-odor distances with the ORNs {{data:rho_remy_soma_orn ~ .2f}} against {{data:rho_ctrl_orn_66 ~ .2f}}; Yang's flies with APL output blocked {{data:rho_tnt_orn_66 ~ .2f}}), and Yang's APL-blocked matrix agrees with Remy's somata as well as the control matrix does ({{data:rho_tnt_remy_soma ~ .2f}} against {{data:rho_ctrl_remy_soma ~ .2f}}): on this panel, Remy's somata are intermediate between Yang's two genotypes in their ORN-likeness. What they share with the control matrix in particular is the part beyond the ORN input: the 66-pair residuals agree at ρ={{data:ctrl_struct_wt ~ .2f}} for the control flies and {{data:tnt_struct_wt ~ .2f}} with APL output blocked, which is why the "structure vs wild-type somata" of a model is read against the control flies' value. The pairs that differ most between the two wild-type matrices are 2h–eb (far apart in Yang's control flies, among the closer pairs in Remy's somata) and the pairs of ms and t2h with other odors, which Remy's somata place further apart. A separate note (reports/note_yang_remy_agreement.html) has the full tables and figure.

### A KC spike's drive to APL and the integration step {#fn-spike-step}

olfsysm integrates the model in steps of 0.5 ms, and a KC spike lasts one step. In the models whose APL is driven by KC spikes, each spike used to add a fixed amount to APL's input current for that one step, so the drive per spike scaled with the step. A time-step check of the integrator, at fixed tuned parameters, found that the 70 Hz spike-driven base's APL peak fell from {{numerics_dt_check/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.5:inh_peak_on ~ .1f}} to {{numerics_dt_check/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.25:inh_peak_on ~ .1f}} and {{numerics_dt_check/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.125:inh_peak_on ~ .1f}} and its APL-on response rate rose from {{numerics_dt_check/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.5:rate_on ~ .1%}} to {{numerics_dt_check/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.25:rate_on ~ .1%}} and {{numerics_dt_check/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.125:rate_on ~ .1%}} as the step went from 0.5 to 0.25 and 0.125 ms, with {{numerics_dt_check/flip_fractions.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.25;condition=on:frac_responses_flipped ~ .0%}} to {{numerics_dt_check/flip_fractions.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.125;condition=on:frac_responses_flipped ~ .0%}} of its APL-on responses changing, while the claw-driven base changed {{numerics_dt_check/flip_fractions.csv:base=claw_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.25;condition=on:frac_responses_flipped ~ .0%}} to {{numerics_dt_check/flip_fractions.csv:base=claw_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.125;condition=on:frac_responses_flipped ~ .0%}} of its responses. The spike's drive is now scaled by the ratio of a reference step (0.5 ms) to the step in use, so each spike adds the same charge to APL's input whatever the step, and at 0.5 ms every output is identical to before; no result on this page changed. Re-run with the fix, the same base's APL peak goes from {{numerics_dt_check_spike_fix/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.5:inh_peak_on ~ .1f}} to {{numerics_dt_check_spike_fix/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.25:inh_peak_on ~ .1f}} and {{numerics_dt_check_spike_fix/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.125:inh_peak_on ~ .1f}} and its APL-on response rate from {{numerics_dt_check_spike_fix/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.5:rate_on ~ .1%}} to {{numerics_dt_check_spike_fix/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.25:rate_on ~ .1%}} and {{numerics_dt_check_spike_fix/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.125:rate_on ~ .1%}}, with {{numerics_dt_check_spike_fix/flip_fractions.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.25;condition=on:frac_responses_flipped ~ .0%}} to {{numerics_dt_check_spike_fix/flip_fractions.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.125;condition=on:frac_responses_flipped ~ .0%}} of its APL-on responses changing, as few as in the claw-driven base; the remaining changes come from spike times falling on whole steps. The odor-odor distances were unaffected throughout (ρ on odor-odor distances with the 0.5 ms matrices {{numerics_dt_check/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.125:rho_on_vs_ref_dt ~ .3f}} before the fix and {{numerics_dt_check_spike_fix/per_dt_stats.csv:base=spike_driven_thr70;dt_ms=0.125:rho_on_vs_ref_dt ~ .3f}} after, at 0.125 ms).

### What "oracle" means on this page {#fn-oracle}

An oracle, here, is a model manipulation that is handed part of the answer. Instead of a circuit rule the fly could implement, the manipulation is given a list of exactly which KCs or which glomeruli to change, read off the KC data or off the model's own output scored against the data, and changes exactly those. Its result is a ceiling: an existence proof that changing those elements would be sufficient, made meaningful by controls that make the same change to random KCs or random glomeruli and show that the identity of the elements is what matters. It is never a mechanism; the question each oracle leaves behind is what in the circuit could single out those elements. The page uses two oracles, the oracle co-responder suppression and the oracle glomerulus gain, each with a footnote below, and one partial one: the breadth × shared-input APL rule's shared-claw term used glomeruli chosen from the two ester groups themselves, which the panel-general rule was then built to avoid. The progress plot leaves all of these out (the co-responder suppression, the gain's glomerulus subsets in the decomposition, the block-specific glomerulus set), and with them every model that carries the glomerulus gain at all, whether applied at fixed parameters or held fixed inside a fit (the combined-model fits, the reference model and the operating-point fits included), because the best of them on any statistic would be a selection made with the data in hand: on that plot the best so far is always a model given nothing from the KC data but its fitted parameters. The best-models table, by contrast, shows the gain-carrying fits beside the rest, with the gain named in their headers.

### The oracle co-responder suppression {#fn-oracle-coresponders}

**What was done.** On the claw-dynamics base, with and without the coincidence rule, the model was tuned with APL on and run with APL off, and the co-responders were defined from that APL-off run as the KCs responding to at least one fruity ester (2h, IaA, pa) and to at least one small ester (2-but, eb, ep). The connectome APL→KC weights of those KCs' claws were then multiplied by a factor (4 in the variant this page quotes; 8, and 1000, which leaves APL acting on those KCs alone, were also run), the APL re-tuned to the 5 % response rate, and the model scored with APL on and off like every other variant.

**Why it is an oracle.** The set of KCs to inhibit was chosen by the outcome wanted. The block is the pair-specific separation of the fruity esters from the small esters, and these are exactly the KCs that keep the two groups' representations close; no circuit rule picked them, the model's own output did, read against what the data show.

**The controls.** The same factor on an equal number of random KCs outside the set, and on the co-responders of a different pair of odor groups (the alcohols and the aldehydes); neither produces the block.

**What it established.** Removing exactly those KCs is sufficient for the block at the measured size, with the control flies' excess profile and the best ρ on odor-odor distances of its base, so the mechanisms that followed (the breadth × shared-input rule, KC–KC lateral inhibition) were judged by how much of this suppression they reproduce without being told the set. "The oracle's suppression without the oracle" is the KC–KC term achieving most of the same removal through the connectome's claw-to-claw contacts.

### The oracle glomerulus gain {#fn-oracle-gain}

**What was done.** In the PN→KC wiring, the columns of eight glomeruli (VA7m, DL1 and D for ms; DM1 for B-myr and paa; DP1m and VC1 for euc and -aPine; DC2 and DM5 for 1-8ol) are multiplied by a factor, 1.5 on this page unless a column says 1.25 (2 and 3 were also run in the original experiment), so every claw a KC receives from those glomeruli counts that much more in its summed input: a PN→claw gain, applied before the per-claw threshold. The somatic threshold is then re-tuned to the target response rate and, in the fitted models, the APL scales and the KC–KC scale are fitted with the gain held fixed.

**Why it is an oracle.** The glomeruli were chosen as the ones the five weak-input odors drive most in the lab's ORN data, after those five odors had been identified as the ones the threshold model fails to recruit relative to the KC data. The set therefore encodes which odors the model was known to miss, and the factor 1.5 was set by hand to recruit them, not derived from anything in the model or the anatomy.

**The controls.** The same gain on eight random ORN-driven glomeruli outside the set (it recruits nothing and disorders the code), and the anatomical alternative, each claw weighted by its PN→KC synapse count (it recruits two of the five odors and loses the alignment).

**What it stands for.** Since the identity of the glomeruli carries the effect, not the extra drive, and the calyx anatomy of those glomeruli is ordinary (the substrate check), the gain is read as a proxy for something glomerulus-specific upstream of the calyx: an ORN→PN gain, a release probability the synapse counts do not show, or ORN input the lab's data understate for these odors by about that factor at the PN level; the Hallem-input test supports the last reading for ms and paa.

**Where it lives on.** The gain is a fixed setting of every combined model, of the reference model and of the operating-point fits, and the decomposition and the reduced-set fits ask which of the eight glomeruli it needs (next footnote).

### The oracle glomeruli as a set {#fn-oracle-glomeruli}

The eight glomeruli of the oracle gain (VA7m, DL1, D, DM1, DP1m, VC1, DC2, DM5) are also treated as a set in three later analyses. The substrate check compared their representation in the model's calyx wiring (PNs present, boutons, claws per presynaptic site) with the other ORN-driven glomeruli's, to ask whether the hemibrain's calyx anatomy could justify a gain of 1.5 for them; it cannot, the correction the anatomy would justify being centred on 1. The decomposition split the gain by glomerulus at the reference model's fitted parameters and found that its structural part, the natural-source alignment, comes from DC2, which is not a weak-odor glomerulus, while the five weak odors are recruited by their own glomeruli and the block needs DM5. The reduced-set fits then put DC2 alone, or DC2 with DM5 and DM1, into the joint fit in place of the full set. In each of these the set is still the oracle's: asking which members it needs does not make the choice of the set less of an oracle, which is why the decomposition's subsets are left out of the progress plot (the best subset per statistic would be a selection made with the data in hand). The open measurement question names the same glomeruli: the ORN responses of the oracle glomeruli to the five weak-input odors at the KC data's concentrations.

### The trial-to-trial noise put into the model, and the pipeline that measures it {#fn-trial-noise}

The noise is per-KC excitability jitter. On each simulated trial every KC's spiking threshold is multiplied by its own random factor, 1 + σ ξ, with ξ a standard normal draw made once per KC and trial and shared by that trial's APL-on and APL-off runs (a factor below 0.01 is clipped to 0.01). σ is calibrated per model so that the model's single-trial same-odor floor, one minus the cosine similarity between two trials of the same odor, matches the control flies' {{data:ctrl_single_trial_floor ~ .2f}}, and the mean threshold is re-tuned so that the per-trial APL-on response rate stays at the deterministic run's. Thirty such trials, drawn from a seeded generator so that every model gets the same draws, are grouped into ten pseudo-recordings of three and processed as the KC data are: the three trials of a recording are averaged, the cosine distances between odors are taken on those means and averaged over the recordings, and the fruity × small-ester block is the mean isotonic excess of the nine pairs; the grand mean over all thirty trials is reported beside it. "The pipeline" on this page is this procedure, and "the block through the noise" its block. Noise on the PN firing rates was tried first and set aside because it averages out over the response window and cannot reach the data's floors; jitter of each glomerulus's input gain reaches them as the threshold jitter does and is the alternative the trial-to-trial variability experiment kept beside it.

### The threshold margin of a KC × odor entry {#fn-margin}

For one KC and one odor in a deterministic run, the margin is the factor by which that KC's threshold can be scaled before its response to the odor disappears (for a responder) or appears (for a non-responder), minus one: a margin of +0.2 means the response survives a threshold 20 % higher. It is found by recovering the KC's exact input drive over time from its membrane-voltage trace (the summed claw input after the per-claw knee and the APL inhibition, minus the KC–KC inhibition from the other KCs' spikes in that run) and integrating that drive again with olfsysm's membrane equation at scaled thresholds, every other KC held as in the deterministic run, the scale found by bisection. Because the trial noise multiplies thresholds by 1 + σ ξ, a margin expressed in units of σ gives the probability that the entry flips on a noisy trial with the rest of the network held fixed.

### The margin proxy and the replayed trials {#fn-replay}

The margin proxy predicts what the pipeline would measure (the floors, the per-trial response rates, the odor-odor distances, the block through the noise) from one deterministic run, without simulating the noise. It does so by replaying trials: for each of the pipeline's thirty trials it takes the exact jittered thresholds the pipeline draws for that trial (the same seeded draws, the same re-tuned mean threshold, the same clipping) and integrates every KC's recovered drive at those thresholds; the spike counts per KC and odor that come out are one replayed trial, and the thirty are processed exactly as the pipeline processes its simulated trials. The one difference from the pipeline is that nothing else in the network reacts to the jitter: each KC's drive carries the KC–KC inhibition from the deterministic run's spikes, not from the other KCs' spikes on that noisy trial. APL is driven by the claws and the PN layer is not jittered in these models, so the KC–KC inhibition is the only feedback the replay leaves out, which is why the proxy reproduces the pipeline exactly for a model without KC–KC inhibition and departs from it in proportion to that inhibition's weight relative to the threshold.

### The network-aware margin proxy {#fn-network-proxy}

The margin proxy holds the other KCs' spikes, and with them each KC's KC–KC inhibition, at the deterministic run's; on a real noisy trial those spikes change too. The network-aware version adds that reaction by iteration: the replayed spikes of all KCs from the first pass are low-pass filtered as olfsysm filters them, each KC's inhibition is recomputed from them through the KC–KC weights, every KC is replayed again at the same jittered thresholds, and the step is repeated. Each pass adds one round of the network responding to the jitter, still without a new simulation. The iteration is synchronous over whole trials, where olfsysm's integration couples the KCs step by step, so whether the passes converge to the pipeline's trials is the question the experiment asks: if a few passes converge onto the pipeline's numbers, a noise-robustness score cheap enough for a fitting loss exists; if they oscillate, the network's response at that inhibition strength needs the simulation.

### How the calibrated readout of the model's spike counts works {#fn-calibrated-readout}

**What is done.** A model's distance between two odors is a cosine distance across KCs on spike counts (one count per KC and odor, the spikes in the response); the flies' is a cosine distance on z-scored trial means of a nuclear calcium signal, which compresses large responses. The readout replaces each spike count n by n / (n + k) before the model's distances are taken; k, the half-saturation count, is the number of spikes at which the readout reaches half of its maximum of 1. Zero stays zero and a larger count always reads larger, so which KCs respond (two or more spikes), every response rate and the realism bands are exactly as on raw counts; what changes is how much each responding KC weighs in the cosine. With the current reference model's k of {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=saturating_cal_pr:param ~ .1f}} spikes, a KC that fires 2 spikes reads {{lit:0.16 # n / (n + k) at n = 2 with the reference model's calibrated k, 10.46 spikes (results/E65_spike_count_readout/key_models.csv, readout saturating_cal_pr, column param)}}, one that fires 20 reads {{lit:0.66 # n / (n + k) at n = 20 with the reference model's calibrated k, 10.46 spikes (results/E65_spike_count_readout/key_models.csv, readout saturating_cal_pr, column param)}}, and the model's most active KC with APL on, at {{E65/model_amplitude.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:max_count_on ~ .0f}} spikes, reads {{lit:0.88 # n / (n + k) at n = 78, the reference model's largest APL-on count, with its calibrated k, 10.46 spikes (results/E65_spike_count_readout/key_models.csv, readout saturating_cal_pr, column param)}}: it weighs {{lit:5.5 # the readout of 78 spikes over the readout of 2 spikes, n / (n + k) with the reference model's calibrated k, 10.46 spikes: 0.882 / 0.160}} times as much as the 2-spike KC, where on raw counts it weighs {{lit:39 # 78 spikes, the reference model's largest APL-on count (results/E65_spike_count_readout/model_amplitude.csv, column max_count_on), over 2 spikes}} times as much.

**How k is set.** k is set for each model separately, on that model's spike counts with APL on, and never on the APL effect. The quantity matched to the flies is the participation ratio of an odor's response vector, (Σx²)² / (N Σx⁴) over the N KCs' values x: the effective fraction of the KCs that carry the odor's squared response, the quantity a cosine weighs (1 when all KCs carry it equally, 1/N when one KC carries all of it). It is computed on responders only (in a recording, trial means at or below 1 set to 0; in a model, entries under two spikes set to 0, for this statistic alone) and averaged over the 16 odors and, in the flies, over the {{data:n_recordings_ctrl ~ .0f}} control recordings. The control flies' value is {{data:ctrl_participation_ratio_resp ~ .1%}} ({{data:tnt_participation_ratio_resp ~ .1%}} with APL output blocked (TNT)); the current reference model's raw counts with APL on give {{E65/model_amplitude.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3:participation_ratio_resp_on ~ .1%}}, a response more concentrated than the flies'. A small k evens out the responders and raises the ratio, a large k leaves the counts almost as they are, and k is the value at which the readout of the model's APL-on counts has the control flies' ratio, found by bisection on log k between 0.001 and 1000 spikes (when no k in that range reaches it, the nearer end is used, as for {{lit:47 # results/E65_spike_count_readout/by_variant_readout.csv: rows with readout saturating_cal_pr whose at_bound is true}} of the {{E65/readout_summary.csv:readout=saturating_cal_pr:n_variants ~ .0f}} saved models). The same k is then applied to the model's APL-off counts, every count read, single spikes included. Nothing from the flies with APL output blocked (TNT) and nothing about the effect enters the calibration, so the APL-off distances and the per-pair APL effect remain predictions. The calibration gives {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=saturating_cal_pr:param ~ .1f}} spikes for the current reference model, {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E10_joint_fit;tag=k2;readout=saturating_cal_pr:param ~ .1f}} for the first joint fit and between {{lit:4.5 and 15 # results/E65_spike_count_readout/key_models.csv, readout saturating_cal_pr, column param: 4.52 to 14.94 over the nine key models (E65_spike_count_readout.md)}} spikes across the models examined most closely, far from both a k below one spike, which would count every firing KC alike, and a k above the largest counts, which would leave the counts as they are. Fits of the main target that carry the readout in their loss set k again at every evaluation, on that evaluation's APL-on counts.

**The alternatives, and what the readout is not.** Other ways of fixing the compression without reference to the APL effect were run beside this one: a power-law readout, n raised to a power below 1, calibrated in the same way; the saturating readout calibrated instead to the share of an odor's squared response held by the top 1 % of KCs among the control flies' responders ({{data:ctrl_top1_share_resp ~ .0%}}), to the coefficient of variation (standard deviation over mean) of their responders' amplitudes ({{data:ctrl_resp_amp_cv ~ .2f}}) or to their participation ratio on all values, sub-threshold ones included ({{data:ctrl_participation_ratio_all ~ .1%}}); and a map that sends the model's APL-on counts, rank for rank, onto the control flies' pooled responder amplitudes. They agree: for the current reference model the APL-effect loss is between {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=power_cal_pr:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} and {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=saturating_cal_pr_all:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} under these alternatives, against {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=saturating_cal_pr:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} under the readout used and {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=raw:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}} on raw counts (lower is better; the data's floor is {{data:apl_effect_rmse_floor ~ .3f}}), and an uncalibrated square root gives {{E65/key_models.csv:folder=E44_operating_point;tag=thr40_gain1.5_rate0.068_k2_cap3;readout=sqrt:apl_effect_rmse ~ .3f}}. With so little between them, the participation ratio was kept because it measures directly what the cosine weighs and is computed in the same way on the model and on the recordings. The readout is a static, elementwise map, not a model of the imaging: it has no indicator kinetics and no nuclear localization, and it does not emulate the recordings' per-KC normalization (how the trial means were z-scored is still to be confirmed). Below the response criterion the readout holds nothing, where the recordings hold weak and negative values; giving it measurement noise and a calibrated weight for single spikes was tried and leaves the scores where they were ([the next footnote](#fn-readout-low-end)), so it carries neither. Whether the main target is scored on raw counts or through this readout is still to be decided, and until then both are reported; in a model whose KCs have a firing ceiling the two scores nearly coincide ([the firing ceiling for the KCs](#firing-ceiling)).

### How the low end of the readout was tested {#fn-readout-low-end}

**The recordings below the response criterion.** Everything here is computed per recording, from the z-scored value of each KC, odor and trial, and averaged over the recordings of a genotype; a KC × odor entry is a responder when its mean over the three trials is above 1 z and sub-threshold otherwise. For the reproducibility of a band of values, entries are selected by their mean over two of the three trials and read on the third, each trial held out in turn.
